## Rendering, viewport & layout

### Key-column anchoring: bounded float drift on long lines without per-character measurement

`missing` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** For a monospace ASCII line, `charWidth * column` is exact enough for the first 300 columns. Beyond that, accumulated float error from a non-integral `charWidth` (e.g. 7.2266px) visibly desyncs the caret from the glyphs — Monaco cites microsoft/vscode#33178. So `FastRenderedViewLine` keeps a `Float32Array` of 'key column' pixel offsets, one per 300 columns. `_getColumnPixelOffset(column)` computes `keyColumnOrdinal = floor((column-1)/300) - 1`, does ONE real DOM read at that key column (memoized forever), and then extrapolates: `keyColumnPixelOffset + charWidth * (horizontalOffset(column) - horizontalOffset(keyColumn))`. Error is therefore bounded by at most 300 characters of accumulation regardless of line length, at a cost of ceil(len/300) DOM reads per line. `getWidth` uses the same threshold: pure arithmetic under 300 chars, `offsetWidth` above it (viewLine.ts:383-393). A background `RunOnceScheduler` re-validates the assumption on the longest visible line and downgrades every visible line to the slow path if the measured width is >2px off the predicted one (viewLine.ts:403-417; viewLines.ts:574-603, scheduled only on Linux at viewLines.ts:659-669).

**Why it matters here.** Our calculated (monospace) geometry path is `column * characterWidth` with no anchoring, so on a 5000-character line the caret and selection edges drift progressively away from the glyphs. Users notice this as 'clicking puts the cursor a character or two off, and it gets worse the further right I go' — precisely the class of bug that makes an editor feel unreliable.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/viewLine.ts:342-351 (Constants.MaxMonospaceDistance = 300, with the rationale comment), :366-377 (_keyColumnPixelOffsetCache sizing), :429-455 (_getColumnPixelOffset)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts:378-391 `buildCalculatedRowGeometry` and :417-432 `appendCalculatedChunkBoundaries` compute `rowTextInsetLeft(row) + column * cellWidth` for every offset with no DOM anchor at any interval. :393-402 `calculatedXToOffset` inverts the same pure multiplication. `rowUsesCalculatedGeometry` (:373-376) selects this path for every simple-ASCII row with no inline mapping, i.e. the common case, at any length. There is no equivalent of `monospaceAssumptionsAreValid` — no runtime check that `view.metrics.characterWidth` still matches reality after a zoom or font-load.

**Verifier.** Genuinely absent and directly portable — our calculated path is structurally identical to Monaco's `FastRenderedViewLine`, so the key-column array drops straight into `buildCalculatedRowGeometry`. One mitigation the claim missed: browserMetrics.ts:8 probes with a 16-char string ('mmmmmmmmmmmmmmmm') and divides by 16 (:50), so our `characterWidth` already carries ~1/16th the quantisation error of a single-char probe. Drift is therefore slower than Monaco's but still unbounded — at 7.2266px/char a 1/16-px error accumulates past a pixel by column ~5000. Value is confined to very long lines (minified JS, CSV, log files); moderate. The cheaper companion fix is the missing `monospaceAssumptionsAreValid` check: a stale cached `characterWidth` after a webfont swap desyncs the caret on EVERY line, not just long ones, and one `offsetWidth` comparison per frame catches it.

**How to implement.** In `buildCalculatedRowGeometry`, when `row.text.length > KEY_COLUMN_DISTANCE` (300), do one `measuredTextSegmentRect` read at each multiple of 300 and use those as anchors, offsetting subsequent boundaries from the nearest preceding anchor instead of from 0. Cache the anchors on the row alongside `geometryCache` so they survive re-queries; they are already invalidated correctly because `rowGeometryCacheKey` (:340-358) includes `view.metrics.characterWidth`. Separately, add a cheap validity probe: after `renderRows`, if any visible simple row is longer than 300 chars, compare `element.firstChild` offsetWidth against the predicted width once (deferred to an idle callback, as viewLines.ts:659 does) and set a `view.monospaceValid = false` flag that forces `rowUsesCalculatedGeometry` to return false — this also covers the font-loading race where `characterWidth` was measured against a fallback font.

### Lazy, memoized per-column pixel offsets with a shared Range and one cached container rect

`partial` · value **high** · effort **M** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco never measures a line eagerly. `RenderedViewLine` allocates one `Float32Array(len+1)` of -1 sentinels (viewLine.ts:506-509) and fills a slot only when someone asks for that exact column. The DOM read itself goes through `RangeUtil.readHorizontalRanges`, which (a) reuses ONE process-wide `Range` object rather than allocating per measurement, and parks it on a scratch `endNode` afterwards (rangeUtil.ts:17-30), (b) converts client rects to line-relative coordinates using `DomReadingContext.clientRectDeltaLeft`/`clientRectScale`, which are read from the *lines container* exactly once per render pass and cached (domReadingContext.ts:17-40) — including a `rect.width / offsetWidth` scale factor so measurements stay correct when the editor sits inside a CSS `transform: scale()`, and (c) merges adjacent rects with a 0.9px tolerance for browser rounding (rangeUtil.ts:61). It also snaps back to the predicted monospace value when the DOM read is within 1px of `spaceWidth * horizontalOffset` for basic-ASCII lines (viewLine.ts:650-656), which kills sub-pixel caret jitter between lines.

**Why it matters here.** Caret placement, selection rectangles, hit-testing and any overlay that needs an x-coordinate all go through this path. Making it lazy + memoized means a click on column 4 of a CJK line costs one client-rect read, not one per grapheme in the line; and the cached container rect + reused Range removes per-call allocation and per-call layout flushes. The `clientRectScale` correction additionally fixes measurement inside scaled/zoomed containers, which is a silent-wrong-answer class of bug.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/viewLine.ts:494-511 (_pixelOffsetCache: Float32Array), :612-626 (_readPixelOffset memoization), :628-658 (_actualReadPixelOffset); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/rangeUtil.ts:10-45 (single static reused Range + _detachRange), :74-89 (_createHorizontalRangesFromClientRects with clientRectDeltaLeft/clientRectScale + _mergeAdjacentRanges); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/domReadingContext.ts:6-51

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts:434-522 — `buildMeasuredRowGeometry` walks every chunk, every part, and every grapheme segment (`textPartSegments`) and calls `measuredTextSegmentRect` for each one, building the FULL boundary array before answering a single `offsetToX`. :668-691 `measuredTextSegmentRect` calls `node.ownerDocument.createRange()` fresh per segment, and `measuredRangeRect` calls `row.element.getBoundingClientRect()` per segment (so 2 rect reads and 1 Range allocation per grapheme). There is a per-row cache (:327-338 `ensureRowGeometry`) but it is all-or-nothing: the first query for any column pays for the whole row. No `clientRectScale` equivalent — we subtract `rowRect.left` but never divide by a scale factor, so measurements are wrong inside a CSS-transformed host.

**Verifier.** Real, but scope it correctly. `rowUsesCalculatedGeometry` (:373-376) routes every simple-ASCII row without an inline mapping to the pure-arithmetic path, so plain code never touches this. The eager path fires on (a) any row with non-ASCII text and (b) EVERY row carrying an inline mapping — which per our markdown-view architecture is the entire WYSIWYG document. So this is a markdown-mode cost, not a code-editing cost, and that raises its value rather than lowering it. Three separable wins, ordered by effort: (1) hoist `row.element.getBoundingClientRect()` to one read per geometry build (currently once per grapheme) — ~10 lines, immediate; (2) module-level reusable Range instead of `createRange()` per segment; (3) the lazy Float32Array-with-sentinel scheme, which is the big one but requires reworking `boundaries` from an eager array into a fill-on-demand cache and touches `xToOffset`/`rangeSegments`. Also add `clientRectScale` — without it we measure wrong under any CSS-transformed host (zoom UIs, Electron scaling), and that is a correctness bug, not a perf one.

**How to implement.** Change `RowGeometry` from an eagerly-built `boundaries` array into a lazy structure: keep the `chunks`/`parts` layout (which we already compute cheaply and without DOM), and add a `Float32Array` of per-local-offset x values initialised to NaN plus a `resolve(localOffset)` that measures just the containing segment on demand and fills its two endpoints. Hoist a module-level `const sharedRange = document.createRange()` in virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts to replace the per-call `createRange()` in `measuredTextSegmentRect`. Introduce a `RowReadingContext` created once per render pass in `renderSnapshot` (virtualizedTextView.ts:806) holding the cached `view.spacer.getBoundingClientRect()` and a `scale = rect.width / spacer.offsetWidth`, threaded into `measuredRangeRect`/`measuredElementRect` in place of the per-segment `row.element.getBoundingClientRect()`. Keep `rangeSegments` (:181-197) working by resolving only the two endpoints per chunk instead of every boundary.

### Deferred true line-width measurement to fix the horizontal scroll extent

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Three-tier strategy. (1) During `renderText`, `_updateLineWidthsFast()` asks each visible line for its width but only if `getWidthIsFast()` — true when the line is short-and-monospace (pure arithmetic) or its `offsetWidth` is already cached. (2) If any line couldn't answer cheaply, a `RunOnceScheduler` is armed; when it fires (off the critical path) `_updateLineWidthsSlow()` reads real `offsetWidth` for every visible line. (3) The clever part: `DomReadingContext` tracks a `didDomLayout` flag that any code path sets when it forced a layout for some *other* reason (a caret measurement, a widget position). `_updateLineWidthsSlowIfDomDidLayout` (viewLines.ts:533-544) then cancels the scheduled slow pass and does it immediately — because the layout is already clean, the reads are free. Widths only ever grow (`_ensureMaxLineWidth`), except when the whole document is rendered, where `_maxLineWidth` is reset to 0 so the scrollbar can shrink (viewLines.ts:564-567).

**Why it matters here.** Our horizontal scroll extent is a pure character-cell estimate, so any line whose real width exceeds the estimate cannot be scrolled to its end. This bites exactly where our editor is differentiated: markdown inline replacements render headings in a larger/bold font, and CJK/emoji/ligature lines are wider than `visualColumnLength * characterWidth`. Our own CSS comment already documents this as a known regression.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/viewLines.ts:521-572 (_updateLineWidthsFast / _updateLineWidthsSlow / _updateLineWidths), :533-544 (_updateLineWidthsSlowIfDomDidLayout), :652-657 (RunOnceScheduler dispatch in renderText); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/viewLine.ts:395-397 (getWidthIsFast), :383-393 (getWidth); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/view/domReadingContext.ts:8-15,48-50 (didDomLayout)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:1717-1780 — `updateContentWidth` → `scanVisualColumns` → `estimatedDisplayRowWidthPx(view, row) / characterWidth(view)`. Purely arithmetic; no DOM width is ever consulted, and there is no deferred correction pass. packages/editor/src/style.css:166-177 states the tradeoff explicitly: "horizontal scroll extent now trusts the JS column estimate (visualColumnLength), which undercounts double-width glyphs." `maxVisualColumnsSeen` is monotonic and only reset via `resetContentWidthScan` (virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:1621).

**Verifier.** Real user-visible bug: a line of CJK or emoji cannot be scrolled to its end, because every wide glyph counts as one cell. But the implementation is cheaper for us than the claim implies — we already compute a true measured width and then throw it away. virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts:441-445 sets `RowGeometry.width` from measured boundaries, and grep shows `width` is never read off `RowGeometry` by any caller. So the deferred pass is: after render, for rows where a geometry cache already exists (i.e. width is free, Monaco's `getWidthIsFast()`), feed `geometry.width` into `maxVisualColumnsSeen`; for the rest, schedule an idle pass. Note `contain: size` means row `offsetWidth` is useless to us — measure the chunk spans or the Range, not the row — so do not port Monaco's `_getReadingTarget().offsetWidth` literally. Also `maxVisualColumnsSeen` is monotonic (only reset by `resetContentWidthScan`, :1621), so a corrected-upward width sticks correctly without further plumbing.

**How to implement.** Add `rowWidthIsFast(row)` returning true when `rowUsesCalculatedGeometry(row)` holds (pure math) or a cached `row.measuredWidth` exists. In `updateContentWidth`, use the fast value where available and, when any visible row can't answer, arm a debounced idle task (we already have `packages/editor/src/editor/secondaryWorkScheduler.ts` and `workScheduler.ts` for this) that reads `row.element.scrollWidth`/`offsetWidth` for the visible rows and calls `applyContentWidth` with the real maximum. Note our `.editor-virtualized-row` uses `contain: layout paint style size` (style.css:177), so measure the inner text span rather than the row box, or temporarily drop `size` containment for the measured row. Mirror Monaco's reset rule: when the visible range covers the entire document, clear `maxVisualColumnsSeen` first so the scrollbar can shrink after a long line is deleted.

### Measure-back-and-correct loop with scroll-position compensation for variable-height rows

`partial` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **codemirror5**

**What it is.** CM5 renders lines into normal document flow inside a `mover` div whose `top` is set to `viewOffset` (update_display.js:128-130) — so it does NOT need to know a line's height before rendering it. After the DOM update, `updateHeightsInViewport` reads each rendered line's real `getBoundingClientRect()` height, compares it to the stored estimate, and calls `updateLineHeight` on mismatch. Crucially it accumulates `mustScroll -= diff` for every corrected line whose *bottom is above the viewport top* (`if (oldHeight < viewTop)`), and at the end applies `display.scroller.scrollTop += mustScroll` when the drift exceeds 2px. That is scroll anchoring: correcting an estimate above the fold no longer yanks the content the user is reading. Then `postUpdateDisplay` loops — recompute `visibleLines` from the corrected heights, and if the drawn range no longer covers the viewport, render again — until it converges.

**Why it matters here.** Any editor with measured-height rows has this bug latently: a row above the viewport reports its real height, the height index shifts, and the text under the user's cursor jumps by that delta. It is one of the most visceral 'this editor is janky' symptoms, and it shows up exactly during async work (image loads, a widget expanding, a preview re-laying out). Note that the block-surface subsystem — the only thing that ever fed non-uniform row heights here — has since been deleted, so this is latent rather than live until the replacement variable-height primitive lands.

**Reference.** references/codemirror5/src/display/update_lines.js:8-49 (updateHeightsInViewport, `mustScroll` accumulation at :33 and application at :48; updateWidgetHeight at :53-58); references/codemirror5/src/display/update_display.js:165-195 (postUpdateDisplay re-render loop until the drawn range covers the viewport)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/rowHeightIndex.ts rebuilds the prefix array from `rowSizes` with no notion of a scroll correction. Nothing reads a measured row height back or compensates `scrollTop`: grep for `scrollTop +=` and `anchor` across src/virtualization returns no scroll-anchoring code. `renderSnapshot` (virtualizedTextView.ts) renders once and returns; there is no convergence loop equivalent to `postUpdateDisplay`. The measured-height feeder that made this observable (block surfaces) has been deleted, so `rowSizes` currently has no production producer.

**Verifier.** Status corrected missing → partial: we have the measure-and-correct convergence loop, we lack scroll anchoring. Retarget the recommendation entirely at the missing half: whatever ends up measuring a row's real height must, when that row sits above the current viewport top, add the delta to `scrollTop` before the next emit, so an image or preview resolving its height does not shove the user's cursor down the screen. This belongs with the replacement variable-height primitive rather than being buildable today — the block-surface controller that would have hosted it is deleted. The generalised CM5 'render into flow, measure everything, converge' architecture is NOT worth porting — it is incompatible with absolute row positioning and our virtualizer already converges via the ResizeObserver path.

**How to implement.** After `renderRows` in `renderSnapshot`, add a `reconcileMeasuredHeights(view, snapshot)` pass: for each mounted row whose height is measured rather than uniform, read its element's `getBoundingClientRect().height` (this belongs in the read phase from the previous finding), diff against `item.size`, and accumulate `mustScroll` for rows whose bottom is above `snapshot.scrollTop`. Feed corrected sizes back through `createRowHeightIndex` (or the incremental version from the sparse-index finding), then apply `view.scrollElement.scrollTop += mustScroll` when `|mustScroll| > 2`. Wrap `renderSnapshot` in a bounded loop (CM5 loops unbounded; 3 iterations is enough in practice) that re-runs while the corrected height index means the drawn range no longer covers the viewport. Our existing `ResizeObserver` in fixedRowVirtualizer.ts:194,454 gives an async path for widgets that change size later — route it through the same compensation function so async growth above the fold is also anchored.

### Type-enforced read/write phase separation (RenderingContext vs RestrictedRenderingContext)

`missing` · value **high** · effort **L** · api-design · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Every frame runs exactly four ordered phases: `prepareRenderText` (pure model work, computes ViewportData), `renderText` (writes line DOM only), `prepareRender` (the ONLY phase allowed to read geometry — each view part is handed a full `RenderingContext` and stashes computed strings/positions in local fields), `render` (writes only — each part gets a `RestrictedRenderingContext`, whose type simply does not have `visibleRangeForPosition` or `linesVisibleRangesForRange`). Because the restriction is expressed in the type signature `render(ctx: RestrictedRenderingContext)` (viewPart.ts:31), a view part physically cannot measure during the write phase; the compiler enforces the read/write batching that everyone else enforces by convention and gets wrong. `renderText` also re-collects `_getViewPartsToRender()` afterwards (view.ts:644-646) because writing lines may itself fire scroll events that dirty more parts. The whole thing is wrapped so that `contain: strict` and layer hinting are applied to the lines container once per frame (viewLines.ts:672-676).

**Why it matters here.** This is the single structural mechanism that prevents layout thrash. Without it, every new overlay someone adds (whitespace dots, scope lines, inline hints, diagnostics squiggles) silently interleaves a read between two writes and turns one forced layout per frame into N.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/view/viewPart.ts:27-32; references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/view/renderingContext.ts:17-73 (RestrictedRenderingContext) and :75-105 (RenderingContext adds linesVisibleRangesForRange/visibleRangeForPosition); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/view.ts:567-580 (_flushAccumulatedAndRenderNow) and :593-661 (_createCoordinatedRendering)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextView.ts:836-854 `renderSnapshot` runs write → read → write in one synchronous pass: `renderRows` writes row DOM (virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:111-121) and then, at :120, calls `renderHiddenCharacters` which calls `offsetToX` per whitespace character (virtualizedTextViewHiddenCharacters.ts:102-103) — a geometry read that for measured rows hits `getBoundingClientRect` — and immediately writes marker spans back (:55-58). Control then returns to `renderSnapshot`, which reads/writes highlights. There is no phase concept and no restricted context type; `packages/scope-lines/src/index.ts` and other plugin contributions render off the same snapshot with no read/write discipline.

**Verifier.** True gap, but its value is much lower for us than for Monaco, and the claim overstates the thrash. `contain: layout paint style size` on `.editor-virtualized-row` (style.css:166-177) makes each row its own relayout boundary, so writing marker spans into row N does not dirty row N+1's layout — the interleaving costs far less here than in Monaco's uncontained line DOM. On top of that `offsetToX` only touches the DOM for measured rows; simple-ASCII rows resolve arithmetically, and default `hiddenCharacters` is 'show-on-selection'. Recommend the lightweight version and skip the type hierarchy: (a) split `renderHiddenCharacters` into a marker-computation pass over all rows followed by a DOM-write pass, and (b) document the read-then-write contract for plugin render hooks (packages/scope-lines, packages/minimap, packages/find, packages/diff all render off the same snapshot with no discipline today). The `RenderingContext`/`RestrictedRenderingContext` class split is a large refactor whose main payoff — preventing accidental reads in write phase — is what the doc comment plus the two-pass split already buys.

**How to implement.** Introduce two types in virtualizedTextViewTypes.ts: `EditorRenderReadContext` (exposing `offsetToX`, `rangeSegments`, row lookup, viewport data) and `EditorRenderWriteContext` (viewport data + scroll geometry only, no measurement functions). Restructure `renderSnapshot` into: (1) write rows and block lanes; (2) a read loop where hidden characters, scope lines, and any plugin `viewContribution` compute their layout into plain data (arrays of {left,width,top}); (3) a write loop that applies that data to the DOM. Change `renderHiddenCharacters` to split into `prepareHiddenCharacters(view): MarkerData[][]` and `applyHiddenCharacters(view, data)`, moving the `offsetToX` calls out of `renderRows`. Extend `packages/editor/src/editor/viewContributions.ts` to expose the same two-phase hook so plugins inherit the discipline. Pair this with the shared `RowReadingContext` from the first finding so the read phase pays exactly one container-rect read.

### Sticky scroll: enclosing-scope header from the fold/outline model, with a push-out transition

`missing` · value **high** · effort **L** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Candidates come from a nested tree (outline model, falling back to folding/indentation — stickyScrollModelProvider.ts), queried by binary search for the elements intersecting the visible range. For each candidate, if `topOfElement > topOfBeginningLine && topOfElement <= bottomOfEndLine`, the header line belongs on screen. The 'push out' effect is one line of arithmetic: when the widget's own bottom would overlap the end of the scope (`bottomOfElement > bottomOfEndLine`), `lastLineRelativePosition = bottomOfEndLine - bottomOfElement` — a negative offset applied to every sticky line, so the stack slides up and out rather than popping. Crucially the widget reuses `renderViewLine` with a real `RenderLineInput`, so sticky lines get identical tokens, decorations and font metrics to the lines they mirror; there is no second rendering path to keep in sync.

**Why it matters here.** Sticky scroll is the highest-visibility rendering feature we lack, and it is the difference between navigating a 400-line function comfortably and not. The push-out arithmetic is what makes it feel native rather than a jumpy header.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/stickyScroll/browser/stickyScrollController.ts:608-639 (findScrollWidgetState); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/stickyScroll/browser/stickyScrollProvider.ts:164-171,176-200 (getCandidateStickyLinesIntersecting — binary search over the nested StickyElement tree, then filterHiddenRanges); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/stickyScroll/browser/stickyScrollWidget.ts:19,465-475 (the widget renders its lines through the SAME renderViewLine/RenderLineInput pipeline as real lines), :322 (per-line top adjusted by _lastLineRelativePosition)

**Our code.** grep -rEl 'stickyScroll|sticky-scroll' over packages/**/*.ts and *.css returns nothing. However the feeders exist: packages/editor/src/editor/folds.ts:23-85 supplies `VirtualizedFoldMarker` ranges from tree-sitter, and packages/scope-lines/src/index.ts:264-320 already implements `createScopeGuides` → `candidateFoldMarkers` → `markerIntersectsVisibleRows` → `visibleTextRowBounds`, i.e. exactly the 'which scopes intersect the viewport' query sticky scroll needs. There is no viewport-pinned overlay host today; sticky scroll has to add one.

**Verifier.** Genuinely missing and worth building; the hard parts are already done. `rejectCrossingFoldRanges` giving us a non-crossing marker set means the ancestor chain for a viewport top row is a straight filter, no outline model needed — Monaco falls back to folding/indentation for exactly this and we start there. One correction to the claim's original implementation sketch, which proposed hosting the header on the (since-deleted) block-lane layer: anything living in document flow inside the translated spacer scrolls away with the content, and a sticky header must be pinned to the viewport. The right host is a new layer alongside `caretLayerElement`/`blockLaneLayerElement` (virtualizedTextView.ts:201-202, 320-327) using the same `position: sticky` technique the gutter already proves at style.css:107-115. The push-out transition is trivial arithmetic once the header layer exists. Budget the work as: ancestor query (reuse scope-lines logic, ~free) + sticky layer + row-content cloning for the header text.

**How to implement.** Build it as a plugin next to scope-lines, reusing `candidateFoldMarkers`/`markerIntersectsVisibleRows` (packages/scope-lines/src/index.ts:277-320) to produce the candidate list, then port `findScrollWidgetState` (stickyScrollController.ts:608-639) verbatim — the arithmetic needs only `getTopForRow`/`getBottomForRow`, which our virtualizer's `rowHeightIndexStart` provides. Render the sticky lines by calling into the same row-building code as `createRow`/`updateRowTextChunks` (virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:158-228, :791-806) rather than a second renderer, so tokens and inline replacements match; a mounted-but-unmanaged row pool is the natural fit. Position the widget absolutely over the text pane in editor coordinates and apply `lastLineRelativePosition` as a translateY on the whole stack. Filter out candidates inside collapsed folds, as `filterHiddenRanges` does.

### Semantic render-input equality as the invalidation key, not a scroll-position-derived string

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** A line re-renders only when the *inputs that determine its HTML* differ. Everything else — scroll position, viewport bounds, sibling lines — is deliberately excluded from the comparison, because it changes the line's `top` (handled by `layoutLine`, a single style write) and nothing else. The dirty flag is additionally gated per event type: `onSelectionChanged` returns false, i.e. does not invalidate, unless the theme is high-contrast or `renderWhitespace === 'selection'` (viewLine.ts:94-100) — the selection is drawn by a separate overlay, so moving it must not re-render text.

**Why it matters here.** An invalidation key that mixes in scroll position turns a smooth scroll into a re-render storm. This is a concrete live cost in our chunked long-line path.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/viewLine.ts:175-178 (`if (this._renderedViewLine.input.equals(renderLineInput)) return false`), :81-100 (onContentChanged/onTokensChanged/onDecorationsChanged/onSelectionChanged set _isMaybeInvalid, and onSelectionChanged returns false unless whitespace-on-selection or high contrast actually depends on selection); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/viewLayout/viewLineRenderer.ts:175-209 (RenderLineInput.equals compares the 20-odd fields that actually affect the rendered HTML)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:1081-1090 `rowChunkKey` returns `${window.start}:${window.end}:${snapshot.viewportWidth}:${snapshot.scrollLeft}` — raw `scrollLeft` is in the key, so the key changes on every pixel of horizontal scroll even though `window.start`/`window.end` are quantised to `longLineChunkSize` (:1111-1113) and usually do NOT change. That key is stored as `row.chunkKey`, and `rowGeometryCacheKey` (virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts:340-358) includes `row.chunkKey` — so every 1px of horizontal scroll discards and rebuilds the measured geometry of every chunked row. `horizontalWindowKey` (:1142-1151) likewise floors `scrollLeft` into `rowsKey`, so `renderSnapshot`'s early-out at virtualizedTextView.ts:812 never fires while horizontally scrolling a document containing any long line.

**Verifier.** Best value-to-effort item in the set. The fix is two lines: drop `snapshot.scrollLeft` from `rowChunkKey` (the window bounds it derives already encode everything that changes the rendered chunks) and floor/quantise it in `horizontalWindowKey` to `longLineChunkSize` boundaries rather than whole pixels. Effect: horizontal scrolling across a long line stops rebuilding measured geometry and stops re-rendering rows on every pixel, and the render early-out starts firing. No architectural change, no new abstraction — we already have the semantic-equality mechanism, one field is polluting it.

**How to implement.** Drop `snapshot.scrollLeft` (and `viewportWidth`, which is already implied by the window bounds) from `rowChunkKey` — the aligned `window.start:window.end` pair is the complete description of what was rendered. Do the same in `horizontalWindowKey`: key on the quantised window, not on `Math.floor(scrollLeft)`. Then verify the chunk window is still recomputed on scroll (it is — `horizontalChunkWindow` reads the live snapshot at :1092-1115) so correctness is unaffected while the re-render only fires when the window actually shifts by a chunk. Longer term, replace the string keys with a small `RowRenderInput` object plus an `equals` method mirroring RenderLineInput.equals, which makes it structurally impossible to smuggle a viewport coordinate into an invalidation key.

### Whitespace rendering as an overlay, with boundary/trailing modes and an advance-width-matched dot glyph

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Two things worth stealing. (1) Mode semantics: `boundary` shows leading/trailing runs plus interior runs of ≥2 spaces but not single word-separating spaces (:187-194); `trailing` shows only whitespace after the last non-whitespace char, and suppresses itself on wrapped continuations (:128-130); tabs are always shown. (2) Glyph choice: font metrics measure BOTH U+00B7 MIDDLE DOT and U+2E31 WORD SEPARATOR MIDDLE DOT, and the renderer picks whichever advance width is closer to the font's actual space width (`wsmiddotDiff < middotDiff ? 0x2E31 : 0xB7`, :144-149) — so inserting the dot never nudges the following text. Tabs render as U+FFEB halfwidth rightwards arrow when the font supports it, else U+2192 (:223), or as a hand-drawn SVG path scaled to `spaceWidth` (:242-262). The overlay form is preferable to the in-line form for the same reason our hidden-character layer is: it does not split tokens into extra spans, so ligatures, letter-spacing and bidi layout are untouched, and switching selection under `renderWhitespace: 'selection'` does not re-render any text.

**Why it matters here.** We already chose the right architecture (overlay) but implement only two of the four useful modes, and we position markers by width rather than drawing a metric-matched glyph. `boundary` in particular is the mode most users leave on permanently, because it surfaces the indentation and alignment mistakes that matter without visually shredding prose.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/whitespace/whitespace.ts:124-240 (_applyRenderWhitespace overlay), :141-149 (middotWidth vs wsmiddotWidth comparison selecting U+00B7 or U+2E31), :182-202 (trailing and boundary predicates, including the wrapped-line single-trailing-space exception), :214-227 (SVG vs div output), :242-262 (_renderArrow); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/viewLayout/viewLineRenderer.ts:752-914 (the in-line variant, incl. the RTL guard at :829-839 that refuses to split whitespace inside RTL tokens because it would change the browser's bidi layout)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewHiddenCharacters.ts implements an overlay with modes 'hidden' | 'show' | 'show-on-selection' (:23-30, :118-126) — no 'boundary', no 'trailing'. `hiddenCharacterKind` (:112-116) only distinguishes space and tab; the marker is a bare span sized by `left`/`width` (:142-153) with the glyph left to CSS, so there is no advance-width matching and no tab-arrow rendering. The per-row work is also unbatched: `renderHiddenCharactersForRow` builds a `|`-joined string key from every marker (:134-140) and calls `replaceChildren` with freshly created spans on any change (:55-58), for every visible row on every render pass (:32-36).

**Verifier.** Partial confirmed; two independent items, and the perf one is worth more than the feature one. (1) Perf: the `hiddenCharactersKey` early-out at :48-52 guards only the DOM write. `hiddenCharacterMarkersForRow` (:59-72) still runs for every visible row on every render pass (:32-36) and calls `offsetToX` TWICE per whitespace character (:102-103) to build the key it will then compare. On an indented file that is hundreds of geometry lookups per frame to usually discover nothing changed. Gate marker recomputation on a cheap precondition (row textRevision + chunkKey + selection revision) before touching geometry. (2) Feature: 'boundary' and 'trailing' modes are contained additions to `hiddenCharacterKind`/`shouldShowHiddenCharacterAtOffset` and are what users actually want — 'show' is too noisy to live with, 'show-on-selection' too quiet. The advance-width-matched middot needs a metrics probe alongside browserMetrics.ts's existing one; low effort, and it fixes the visible misalignment when the dot glyph is wider than a space in the user's font.

**How to implement.** Extend `HiddenCharactersMode` in virtualizedTextViewTypes.ts:87 with 'boundary' and 'trailing' and port the predicates from whitespace.ts:182-202 into `shouldShowHiddenCharacterAtOffset` (virtualizedTextViewHiddenCharacters.ts:118-126) — they need only `firstNonWhitespaceIndex`/`lastNonWhitespaceIndex` of `row.text`, both cheap. Add the middot measurement to packages/editor/src/virtualization/browserMetrics.ts:24-47 (measure U+00B7 and U+2E31 alongside the existing character width) and emit the chosen codepoint as the marker's text content instead of relying on CSS. Render tabs as the arrow glyph. While there, hoist the key computation: markers already carry `left`/`width` derived from `offsetToX`, so the key can be `row.textRevision + ':' + mode + ':' + selectionRevision` rather than a string built from every marker.

### Long-line defences: token-span splitting, render truncation with an escape hatch, and bounded coordinate magnitudes

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · performance · from **both**

**What it is.** Three independent defences. (1) `splitLargeTokens` caps any single `<span>` at 50 characters — splitting at spaces when ligatures or non-ASCII are involved, blindly otherwise — because browsers read character positions out of very large text nodes in super-linear time. (2) `stopRenderingLineAfter` truncates the rendered HTML at N columns and appends a rendered overflow count; `LongLinesHelper` then watches for a click at a column beyond the cutoff and flips the option off for that editor, so the truncation is invisible until the user actually needs the tail. (3) `bigNumbersDelta`: when the viewport's vertical offset exceeds 500,000px, all rendered `top` values are expressed relative to a delta that is itself floored to a multiple of the default line height (linesLayout.ts:647-648) — so the numbers browsers must lay out stay small and the sub-line phase is preserved — while the lines container's own height is clamped to 1,000,000px (viewLines.ts:618).

**Why it matters here.** We chose a better long-line strategy than truncation (horizontal chunk windowing), so (2) is mostly moot for us — but (1) and (3) are orthogonal to it. Our chunks can still contain very large text nodes, and our absolute row positioning uses raw document coordinates, which for a multi-million-line file exceeds the magnitudes browsers position reliably.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/viewLayout/viewLineRenderer.ts:569-645 (splitLargeTokens, Constants.LongToken = 50, with the microsoft/vscode#6885 rationale), :471-479 (stopRenderingLineAfter truncation) and :1205 (renderOverflowingCharCount); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/longLinesHelper/browser/longLinesHelper.ts (clicking past the truncation point disables it for the session); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/viewLayout/linesLayout.ts:642-651 (bigNumbersDelta, quantised to a multiple of defaultLineHeight) and references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/viewLines.ts:618,674-676 (domNode height clamped to 1e6; lines positioned relative to the delta)

**Our code.** We have the better half: packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:1076-1115 (`shouldChunkLine` + `horizontalChunkWindow`) renders only the horizontally visible window of a long line, aligned to `longLineChunkSize` — strictly better than `stopRenderingLineAfter`. What is missing: `createRowChunk` (:1039-1075) does not cap the size of an individual text node within a chunk, and `setDirectRowText`/`isSimpleRowText` (virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts:81-87) put an entire un-chunked line into a single Text node, so a 9,000-character line under `longLineChunkThreshold` is one enormous text node that Range measurement must scan. On magnitudes: `positionRowElement` (virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:1204-1217) writes `translateY(${top}px)` with `top = item.start`, an absolute document coordinate, and `applyTotalHeight` (:1812) sets the spacer to the full `snapshot.totalSize` with no clamp — grep for `1000000`, `maxHeight`, `33554` across src/virtualization returns nothing.

**Verifier.** Status unchanged at partial, but the survey's evidence needs the correction above before anyone acts on it — we already solved the browser scroll-height ceiling, and a plan that re-implements it would be wasted work. Scope the remaining work to the text-node cap alone: split chunk text into ≤50-char Text nodes inside `createRowChunk`, and apply the same cap on the `setDirectRowText` path for sub-threshold long lines. That directly speeds up `measuredTextSegmentRect`'s Range scans and composes with claim 1's measurement fixes. The residual magnitude concern is minor and I would not chase it: `positionRowElement` (:1204-1217) still writes an unclamped logical `translateY`, so on a document past 16M logical px the transform value exceeds the clamped scroll space; float precision degrades but nothing breaks, and `logicalScrollTopForNative` keeps hit-testing consistent.

**How to implement.** For (1): in `createRowChunks` (virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:1019-1038), subdivide any text part longer than ~50-100 chars into multiple Text nodes — split at spaces when `!isSimpleRowText`, at fixed stride otherwise, exactly as splitLargeTokens does. This is invisible to `offsetToX` because `appendTextBoundaries` already iterates parts. For (3): thread a `verticalDelta` through the snapshot — computed in fixedRowVirtualizer's `scrollGeometry` as `floor(scrollTop / 500000) * 500000` re-floored to a multiple of the row height — subtract it in `positionRowElement` and add it back in the inverse mapping used by hit-testing and `selectionReveal`. Clamp the spacer height (`applyTotalHeight`) to ~1e6 while keeping the logical total in `snapshot.totalSize`, which requires the scroll position to be scaled; alternatively note this as a known ceiling and just add the `verticalDelta`, which fixes precision without the scaling complexity.

### CharacterMapping: a packed bidirectional column↔DOM-position index

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **M** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** The line renderer emits HTML and, in the same pass, records for every column: which `<span>` index it landed in, the char offset within that span, and its *visual* (tab-expanded, full-width-aware) horizontal offset. Both go into flat `Uint32Array`s — one 32-bit word per column, no objects. Column→DOM is O(1) array indexing. DOM→column inverts by binary search over the monotonically-increasing packed `(partIndex<<offset | charIndex)` words, and `getColumnOfNodeOffset` recovers the span index by walking `previousSibling` from the hit node. `getHorizontalOffset(column)` is the visual-column accumulator that both the monospace fast path and the key-column extrapolation multiply by `charWidth`.

**Why it matters here.** It collapses three separate concerns — tab expansion, DOM node identity, and pixel prediction — into one array built once per line render, with zero allocation per query. Our equivalent is an array of `{offset, x}` objects plus a second sorted copy for hit-testing, which is both heavier and rebuilt wholesale on invalidation.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/viewLayout/viewLineRenderer.ts:221-354 — _data: Uint32Array packing (partIndex, charIndex) per column, _horizontalOffset: Uint32Array; :241-248 setColumnInfo; :250-256 getHorizontalOffset; :271-276 getDomPosition; :278-341 getColumn/partDataToCharOffset (binary search over the packed entries, with a tie-break for positions sitting between parts); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/viewParts/viewLines/viewLine.ts:724-734 getColumnOfNodeOffset

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts:524-537 `geometryFromBoundaries` produces `boundaries: GeometryBoundary[]` (objects) plus `hitBoundaries: boundaries.toSorted(compareBoundaryX)` — a full second array copy per row purely for x→offset lookup. :561-666 does linear/binary scans over these object arrays. Our DOM→offset direction is handled separately and structurally: :1033-1145 (`offsetFromTextPartBoundary`, `offsetFromControlPartBoundary`, `partForNode`, `partContainsNode`) walk part lists to identify which node a boundary belongs to, rather than indexing. Tab expansion lives in a third place (`bufferColumnToVisualColumn`, `estimatedDisplayCellForColumn` at :205-215).

**Verifier.** Partial is right, and the strongest sub-finding is one the survey missed: the per-character object allocation applies to the CALCULATED path too, i.e. plain ASCII code, the common case. Two objects and a sort per row on every geometry rebuild is pure garbage pressure during scroll. Highest-value, lowest-risk change: replace `GeometryBoundary[]` + `hitBoundaries` with two parallel `Float64Array`s (offsets, x) — offsets are already monotonic so the second sorted copy is unnecessary except where inline replacements reorder, which can be detected in one pass. That is contained to virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts and preserves both public functions' signatures. The DOM→offset direction is a separate, smaller win: `partForNode` is O(chunks × parts) and runs on every `selectionchange`; a WeakMap<Node, part> built at chunk-creation time fixes it without a packed index. packedTokens.ts is the in-repo precedent to follow for style.

**How to implement.** Replace `RowGeometry.boundaries`/`hitBoundaries` with two typed arrays sized to `row.text.length + 1`: a `Float32Array` of x positions (indexed by local offset, so offset→x is O(1) with no search, and NaN doubles as the 'not yet measured' sentinel needed by the lazy-measurement finding) and a `Uint32Array` packing `(partIndex, localOffsetWithinPart)`. x→offset becomes a binary search over the Float32Array, which is monotonic for LTR rows — dropping the `toSorted` copy entirely. Keep the existing part-walking DOM→offset code as the fallback for control/inline-replacement parts, but use the packed array for plain text parts. Fold `bufferColumnToVisualColumn` into the same fill loop so tab expansion is computed once per row instead of per boundary.

### Sparse, lazily-summed layout index instead of a dense per-line prefix array

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **M** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco does not store a height per line. Uniform lines are pure arithmetic; only *deviations* — view zones (`EditorWhitespace`) and explicitly customised line heights — live in a sorted array of W entries. `getVerticalOffsetForLineNumber` is `accumulatedLineHeights(n-1) + whitespacePrefixSum(binarySearch(n)) + paddingTop`. The prefix sums over that array are computed lazily and incrementally: `_prefixSumValidIndex` records how far the sums are valid, an insertion at index i only sets it back to i-1, and `getWhitespacesAccumulatedHeight(index)` extends the sums forward only as far as the caller actually asked. Insert order is stabilised by `(afterLineNumber, ordinal)` so two zones anchored to the same line keep a deterministic order.

**Why it matters here.** With a dense array the memory and the rebuild cost are both O(document), so a 2M-line file costs ~32MB of layout index and a full re-walk every time one block widget changes height. With the sparse form both are O(number of deviations), which is typically single digits — a file with no widgets costs zero.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/viewLayout/linesLayout.ts:385-399 (getWhitespacesAccumulatedHeight with _prefixSumValidIndex), :271/:277/:297/:317 (mutations only invalidate from the touched index backwards), :430-455 (_findLastWhitespaceBeforeLineNumber binary search), :486-499 (getVerticalOffsetForLineNumber = uniform line arithmetic + whitespace prefix sum), :115-140 (findInsertionIndex ordered by afterLineNumber then ordinal)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/rowHeightIndex.ts:7-19 `createRowHeightIndex` allocates `rowStarts` of length n+1 and walks all n rows on every construction — an O(n) rebuild with O(n) memory, with no incremental invalidation and no uniform-height fast path. Lookups (:25-44) then binary-search that dense array. fixedRowVirtualizer.ts (`if (options.rowHeightIndex) return options.rowHeightIndex.totalSize`) shows the index is optional, so the uniform path does exist when no index is supplied — but as soon as one row needs a custom height, the whole document pays for a dense index. No production code supplies `rowSizes` today; the machinery is retained for the replacement variable-height primitive.

**Verifier.** Partial confirmed. Reframe the recommendation, though: Monaco's headline win — storing zero per-line data — is unavailable to us, because `view.model.rows` is already a materialised dense display-row array, so `rowStarts` is a constant-factor addition to something O(n) we already pay. The win that DOES apply is `_prefixSumValidIndex`-style suffix invalidation. Concretely: any producer that hands `setOptions` a fresh `rowSizes` array on every ResizeObserver tick fails `cachedRowHeightIndexValid`'s identity check and triggers a full O(n) `createRowHeightIndex` — on a 100k-line document with one resizing row, a 100k-element rebuild per animation frame while it settles. Tracking a valid-prefix watermark and re-summing only from the changed row onward is a contained change to rowHeightIndex.ts plus a dirty-row hint from the layout caller. This only becomes live again when a variable-height row producer exists; it is negligible in plain code.

**How to implement.** Rewrite `RowHeightIndex` as `{ defaultRowHeight, deviations: Array<{ row, height, prefixSum }>, prefixSumValidIndex }`. `startForRow(r)` = `r * defaultRowHeight + accumulatedDeviation(lastDeviationBefore(r))`; `rowAtOffset` binary-searches the deviation array then solves the uniform remainder arithmetically. Port `_prefixSumValidIndex` verbatim from linesLayout.ts:385-399 so mutating one block height is O(1) plus a lazy forward extension. This also makes the height corrections from the scroll-anchoring finding cheap enough to run every frame, and it removes the O(n) rebuild that currently sits between a block widget resizing and the next paint.
