## Cursor, selection & multi-cursor

### Multi-cursor merge rules: touching vs overlapping, direction preservation, and last-added priority

`partial` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco's `normalize()` sorts a copy of the cursors by range start and merges neighbours, but the merge predicate is asymmetric: if either selection is empty, merge when they merely *touch* (`nextStart.isBeforeOrEqual(currentEnd)`); if both are non-empty, merge only on strict *overlap* (`isBefore`). Two adjacent non-empty selections therefore survive as two cursors. When a merge does happen, the surviving state is chosen by original cursor index — the lower-indexed (older) cursor is the 'winner' and its LTR/RTL direction is kept, *except* when the loser is `lastAddedCursorIndex`, in which case the last-added cursor's direction wins and `lastAddedCursorIndex` is transferred to the survivor. Sort order never becomes identity order: `cursors[0]` stays the primary cursor, is what reveal/scroll follows, and is the one that survives Escape (`killSecondaryCursors`).

**Why it matters here.** Ours merges any two selections that touch, so Ctrl+D-selecting two adjacent identical tokens (`abab` → `ab|ab`) collapses them into one selection instead of two cursors, and typing then replaces the whole run. Merging also throws away direction and goal column, so after two cursors collide, Shift+Arrow reverses meaning and Up/Down loses the column. And because we treat `selections[0]` (document order) as primary, Escape from a multi-cursor set keeps the topmost cursor rather than the one the user started from, and reveal jumps to the top of the document.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorCollection.ts:158-252 (normalize), :19-32 (cursors[0] is primary, lastAddedCursorIndex), :140-142 (killSecondaryCursors keeps cursors[0]), :144-148 (_addSecondaryCursor updates lastAddedCursorIndex), :157-160 (_removeSecondaryCursor shifts it)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/selections.ts:209-210 — `shouldMergeRanges = right.start <= left.end`, a single unconditional touching test with no empty/non-empty distinction. :233-250 `mergeResolvedSelections` hard-codes `reversed: false` and `goal: SelectionGoal.none()`, discarding both. :200-207 `compareResolvedSelections` sorts by offset and ties on `id.localeCompare`, and :275 returns the sorted array, so document order becomes cursor order. There is no `lastAdded` concept anywhere. packages/editor/src/documentSession.ts:705-710 and :1013-1018 collapse to `normalized.selections[0]` — the topmost, not the original.

**Verifier.** True and this one has a user-visible bug attached, not just a fidelity difference: Escape after adding a cursor below jumps the caret to the top of the document region rather than back to where you were. Three independent fixes, all cheap and all in selections.ts: (1) make `shouldMergeRanges` take both ranges' collapsed-ness and require strict overlap when both are non-empty; (2) in `mergeResolvedSelections`, take reversed/goal from whichever input's head survives as the merged head instead of hardcoding false/none; (3) add a `lastAddedIndex` alongside the selection set so Escape and Cmd+D anchor on it. (3) is the one with real reach — Cmd+D (claim 10) depends on it too.

**How to implement.** In packages/editor/src/selections.ts, change `shouldMergeRanges` to take both resolved selections and apply the empty/non-empty split. In `mergeResolvedSelections`, keep the winner's `reversed` and `goal` instead of resetting them. Add an `order`/creation-index field to `Selection<T>` (or reuse a monotonic counter in the existing `createSelectionIdFactory` at :73) so `normalizeSelections` can sort for merging but restore creation order for the returned array, keeping index 0 = primary; then `documentSession.ts:705/1013` collapse to the true primary for free. A `lastAddedIndex` on `SelectionSet` set by `addSelection` (inputSelectionController.ts:931) gives the direction-priority rule.

### Cmd+D from a collapsed caret is whole-word and case-sensitive, and searches from the last-added selection

`partial` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** The session captures search settings at creation time and remembers whether it owns them. Starting from a bare caret it forces wholeWord+matchCase and disconnects from the find widget's state, so Cmd+D on `foo` will not match the `foo` inside `foobar` or `Foo`. `currentMatch` makes the first press a pure 'select the word under the caret' with no search at all. Subsequent presses anchor the search at the end of the *most recently added* selection, which is what makes Cmd+D feel like it walks forward from where you last were even when earlier cursors sit later in the document.

**Why it matters here.** In our editor, placing the caret in `id` and pressing Cmd+D repeatedly picks up `id` inside `width`, `valid`, `hidden` — the classic wrong-rename accident, because the very next thing users do is type over all the cursors. This is a small fix with a large blast radius avoided.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/multicursor/browser/multicursor.ts:276-325 (MultiCursorSession.create: `if (selections.length === 1 && selections[0].isEmpty()) { isDisconnectedFromFindController = true; wholeWord = true; matchCase = true; }`, and the empty-selection branch expands to `getConfiguredWordAtPosition`), :368-383 (_getNextMatch: `currentMatch` is consumed once so the first Cmd+D selects the word under the caret rather than jumping, then search resumes from `allSelections[allSelections.length - 1].getEndPosition()` — the **last added** selection, not the last in document order), :335-347 / :387-399 (addSelectionToNext/Previous), :440-452 (selectAll)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/occurrences.ts:99-115 `findExactOccurrenceFrom` uses plain `text.indexOf(query)` with no boundary test; :45-58 `findAllExactOccurrences` likewise. :60-76 `findNextExactOccurrence` computes `searchStart = selected.reduce((o, r) => Math.max(o, r.end), 0)` — the maximum end across *all* selections, i.e. document order, not the last added. :20-28 `getOccurrenceQuery` takes `selections.find(s => !s.collapsed)` — the first non-collapsed in document order — as the query source. We already have the needed predicate: packages/editor/src/textRanges.ts:133-141 `isWholeWordRange`, used by packages/find/src/search.ts:205 but never by occurrences.ts.

**Verifier.** True but smaller than presented — two of the four sub-behaviours (currentMatch first press, case sensitivity) are already right, and the last-added anchor is already implemented for moveSelectionToNextFindMatch. The remaining work is genuinely small: pass a wholeWord flag into `findExactOccurrenceFrom` gated on 'the session started from a collapsed caret' and filtered through the existing `isWholeWordRange`, and switch `addNextExactOccurrence` from `resolved[0]`/`findNextExactOccurrence` to `resolved.at(-1)`/`findNextExactOccurrenceFromRange` — i.e. make it match the sibling function 30 lines above it. The 'session remembers whether it owns the search settings' idea is the part we'd be building new, and it is the part that makes wholeWord not leak into the find widget.

**How to implement.** Thread a `{wholeWord: boolean; matchCase: boolean}` session state through `addNextExactOccurrence` (packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:1382-1411), set to `{true, true}` on the branch that already detects `resolved.length === 1 && primary.collapsed` (:1391), and reset it when the user starts from a real selection. In `findExactOccurrenceFrom` (occurrences.ts:99), reject candidates failing `isWholeWordRange(text, range)` when the flag is set. Change `searchStart` to derive from a tracked last-added selection index rather than `Math.max` over ends — the same `lastAdded` field proposed for the merge-rules finding.

### Grapheme-cluster movement and emoji-aware backspace (move ≠ delete granularity)

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **both**

**What it is.** Monaco deliberately uses two different granularities. Arrow-key movement steps a full extended grapheme cluster: `MoveOperations.leftPosition` calls `strings.prevCharLength`, which runs a `GraphemeIterator` over code points and consults a `GraphemeBreakTree` implementing UAX#29 rules GB3-GB11 (no break before Extend/ZWJ/SpacingMark, no break inside Hangul L/V/T sequences, no break inside emoji-modifier or ZWJ sequences, no break between paired regional indicators). Backspace uses a *different* function, `strings.getLeftDeleteOffset`, which first tries `getOffsetBeforeLastEmojiComponent`: it walks back over skin-tone modifiers / VS16 / keycaps, expects an emoji base, then optionally consumes a preceding ZWJ plus its emoji — so backspace on 👨‍👩‍👧 peels off one family member per press, while backspace on `e`+U+0301 removes only the combining accent. Move-left over the same text jumps the whole cluster. CM5 does a cheaper version of the same idea with a hand-written `extendingChars` regex and `skipExtendingChars`.

**Why it matters here.** Today an arrow key in our editor can park the caret between a base character and its combining mark, or between the two halves of a ZWJ emoji sequence. Rendering then shows the caret mid-glyph, selection highlights are half-glyph wide, and Delete/Backspace can leave orphaned combining marks or lone ZWJs in the buffer. Any user writing Hindi, Thai, Korean, Arabic, or just emoji hits this immediately.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/base/common/strings.ts:586-648 (GraphemeIterator, nextCharLength, prevCharLength), :1007-1090 (breakBetweenGraphemeBreakType, GB3-GB11), :1168-1183 (getLeftDeleteOffset), :1185-1235 (getOffsetBeforeLastEmojiComponent); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorMoveOperations.ts:30-37 (leftPosition uses prevCharLength), :110-118 (rightPosition uses nextCharLength); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorDeleteOperations.ts:222-231 (getPositionAfterDeleteLeft uses getLeftDeleteOffset); references/codemirror5/src/util/misc.js:143-152 (isExtendingChar/skipExtendingChars); references/codemirror5/src/input/movement.js:6-13 (moveCharLogically)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/textRanges.ts:43-75 — `previousCodePointOffset`/`nextCodePointOffset` handle surrogate pairs only (charCode 0xD800-0xDFFF checks) and nothing else; `codePointSizeAt` returns 1 or 2. packages/editor/src/editor/navigationTargets.ts:206-209 `codePointOffset` is what cursorLeft/cursorRight/selectLeft/selectRight use. packages/editor/src/documentSelectionEdits.ts:340-351 `backspaceRangeForSelection` deletes exactly one code point. grep for grapheme/Intl.Segmenter across packages/*/src finds hits only in packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts:68-79,831-838 — i.e. we already segment graphemes for *measurement* but not for *movement*, so measurement and caret placement disagree.

**Verifier.** True gap. Note the duplication: a grapheme fix has to land in BOTH textRanges.ts (string-based, for navigation) and documentSelectionEdits.ts (piece-table-based, for backspace), or move/delete will diverge again. Cheapest correct route given our architecture: extend the existing `fallbackTextSegments`/`consumeCombiningSuffix` machinery in virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts into a shared grapheme primitive rather than porting Monaco's GraphemeBreakTree — we already ship the Intl.Segmenter path and a fallback, they are just in the wrong package. Keeping move=grapheme / delete=grapheme-with-partial-emoji-decomposition is the Monaco nuance worth copying.

**How to implement.** We already construct an `Intl.Segmenter({granularity:'grapheme'})` in virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts:835; lift it into `packages/editor/src/textRanges.ts` as `previousGraphemeOffset`/`nextGraphemeOffset` operating on a line-local slice (segmenting the whole document string is not viable — slice the line via `lineRangeAtOffset`, segment, binary-search the boundary). Point `codePointOffset` in navigationTargets.ts:206 at the grapheme version. Keep backspace on a separate function: code-point delete plus a small `getOffsetBeforeLastEmojiComponent` port (needs only isEmojiModifier / VS16 / keycap / ZWJ code-point tests, ~40 lines) wired into `backspaceRangeForSelection`. Add a fallback path for environments without Intl.Segmenter using CM5's `extendingChars` regex.

### Word navigation has three distinct stop policies; ours is a fourth that matches none

`partial` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco models a word as `{start, end, wordType: Regular|Separator, nextCharClass}` found by scanning one line, where every character is classified Regular / Whitespace / WordSeparator. Crucially a *run of punctuation* is itself a word (`WordType.Separator`), so `foo.bar` has three words. Four navigation policies then sit on top: **WordStart** stops at word starts; **WordStartFast** (the default for Ctrl+Left) is WordStart plus one rule — if the previous word is a single separator character immediately followed by a Regular character, skip it, so Ctrl+Left from `foo.|bar` goes to `foo` not to `.`; this rule is disabled when `hasMulticursor` is true so multiple carets do not diverge (cursorWordOperations.ts:227-240). **WordEnd** (the default for Ctrl+Right) stops *after* the last character of a word, so Ctrl+Right from `|foo bar` lands at offset 3, not 4. **WordAccessibility** skips separator-only words entirely to match screen-reader expectations. Word characters are not a Unicode property test: a cached `WordCharacterClassifier` (LRU of 10) is built from a configurable `wordSeparators` string, defaulting to ``` `~!@#$%^&*()-=+[{]}\|;:'",.<>/? ``` — everything else, including CJK and emoji, is Regular. Optional `Intl.Segmenter({granularity:'word'})` locales layer real CJK/Thai word segmentation on top (findPrevIntlWordBeforeOrAtOffset / findNextIntlWordAtOrAfterOffset, results cached per line).

**Why it matters here.** Concretely: in our editor Ctrl+Shift+Right on `foo bar` selects `foo ` (with the trailing space) because we always consume trailing whitespace, whereas every other editor selects `foo`. Ctrl+Left from `foo.|bar` stops on the `.` instead of jumping to `foo`. Word motion also silently walks across line breaks in ours. These are the highest-frequency keystrokes in an editor, so the mismatch is felt constantly.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorWordOperations.ts:42-47 (WordNavigationType enum), :80-115 (_doFindPreviousWordOnLine), :117-141 (_findEndOfWord), :211-261 (moveWordLeft), :306-366 (moveWordRight); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/wordOperations/browser/wordOperations.ts:123-139 (cursorWordLeft = WordStartFast, bound to Ctrl/Alt+Left), :238-246 + :218-235 (cursorWordEndRight = WordEnd, bound to Ctrl/Alt+Right); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/core/wordCharacterClassifier.ts:18-95

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/textRanges.ts:98-110 — `previousWordOffset` = skip spaces backward then skip one class run (≈WordStart, no fast rule); `nextWordOffset` = skip spaces, skip one class run, *then skip trailing spaces again* (≈WordStart with whitespace eaten, never WordEnd). :88-96 `characterClassAt` classifies with regexes `\s`, `[\p{L}\p{N}_]` — not a configurable separator set, so `$`, `#`, `@` are punctuation here but word characters under Monaco defaults for some languages. No `\n` guard anywhere in these loops, so motion crosses lines. packages/editor/src/editor/navigationTargets.ts:79-96 exposes exactly one left/right pair; packages/editor/src/editor/commands.ts:75-78 has only cursorWordLeft/Right + selectWordLeft/Right — no WordStart/WordEnd/Accessibility variants.

**Verifier.** True. Two separable wins, and the second is much cheaper than the first: (a) a configurable `wordSeparators` string per language replacing the hardcoded WORD_PATTERN — this is what makes `$foo` in PHP/jQuery or `@media` in CSS behave right, and it is a ~20-line change to characterClassAt; (b) treating a punctuation run as its own word so `foo.bar` is three words, which our `characterClassAt` already almost gives us since 'punctuation' is a distinct class — `previousWordOffset` already stops at class transitions, so we are closer to Monaco here than the claim implies. The line-crossing bug in (\n classified as space) is the highest-value single fix in this claim and is a two-line guard.

**How to implement.** Rewrite `previousWordOffset`/`nextWordOffset` in packages/editor/src/textRanges.ts to first slice the line via the existing `lineRangeAtOffset` (bounding the scan to the line and fixing the newline-crossing bug), then port `_doFindPreviousWordOnLine`/`_doFindNextWordOnLine` returning `{start,end,wordType,nextCharClass}`, and add a `WordNavigationType` parameter. Default `cursorWordLeft` to WordStartFast (passing `hasMulticursor = selections.length > 1`, available at inputSelectionController.ts:617-621) and `cursorWordRight` to WordEnd. Replace the per-character regex in `characterClassAt` with a Uint8Array-backed classifier built once from a configurable separators string (see the perf finding). Add the extra command ids to packages/editor/src/editor/commands.ts so keymaps can pick a policy per platform.

### Atomic soft-tab movement and tab-stop-aware backspace inside indentation

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Two related mechanisms. (1) `stickyTabStops`: while the caret is within a line's leading whitespace, Left/Right jump a whole `tabSize` worth of columns instead of one space. `whitespaceVisibleColumn` walks the indent once, returning the previous tab-stop position, its visible column, and the caret's visible column, bailing with -1 on any non-whitespace so the rest of the line is untouched. Going left it then re-scans forward to confirm the indent between the previous tab stop and the caret is a *full* tabSize — this is why `'      foo'` with tabSize 4 moves 6→5 (partial indent, one char) rather than 6→4. Going right it walks to `nextRenderTabStop`. `Direction.Nearest` is used by mouse hit-testing so a click inside an indent snaps to a tab stop. (2) `useTabStops` (default true, independent of stickyTabStops): backspace inside indentation deletes back to `prevIndentTabStop(visibleColumn, indentSize)` — one indent level, not one space.

**Why it matters here.** With soft tabs (spaces), our backspace currently removes one space at a time, so unindenting a line takes 2-4 presses instead of one — this is a daily-friction bug for any spaces-indented codebase. The Left/Right half additionally stops the caret from landing in visually meaningless positions inside indentation.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorAtomicMoveOperations.ts:22-50 (whitespaceVisibleColumn), :62-160 (atomicPosition with Left/Right/Nearest); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorMoveOperations.ts:41-51 (leftPositionAtomicSoftTabs), :120-130 (rightPositionAtomicSoftTabs, both gated on config.stickyTabStops and on being inside the line's indent); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorDeleteOperations.ts:193-220 (getDeleteLeftRange, gated on config.useTabStops which is ON by default)

**Our code.** grep for stickyTabStops|atomicSoftTab|atomicTab across packages/editor/src returns nothing. packages/editor/src/documentSelectionEdits.ts:340-351 `backspaceRangeForSelection` unconditionally deletes one code point back — no indent check, no tabSize. packages/editor/src/editor/editActions.ts only references tabSize at :831-853 for explicit outdent (`outdentLineEdit`/`outdentLength`), never for backspace. We do have the required primitives: `bufferColumnToVisualColumn`/`visualColumnToBufferColumn` and `view.tabSize` in packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewLayout.ts:260-297.

**Verifier.** True, but split it. The backspace-to-previous-tab-stop half is high value / low effort: `outdentLength` in documentSelectionEdits.ts:267-271 is literally the algorithm already, it just is not reachable from backspace — the change is a caret-is-in-leading-whitespace guard in `backspaceRangeForSelection` reusing it. The `stickyTabStops` arrow-movement half is off by default even in Monaco and is a genuine niche preference; I would implement it only behind an option, or not at all. Worth flagging the deeper design point: our horizontal motion has no access to view/tabSize at all (navigationTargets.ts:19-23 NavigationTargetView exposes offsetAtLineBoundary/offsetByDisplayRows/pageRowDelta/visualColumnForOffset but nothing indentation-related), so the arrow half is a wider plumbing change than the backspace half.

**How to implement.** Start with the backspace half — highest value per line of code. In `backspaceRangeForSelection` (documentSelectionEdits.ts:340), when the selection is collapsed, read the line via `lineRangeAtOffset`, compute `firstNonWhitespaceIndex`; if the caret is at or before it, convert caret→visible column with the existing `bufferColumnToVisualColumn`, round down to the previous multiple of indentSize, convert back, and delete that range. Needs `tabSize`/`indentSize` threaded into `SelectionEditResult` options (the shape at documentSelectionEdits.ts:40 already carries `tabSize?`). Then port `AtomicTabMoveOperations.atomicPosition` verbatim (~90 lines, pure string+number, trivially unit-testable) and call it from `horizontalTarget` in navigationTargets.ts:58 behind an option, plus `Direction.Nearest` from `textOffsetFromPoint` in the mouse path.

### Mouse dispatch matrix: shift-click extend, and word/line-granularity drag via a Range-valued anchor

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · feature · from **both**

**What it is.** The decisive design choice is that the anchor is a *Range*, not a point, tagged with a granularity kind. Double-click sets `selectionStart` to the whole word with `SelectionStartKind.Word`; every subsequent drag position is then snapped to word boundaries and `_computeSelection` picks the far end of the anchor range, which is what makes double-click-and-drag select whole words in both directions and never shrink below the original word. Triple-click does the same with a line-sized anchor. Shift+click is not a special case at all — it is simply `inSelectionMode = e.shiftKey` fed into the same dispatch, so shift+click after a double-click extends *by word*.

**Why it matters here.** Three very ordinary gestures are unavailable in our editor today: shift+click to extend a selection, double-click-then-drag to select by word, and triple-click-then-drag to select by line. Shift+click in particular is muscle memory for selecting a large region without dragging through a scrolling viewport.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/view/viewController.ts:217-310 (full dispatch matrix: middle button, gutter-started line drags, count>=4 select-all, count 3 line/lineDrag, count 2 word/wordDrag/lastCursorWordSelect, single click with multicursor modifier, alt+shift = columnSelect); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/controller/mouseHandler.ts:459-500 (drag-and-drop of an existing selection is preferred over re-selecting; `_dispatchMouse(position, e.shiftKey, ...)` — shiftKey *is* inSelectionMode); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursorCommon.ts:342-399 (SingleCursorState.selectionStart is a **Range**, selectionStartKind Simple|Word|Line, and _computeSelection picks whichever end of that anchor range is away from the position); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorWordOperations.ts:741-820 (WordOperations.word: first entry sets a Word-kind anchor; subsequent inSelectionMode calls snap the moving end to whole-word boundaries and flip which anchor end is used)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:891-921 `handleMouseDown` — branches only on `event.detail` (>=4, 3, 2) and `event.altKey`; a grep for `shiftKey` across packages/editor/src/editor/*.ts (only keymap.ts hits) confirms no shift handling in the mouse path. The detail===2 and detail===3 branches `return` immediately after `selectWordAtOffset`/`selectLineAtOffset` (:1103-1111, :1095-1101), so no drag monitor is started at all. packages/editor/src/selections.ts:24-30 — `Selection<T>` carries `start`/`end`/`reversed`; the anchor is recoverable only as a single offset (`anchorOffset`, :181-182), so a word- or line-sized anchor is not representable. packages/editor/src/editor/mouseSelection.ts is 45 lines of auto-scroll math only.

**Verifier.** True and this is the highest value-per-effort item in the whole list. Shift-click alone is table stakes that we simply do not have — every native text control and every editor does it. The Range-valued-anchor design is the right thing to copy verbatim: widening `MouseSelectionDrag` from `anchorOffset: number` to `anchor: {start, end, kind: 'char'|'word'|'line'}` and snapping the drag head through the existing `wordRangeAtOffset`/`lineRangeAtOffset` (textRanges.ts:34-40, :112-131) gets shift-click, double-click-drag and triple-click-drag from one change, because all three reduce to 'anchor is a range, head is snapped to the same granularity, pick the far end'. Note the drag path also calls `session.materializeFullText()` per double/triple click (:1098, :1106) — see claim 13.

**How to implement.** Extend `MouseSelectionDrag` (mouseSelection.ts:4-9) from `anchorOffset: number` to `anchor: {start, end}` plus `granularity: 'char'|'word'|'line'`. In `updateMouseSelectionFromDragPoint` (:1006-1020), expand the pointer offset with the existing `wordRangeAtOffset`/`lineRangeAtOffset` (textRanges.ts:35-41, :112-131) when granularity is word/line, then union with the anchor range and set head to the far end. Have the detail===2/3 branches fall through into `startMouseSelectionDrag` with the matching granularity instead of returning. Add a shift branch at the top of `handleMouseDown` that keeps the current selection's anchor and only moves the head. Longer term, promoting `SelectionGoal`-style metadata to a `selectionStartKind` on `Selection<T>` would also make keyboard shift+arrow after a double-click behave correctly.

### Column (box) selection: a persistent from/to visual-column rectangle, driven by mouse and keyboard

`missing` · value **high** · effort **L** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Column selection is not N independent cursors; it is a single `IColumnSelectData` rectangle `{fromViewLineNumber, fromViewVisualColumn, toViewLineNumber, toViewVisualColumn}` stored on the cursor controller, from which the cursor list is *derived* on every change. `ColumnSelection.columnSelect` walks each line in the range, converts both visual columns to real columns with `columnFromVisibleColumn` (tab-aware), converts back to visible columns, and drops lines whose entire content falls outside the rectangle's column band — so short lines contribute no cursor rather than a clamped one, unless *every* line is short, in which case it falls back to a caret at each line end. The keyboard commands mutate only the `to` corner of the stored rectangle, which is why Ctrl+Shift+Alt+Right can push past the end of short lines and later re-acquire them (`columnSelectRight` computes the max visual column across the whole band to know when to stop). `_columnSelectData` is nulled by any edit (`_interpretCommandResult`, cursor.ts:395) and re-synthesised from the primary cursor with `isReal: false` when absent.

**Why it matters here.** Box selection over columnar data (aligned assignments, CSV-ish blocks, log columns) is one of the two or three reasons people reach for multi-cursor at all, and it is the case pure multi-cursor cannot express because short lines must be *skipped*, not clamped. We have neither the mouse nor the keyboard entry point.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorColumnSelection.ts:12-75 (columnSelect), :77-90 (columnSelectLeft), :86-105 (columnSelectRight scans for max visual column), :107-118 (columnSelectUp/Down with isPaged); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursor.ts:39,130,137-139,285-299,395 (_columnSelectData lifecycle, getCursorColumnSelectData synthesising isReal:false state from the primary cursor, invalidation on any edit); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/view/viewController.ts:217-310 (dispatchMouse: middle button, alt+shift+click, alt+drag, and the `columnSelection` option all route to _columnSelect)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/commands.ts:1-90 has no column-select command of any kind. packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:915-918 — the only altKey handling in mousedown is `addCursorAtOffset`; alt+drag is never distinguished from a plain drag (`startMouseSelectionDrag`, :938-955). No middle-button branch. mouseSelection.ts (45 lines) is only auto-scroll math.

**Verifier.** True gap, and genuinely worth it — column selection is one of the few remaining things people leave an editor over. But be honest about effort: the win in Monaco's design is that the rectangle is the source of truth and the N cursors are DERIVED on every change, which means our `SelectionSet` would need a parallel 'column select data' field on the session that survives normalization (selections.ts:36-40 SelectionSet has only {selections, normalized, normalizedFor}). We do already have both halves of the hard math — displayTransforms.ts:221-253 bufferColumnToVisualColumn/visualColumnToBufferColumn is exactly Monaco's columnFromVisibleColumn, and virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:1119-1124 already picks the fast vs estimated path. So the geometry is free; the state plumbing is the cost.

**How to implement.** The primitives exist: `visualColumnForOffset` and `offsetForViewportColumn` (packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewLayout.ts:260-297) are exactly Monaco's `visibleColumnFromColumn`/`columnFromVisibleColumn`, and `rowForOffset` gives display lines. Add a `columnSelect: ColumnSelectData | null` field alongside `mouseSelectionDrag` in inputSelectionController.ts, cleared in `applySessionChange` on any edit. Port `ColumnSelection.columnSelect` (~60 lines) mapping (row, visualColumn) pairs through the two existing helpers, and have it produce a `setSelections` payload. Wire the entry points in `handleMouseDown` (:891-921): alt+drag and middle button start a column drag; add `columnSelectLeft/Right/Up/Down/PageUp/PageDown` command ids that mutate only the stored `to` corner.

### Cursor undo/redo as an independent bounded stack

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **S** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** A separate 50-deep undo stack that records only pure cursor movements — the `oldModelVersionId === modelVersionId` test is what excludes selection changes caused by edits, and any content change wipes both stacks so cursor-undo can never fight document-undo. Each entry stores scroll position too, so Cmd+U returns you to where you were *looking*, not just where the caret was. The reentrancy flag stops the restore from recording itself.

**Why it matters here.** After a Ctrl+D run, a Go-to-Definition, or an accidental Ctrl+Home in a large file, there is currently no way back to the previous selection state except manual navigation. This is a small, self-contained feature with an outsized 'I can experiment safely' payoff, and it composes especially well with our multi-cursor and find features.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/cursorUndo/browser/cursorUndo.ts:16-35 (CursorState.equals), :37-43 (StackElement also captures scrollTop/scrollLeft), :66-93 (recording: cleared on model or content change, records `e.oldSelections` only when `oldModelVersionId === modelVersionId`, dedupes against the stack top, capped at 50), :96-120 (cursorUndo/cursorRedo push the current state onto the opposite stack), :113-121 (_applyState restores selections *and* scroll position under an `_isCursorUndoRedo` reentrancy guard)

**Our code.** grep for cursorUndo across packages/editor/src returns nothing; packages/editor/src/editor/commands.ts:2-3 has only `undo`/`redo`. packages/editor/src/history.ts (113 lines) stores `selections` inside each *document* history entry (:3,15,49,87,105) — i.e. selection is restored as a side effect of text undo, which is the opposite of what this feature needs.

**Verifier.** True gap but I'd rank it lowest-value of the missing items. It is a real, well-liked Monaco feature (Cmd+U), and the mechanism is genuinely clever — the `oldModelVersionId === modelVersionId` test is what makes it not fight document undo, and storing scroll position is what makes it feel right. We have `revision` on the session already, so that test is available to us cheaply. Keeping it, but as a low-priority nice-to-have rather than a correctness fix; if effort is constrained, claims 1, 7, 12 and 13 all beat it.

**How to implement.** New file packages/editor/src/editor/cursorHistory.ts holding two arrays of `{selections: EditorSelectionRange[], scrollTop, scrollLeft}`. Hook it in `applySessionChange` (packages/editor/src/editor/Editor.ts / inputSelectionController.ts) — we already pass a timing/source name per call, so 'was this an edit or a pure selection change?' is answerable without version-id comparison: clear both stacks on any change whose `edits.length > 0`, push the previous selections otherwise. Add `cursorUndo`/`cursorRedo` to commands.ts and bind Cmd+U in keymap.ts alongside the existing entries at :434.

### Word-part (subword) motion clamped between the word-start and word-end candidates

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco never lets the raw subword scanner decide alone. `moveWordPartLeft` computes three positions — `moveWordLeft(WordStart)`, `moveWordLeft(WordEnd)`, and the raw `_moveWordPartLeft` — sorts them with `Position.compare`, and takes the **last** (nearest to the caret going left); `moveWordPartRight` takes the **first** going right. The raw scanner therefore only ever gets to *shorten* a move that word navigation already bounded; it can never carry the caret past a word boundary, past whitespace, or (because `_moveWordPartLeft` returns column 1 / maxColumn at the line edges) across a line break. The delete variants apply the identical clamp to Ranges. This is a very cheap way to get correct subword behaviour without the subword scanner having to understand whitespace, punctuation, or newlines at all.

**Why it matters here.** Our subword scanner treats every non-alphanumeric character — including `\n` — as a separator to be skipped, so Alt+Ctrl+Right from the end of a line silently jumps to the next line's first subword, and from a position before whitespace it skips the gap and lands at the *end* of the next word rather than its start. Users lose their place, and it makes subword motion unusable for careful editing.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorWordOperations.ts:845-853 (moveWordPartLeft = max of three candidates), :855-863 (moveWordPartRight = min of three candidates), :825-833 / :835-843 (deleteWordPartLeft/Right use the same trick on Ranges via Range.compareRangesUsingEnds/Starts), :264-303 (_moveWordPartLeft: pure camelCase/snake_case/kebab-case scan that stops at column 1 and refuses to leave the line, with the `HTTPResponse` acronym rule at :291-301)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/textRanges.ts:196-233 — `nextWordPartOffset`/`previousWordPartOffset` run standalone with no word-boundary clamp. :248-252 `isSubwordSeparator` is `!/[\p{L}\p{N}]/u.test(char)`, which is true for `\n`, and both functions loop over separators unconditionally (:202, :222) so line breaks are traversed. Commands exist (packages/editor/src/editor/commands.ts:61-64, navigationTargets.ts:98-115) but there is no `deleteWordPartLeft`/`deleteWordPartRight` counterpart in commands.ts. Our acronym rule at textRanges.ts:236-246 is already equivalent to Monaco's.

**Verifier.** True. The clamp is the valuable half and it is nearly free for us: `wordPartTarget` in navigationTargets.ts:98-115 already has `context.text` and `context.resolved.headOffset`, so computing previousWordOffset/nextWordOffset alongside the raw result and taking min/max is a handful of lines — and it fixes the line-crossing bug as a side effect, since word motion would bound it. deleteWordPartLeft/Right is a straightforward addition to editActions.ts mirroring `deleteWordAction` (:194-212). Both worth doing.

**How to implement.** Once `previousWordOffset`/`nextWordOffset` take a `WordNavigationType` (see the word-navigation finding), wrap the existing calls in navigationTargets.ts:98-115: `previousWordPartOffset → Math.max(previousWordOffset(WordStart), previousWordOffset(WordEnd), rawPrevious)` and the symmetric `Math.min` going right. That is ~6 lines and fixes both the newline crossing and the overshoot without touching the subword scanner. Add `deleteWordPartLeft/Right` to commands.ts applying the same clamp to the delete ranges.

### Smart-select as a ranked ladder of ranges, not a single tree walk

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **M** · api-design · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Providers each contribute an unordered bucket of candidate ranges; the framework filters to ranges containing the caret, sorts them, then keeps only a strictly increasing containment chain (dropping duplicates and equal ranges), and finally interpolates two synthetic steps — 'the line's content without leading/trailing whitespace' and 'the whole line' — at every point where the chain jumps to a new line. The resulting ladder is computed once and cached with an index; Expand/Shrink just move the index, and `mov` skips steps that would not visibly change the selection. The state is invalidated by *cursor movement*, not by document version, and the bracket provider is written to yield inner-then-outer so `(a, b)` gives you the contents before the parens.

**Why it matters here.** Our expand/shrink jumps straight from the caret to the smallest named tree-sitter node, so the first press on `fooBar` selects the whole identifier — there is no subword step, no 'line without indentation' step, and no expansion at all in a buffer with no grammar (plain text, markdown, an unsupported language). Shrink is also only guarded by snapshot version, so clicking elsewhere and pressing Shrink pops a stale stack and teleports the selection.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/smartSelect/browser/smartSelect.ts:33-52 (SelectionRanges.mov skips ranges equal to the current one), :74-127 (run: computes the ladder once, prepends the live selection, and drops the whole state on any cursor move via onDidChangeCursorPosition), :245-276 (sort + keep only ranges that strictly contain the previous one), :277-300 (synthesised 'line without leading/trailing whitespace' and 'full line' steps inserted whenever a step crosses onto a new line); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/smartSelect/browser/wordSelections.ts:31-72 (_addInWordRanges: subword step from camelCase/snake_case/kebab-case), :74-79 (word step), :81-89 (whitespace-only-line step); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/smartSelect/browser/bracketSelections.ts:13-27,31-75,77-125 (bracket provider yields *both* inner and outer bracket ranges, and time-slices with _maxDuration=30ms across _maxRounds=2 so huge files never block)

**Our code.** packages/tree-sitter/src/structuralSelection.ts:45-55 (expand) and :57-71 (shrink) — a single tree-sitter provider, one range per press. packages/tree-sitter/src/treeSitter/treeSitter.worker.ts:1585-1615 — `expandedRangeAt` walks to `containingNamedNode`, so the ladder is exactly the named-node chain: no subword, no word, no trivia steps. :1600-1603 requires a parsed layer, so no grammar means no expansion. structuralSelection.ts:147-156 `rangesFromShrinkState` validates only `snapshotVersion !== options.snapshotVersion` — nothing invalidates on cursor movement. :136-142 rebuilds selections with `reversed: false`, losing direction.

**Verifier.** True, and worse than reported: the machinery is orphaned — no command id, no keymap entry, only tests call it. Ranking the work: (1) wire it to a command at all; (2) the synthetic word/line-content/whole-line steps, which are what make Cmd+Shift+Right useful in a file with no grammar and are pure string work we can do with the existing wordRangeAtOffset/lineRangeAtOffset; (3) invalidate the ladder on caret movement; (4) preserve `reversed`. The provider-bucket architecture (unordered candidates -> filter -> sort -> strictly-increasing chain) is worth adopting because it is what lets LSP selectionRange, tree-sitter, and the synthetic steps coexist — we have an LSP package that could contribute to it.

**How to implement.** Keep the tree-sitter worker as one provider but move ladder assembly to the editor package: a `selectionRangeProviders` array where the tree-sitter backend is one entry and a pure-text `WordSelectionRangeProvider` (port of wordSelections.ts, ~60 lines, reusing our `wordRangeAtOffset` at textRanges.ts:112) is another that always works. Add the containment-chain filter and the two synthetic line steps from smartSelect.ts:245-300 — they cost nothing and are what makes the ladder feel smooth. Invalidate the cached stack in structuralSelection.ts on any selection change rather than on snapshot version, mirroring the `_ignoreSelection` guard so our own writes do not self-invalidate.

### Auto-closing: token-aware quote suppression via a 'neutral character' probe, and multi-cursor surround

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Two ideas worth stealing. (1) Whether a typed quote *opens* or *closes* a string is undecidable from the characters alone, so Monaco asks the tokenizer: it pretends to insert a neutral character at the position and asks what token type results. If that says 'string', the quote is closing an existing string and must not auto-close. It refuses to force tokenization (`isCheapToTokenize`) so this never stalls typing — it degrades to not auto-closing. (2) All auto-close and surround decisions are evaluated across *every* selection and only applied if all agree, so multi-cursor typing of `(` or `"` behaves consistently; surround additionally refuses on whitespace-only selections (typing a quote over selected spaces should replace, not wrap) and on a single selected quote character.

**Why it matters here.** Practically: typing the closing `"` of a string in our editor inserts a new pair rather than terminating the string when the tracked-pair record has been lost, and none of our auto-close or surround behaviour happens at all with more than one cursor — so multi-cursor wrapping of several identifiers in quotes, a very common refactor, silently replaces them instead.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorTypeEditOperations.ts:171-280 (getAutoClosingPairClose), :253-262 (the neutral-character trick: `getTokenTypeIfInsertingCharacter(lineNumber, beforeColumn, neutralCharacter)` then `pair.isOK(tokenType)`), :249-252 (`isCheapToTokenize` bail-out — never force tokenization on the typing path), :234-240 (`_isBeforeClosingBrace` allows auto-close before another closer even when the char is not in autoCloseBefore), :283-306 (_findContainedAutoClosingPair for nested multi-char pairs), :395-440 (_isSurroundSelectionType: refuses when *any* selection is empty, refuses when the selected text is whitespace-only, and refuses when typing a quote over a single selected quote character)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/autoClose.ts:88-96 `shouldAutoClose` looks only at the immediately adjacent characters — no token/scope input, and we do have tree-sitter tokens available (packages/editor/src/editor/tokenIndex.ts, tokenProjection.ts). :105-113 `shouldTypeOverCloser` requires `trackedAtCaret`, i.e. type-over works only for pairs we inserted and still track (autoCloseStore.ts caps at MAX_TRACKED_PAIRS, :32). packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:325-332 `surroundSelection` returns null unless `selections.length === 1`, and :380-390 `collapsedCaretOffset` likewise returns null for multiple selections, gating every auto-close path. No whitespace-only guard and no quote-over-quote guard in `surroundSelection`. There is no equivalent of `_isBeforeClosingBrace`, so typing `(` just before an existing `)` does not auto-close even though it should.

**Verifier.** Status stands at partial, but recalibrate the evidence — the closing-brace claim is wrong, and we are further along on language-specific pairs than reported. Two things genuinely worth taking: (1) multi-cursor surround/auto-close, which is a real everyday gap (select three things with Cmd+D, type a quote, nothing wraps) and is mechanically simple since `surroundSelection` at :325-352 already builds a 2-edit batch per selection — it just needs to map over all selections instead of bailing; (2) the token probe for quotes, which is the clever idea and which we can actually implement because tokenIndex.ts/tokenProjection.ts already give us token types at an offset. Copy Monaco's refusal to force tokenization — the value depends on it staying cheap. The whitespace-only-selection guard on surround is a 1-line correctness nit worth folding in.

**How to implement.** Lift the single-selection restriction first: make `surroundSelection` (inputSelectionController.ts:325) map over all resolved selections and emit one pair of zero-width edits per selection — the edits are already at distinct offsets so the existing batch path works — and add the whitespace-only and quote-over-quote guards from _isSurroundSelectionType (~10 lines each). Then add `_isBeforeClosingBrace` to autoClose.ts:88 so `(` closes before `)`. For the quote/token check, query the existing token index at the caret and suppress auto-closing a quote when the caret is already inside a string or comment scope; where tokens are not yet available for that row, follow Monaco and simply do not auto-close rather than blocking on the worker.

### Vertical motion and Home/End from selection edges, wrapped lines, and first-non-blank

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Four related behaviours our code flattens. (a) Up/Down with a non-empty selection and no Shift collapse to the *top* / *bottom* of the selection respectively before moving, matching every native text control. (b) Home escalates: first press goes to the start of the wrapped view line, and once already there (or on the first wrapped row) it goes to the model line's first non-blank column, toggling to column 1 on a further press; End mirrors this. (c) `moveDown` retries up to 10 times when position normalization does not actually change line, which is what makes Down behave across folded regions and injected text. (d) Add-Cursor-Above/Below uses `translateUp`/`translateDown`, which shift *both* endpoints — so with a non-empty selection you get a duplicated selection on the next line, not a bare caret — and takes a `useLogicalLine` flag (true for the default keybinding) so the new cursor lands on the next *logical* line rather than the next wrapped row.

**Why it matters here.** Home not jumping to the first non-blank character is the single most-noticed missing motion in a code editor — it is how you get to the start of an indented statement. Up/Down from a downward selection currently moves from the wrong end, which surprises anyone who drag-selects then arrows. And Add-Cursor-Below on a selection gives a caret instead of a duplicated selection, so the common 'select a token, add the same selection on the lines below' gesture does not work.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorMoveOperations.ts:248-263 (moveUp: with a selection and not extending, moves relative to `selection.start`), :203-227 (moveDown: relative to `selection.end`, plus a retry loop `do { ... } while (i++ < 10)` that re-runs when `normalizePosition` fails to advance past hidden/injected content), :323-338 (moveToBeginningOfLine toggles between first-non-blank column and column 1), :340-344 (moveToEndOfLine with `sticky` sets leftoverVisibleColumns to MAX_SAFE - maxColumn so End-then-Down stays at end of line), :229-243 / :266-281 (translateDown/translateUp move *both* selectionStart and position, preserving each end's leftover columns); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorMoveCommands.ts:58-72 (_moveToLineStart: escalates from wrapped view-line start to model line start on a second press), :86-110 (_moveToLineEnd, symmetric), :18-46 (addCursorUp/Down duplicate the whole cursor *state*, and take a `useLogicalLine` flag)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/navigationTargets.ts:117-130 `verticalTarget` always uses `context.resolved.headOffset` — no start/end collapse for non-extending moves. :152-164 `lineBoundaryTarget` calls `view.offsetAtLineBoundary(head, 'start'|'end')`, which is packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextView.ts:650-655 → `lineStartOffset(view, row)` (virtualizedTextViewLayout.ts:300-308): the display-row start, with no first-non-blank step and no second-press escalation to the logical line. No `sticky` concept — `lineBoundaryTarget` returns no `goal`, so End-then-Down does not hold the end of line (inputSelectionController.ts:642 falls back to `SelectionGoal.none()`). packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:498-520 `applyInsertCursorCommand` pushes `{anchor: selection.anchor, head: selection.anchor}` — always collapsed — and uses `offsetByDisplayRows`, i.e. wrapped rows rather than logical lines. Our goal-column plumbing itself is sound (SelectionGoal.horizontal, selections.ts:14-22; navigationGoalColumn, navigationTargets.ts:196-199) and is tab-aware via `bufferColumnToVisualColumn`.

**Verifier.** True, and the best effort-adjusted cluster after claim 7 — these are four small independent changes in two files, all with the plumbing already present. (a) is ~3 lines and mirrors code that already exists 40 lines above it in the same file. (b) smart-Home is the one users notice most and `firstNonWhitespaceOffset` is already written, just in the wrong module. (c) needs a new SelectionGoal variant ('lineEnd') alongside the existing horizontal/horizontalRange — small, but it is a type change that ripples through goal handling. (d) preserving selection shape when inserting cursors above/below is the most arguable; the display-row-vs-logical-line choice is defensible in a wrapping editor and I would not change it blindly.

**How to implement.** In navigationTargets.ts:117, mirror `horizontalTarget`'s existing collapse logic: when `!extend && !resolved.collapsed`, start from `startOffset` for rowDelta<0 and `endOffset` for rowDelta>0. For Home/End, add a `firstNonBlankOffset(row)` helper next to `lineStartOffset` (virtualizedTextViewLayout.ts:300) and make `lineBoundaryTarget` compare the current head against the display-row start and the first-non-blank column to pick the next stop in the Monaco escalation order. For Add-Cursor, change `cursorSelectionByDisplayRows` (inputSelectionController.ts:1357-1370) to translate both `anchorOffset` and `headOffset` and emit a real selection, and expose a `logicalLine` variant that steps whole buffer lines using the existing `getLineStarts` view.

### Word/character operations are line-scoped with a Uint8Array classifier; ours materialize the whole document and run regexes per character

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **M** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Two compounding decisions. Every word operation reads exactly one line out of the piece table and scans it with `charCodeAt`, and the character classification is a direct array index into a 256-byte table rather than a predicate call. Even the expensive Intl.Segmenter path is memoized against the last line string so repeated Ctrl+Arrow within a line costs one segmentation, not one per keystroke.

**Why it matters here.** Every arrow keypress in our editor calls `session.materializeFullText()`. When the retained-text cache misses — which it does after any edit that does not retain (documentTextSnapshot.ts:42) — that is an O(document) string build on the keyboard path, and it is instrumented as a perf event precisely because it is known to be expensive. On top of that, each character examined during a word motion runs up to two `RegExp.test` calls with the `u` flag. On a large file, held-down Ctrl+Arrow is where this shows up as input lag.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/core/characterClassifier.ts:11-58 (CharacterClassifier: a 256-entry Uint8Array for ASCII plus a Map for the rest, O(1) `get(charCode)`); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/core/wordCharacterClassifier.ts:18-40 (built once from the wordSeparators string), :99-110 (LRUCache of 10 classifiers keyed by separators+locales), :63-77 (Intl.Segmenter results memoized per line, `_cachedLine === line` short-circuit); references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorWordOperations.ts:75-78 / :143-146 (every word operation starts from `model.getLineContent(position.lineNumber)` — one line, never the document)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:616 — `const text = session.materializeFullText()` runs on *every* navigation command including cursorLeft/cursorRight, then passes the full string into `navigationTargetForCommand` (navigationTargets.ts:29). Same at :1099 and :1107 for triple-click and double-click. packages/editor/src/documentTextSnapshot.ts:38-54 — `materializeFullText` returns the retained string only when `materializedText?.length === snapshot.length`, otherwise calls `materializePieceTableFullText`, wrapped in `measureEditorPerformance('textSnapshot.materializeFullText')` and `recordFullTextSnapshotRead`. packages/editor/src/textRanges.ts:88-96 `characterClassAt` runs `/\s/u.test` then `/^[\p{L}\p{N}_]$/u.test` per character, and is called once per character by `skipBackward`/`skipForward` (:143-164).

**Verifier.** Status corrected to missing: there is no line-scoping in the navigation path at all, and the absence of memoization means the cost is paid per keystroke on a dirty buffer, not once. This is the item most likely to be an actual user-visible problem on a large file — hold down ArrowRight after typing and you re-materialize the document per frame. Separate the two ideas by value: line-scoping is the real win and we already have every primitive for it (virtualizedTextViewLayout.ts:300-308 lineStartOffset/lineEndOffset, editActions.ts createLineMap, and documentTextSnapshot.ts:58 `readRange`) — `navigationTargetForCommand` should take a line-reader, not a full string. The Uint8Array classifier and the memoized-Segmenter-per-line are micro-optimizations that only matter once the O(document) allocation is gone; do not lead with them.

**How to implement.** Change the `NavigationTargetContext` in navigationTargets.ts:26-32 from `text: string` to a line-reader — `readRange` already exists on `DocumentTextSnapshot` (documentTextSnapshot.ts:14) and `lineRangeAtOffset` gives the bounds, so word/character operations can read one line. That removes the full-text materialization from the arrow-key path entirely (double/triple click at inputSelectionController.ts:1099,1107 can use the same reader). Separately, replace `characterClassAt` with a prebuilt classifier: a `Uint8Array(256)` for ASCII plus a `Map<number, class>` for the rest, constructed once from a configurable separators string and memoized — a direct port of characterClassifier.ts:11-58, about 40 lines, and it simultaneously delivers the configurable `wordSeparators` option the word-navigation finding needs.
