## Input, IME, clipboard & accessibility

### U+2028/U+2029 line separators are a rendering/geometry landmine

`missing` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** The text buffer records `containsUnusualLineTerminators` while the file is being chunked at load — a single boolean, so the expensive check is paid once and the common case (`false`) costs nothing on every subsequent content change. The contribution re-checks on model change and on content change (but not while undoing, to avoid fighting the user), and either silently strips the characters (`auto`) or prompts with a per-model 'ignore' flag persisted through the codeEditorService model-property bag. Stripping is done as a normal edit with `pushEditOperations(selections, ...)` so it is undoable and selections survive.

**Why it matters here.** This is the highest-severity-per-line-of-code item here. The piece table only treats \n as a line break, but CSS treats LS (U+2028) and PS (U+2029) as forced line breaks inside `white-space: pre` — which is exactly how rows are rendered. A single LS character in a file therefore makes one model row render as two visual lines: the row's measured height, `offsetToX`, hit-testing, caret placement, selection rectangles and the fixed-row virtualizer's height assumptions all silently desynchronise for the rest of that row, with no error anywhere. LS/PS appear in real files from JSON string literals and from text pasted out of Word/PDF.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/base/common/strings.ts:696-702 (UNUSUAL_LINE_TERMINATORS regex, containsUnusualLineTerminators); src/vs/editor/common/model/pieceTreeTextBuffer/pieceTreeTextBufferBuilder.ts:148-165 (the flag is computed once while chunking the file, never re-scanned); src/vs/editor/common/model/textModel.ts:746-754 (mightContainUnusualLineTerminators / removeUnusualLineTerminators pushes one edit per match and preserves selections); src/vs/editor/contrib/unusualLineTerminators/browser/unusualLineTerminators.ts:66-118 (auto | prompt | off, per-model ignore state, skips on undo and on read-only)

**Our code.** grep for `2028|2029` across packages/editor/src returns nothing; the only line-break scanning is on `'\n'` (pieceTable tests at pieceTable.test.ts:31, and `pasteRevealBlock` in inputSelectionController.ts:1580-1583 checks only \n and \r). packages/editor/src/style.css:35,85,184,223,234,279,354 set `white-space: pre` on the row/chunk elements.

**Verifier.** Real correctness landmine, low frequency, near-zero cost to defend — so keep, but implement it as part of item 7 rather than as its own feature. The mechanism worth copying is the cost model, not the UI: the flag is computed once during chunking at load, so the common `false` case makes every later content-change check free. Given our architecture the cheapest defence is even simpler than Monaco's banner: normalise U+2028/2029 to \n at the paste and setText boundaries and surface the rest through the item-7 highlighter.

**How to implement.** Two cheap layers. (1) Sanitise at the entry points: in `handlePaste` (:1163) and `handleDrop` (:1187), replace `[  ]` with `\n` (or strip) before `applyPasteText` — matching the existing `\r\n` expectations. (2) Detect at load: have the piece table builder set a `containsUnusualLineTerminators` flag while chunking initial text (pieceTable/snapshot.ts), and expose an editor option `unusualLineTerminators: 'auto' | 'prompt' | 'off'` whose `auto` path issues one `session.applyEdits` removing all matches, reusing the find machinery in editor/findFeature.ts to locate them. Add a regression test asserting row geometry for a line containing U+2028 — this is exactly the kind of bug the existing virtualizedTextViewLayout.test.ts suite is set up to catch.

### Clipboard carries no editor metadata, so multi-cursor copy/paste degenerates into one blob

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **both**

**What it is.** On copy Monaco writes three things to the DataTransfer: `text/plain`, optionally `text/html`, and a JSON blob under the private MIME type `vscode-editor-data` containing `{version, id, isFromEmptySelection, multicursorText, mode}` where `multicursorText` is the per-selection string array. It ALSO records the same metadata in a process-global `InMemoryClipboardMetadataManager` keyed by the exact copied text, so a paste that loses the custom MIME type (Firefox, OS clipboard round-trip, execCommand paths) can still be matched by comparing the pasted string to the last copied string. On paste, `getTextData` parses the custom MIME first and falls back to the in-memory match. `PasteOperation._distributePasteToCursors` then distributes: if `multicursorText.length === selections.length` each cursor gets its own fragment; else if `multiCursorPaste === 'spread'` and the line count happens to match the cursor count, the lines are split one per cursor; else it's a simple paste at every cursor. CM5 does the same thing more cheaply with a module-level `lastCopied = {lineWise, text: [...]}` compared by `text.join('\n') == inserted`, and also supports N cursors being an integer multiple of the copied fragment count (`sel.ranges.length % lastCopied.text.length == 0`).

**Why it matters here.** Select three identifiers with three cursors, copy, put three cursors elsewhere, paste: today each cursor receives all three strings concatenated with newlines, exploding one line into three. This is the single most common multi-cursor workflow and it currently produces garbage. The in-memory fallback also matters because private MIME types silently vanish through several real clipboard paths.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/clipboardUtils.ts:31-71 (generateDataToCopy), :78-99 (InMemoryClipboardMetadataManager), :131-159 (ClipboardEventUtils get/setTextData, 'vscode-editor-data' MIME), :22-29 (Firefox \r\n normalisation); src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorTypeEditOperations.ts:654-705 (PasteOperation._distributePasteToCursors / _distributedPaste); src/vs/editor/common/viewModel/viewModelImpl.ts:998-1049 (getPlainTextToCopy); codemirror5/src/input/input.js:14-21 (lastCopied), :30-42 (multiPaste distribution)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:1441-1456 `selectedTextForClipboard` maps every non-collapsed selection to text and returns `texts.join('\n')` — the per-selection structure is discarded. :1214-1221 `handleCopy` sets only `text/plain`. :1163-1185 `handlePaste` reads only `text/plain` and calls `applyPasteText` -> `session.applyText(text)`, which inserts the same string at every cursor. No custom MIME type or in-memory metadata store exists (grep for `clipboardData` in packages/ returns only these two sites; the former diff/src/DiffView.ts:446 site went away with `DiffView`, which now uses the editor's own copy path).

**Verifier.** Real gap, but scope it precisely: our plain-text form already matches Monaco's (join with \n). What is missing is the round-trip — copy N selections, paste with N cursors, get line i into cursor i. Verified reference: references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/clipboardUtils.ts:134 reads and :158 writes `vscode-editor-data`. The in-memory manager matters more than the MIME for us: browser clipboard sanitisers strip unknown types, so the durable path is a module-level map keyed by the exact text, with the MIME as the cross-window bonus. Effort: low — `applyPasteText` is a 5-line function and `getSelections()` already returns an ordered array; the only new state is the metadata store. Bundle this with item 3, they touch the same two handlers.

**How to implement.** Add `packages/editor/src/editor/clipboardMetadata.ts` holding a module-level `{lastCopiedText, metadata}` singleton plus `readClipboard(DataTransfer)` / `writeClipboard(DataTransfer, ...)` helpers using a private MIME (e.g. `application/x-editor-clipboard`). In `handleCopy`, keep the per-selection array instead of joining and write both plain text and metadata. In `handlePaste`, resolve metadata (MIME first, in-memory text match second) and, when `multicursorText.length === session.getSelections().selections.length`, build one TextEdit per selection through `session.applyEdits` rather than `applyText` — the batch path already exists and is used by `surroundSelection` (:325-355) for edits at distinct offsets. Normalise `\r\n`->`\n` before the in-memory key comparison as Monaco does for Firefox.

### No cut handler at all, and no empty-selection line copy/cut

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · feature · from **both**

**What it is.** With `emptySelectionClipboard` on (the default), Cmd+C with a collapsed caret copies the whole line including its terminator, and the metadata records `isFromEmptySelection: true`. On paste, `pasteOnNewLine` makes the text land at column 1 of the caret's line rather than at the caret, so copy-line/paste-line reproduces the line above/at the caret instead of splicing it mid-token. `getPlainTextToCopy` also de-duplicates: several empty cursors on the same line contribute the line only once (`if (modelLineNumber !== prevModelLineNumber)`). Cut with an empty selection deletes line N plus its newline, except on the last line where it instead eats the preceding newline, and `lastCutRange` prevents two cursors on adjacent lines from double-deleting. CM5's `copyableRanges` + `lastCopied.lineWise` implements the same contract in ~15 lines.

**Why it matters here.** Cmd+X currently does nothing in this editor — the browser's default cut cannot work because the content is not contenteditable and the hidden textarea is empty, so the user loses a core keybinding. And Cmd+C on a caret with no selection, which experienced users rely on to duplicate a line, silently copies nothing.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/native/nativeEditContext.ts:135-148 (cut listener: fire event, mark screen-reader ignore window, write clipboard, then viewController.cut); src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorDeleteOperations.ts:235-290 (DeleteOperations.cut full-line semantics incl. last-line special case and lastCutRange de-dup); src/vs/editor/common/viewModel/viewModelImpl.ts:1004-1046 (getPlainTextToCopy expands empty ranges to whole lines, one per distinct line, each with a trailing newline); src/vs/editor/contrib/clipboard/browser/clipboard.ts:222-228 (bail out when selection empty and emptySelectionClipboard off); codemirror5/src/input/input.js:105-114 (copyableRanges), :54-55 (lineWise paste inserts at column 0)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:109-123 `install()` registers mousedown/beforeinput/copy/drop/paste/keydown/composition*/keyup — there is no `cut` listener. :1441-1453 `selectedTextForClipboard` filters out collapsed selections and returns `null` when none remain, so an empty-selection copy is a no-op. grep for `'cut'` across packages/ finds no handler.

**Verifier.** Promote this above everything else in the domain: Cmd+X is a straight functional bug, not a missing nicety. The hidden textarea is `readOnly = true` (virtualizedTextViewHelpers.ts:166, re-asserted in virtualizedTextView.ts:517) with `value = ''`, so the native cut deletes nothing and copies nothing — Cmd+X is silently inert on a selection. Effort: trivial for the fix (one listener that reuses selectedTextForClipboard then routes through the existing delete path). The emptySelectionClipboard half is a small extension of `selectedTextForClipboard` plus a `pasteOnNewLine` flag in the metadata from item 2.

**How to implement.** Register `cut` next to `copy` in `install()`/`dispose()`. Factor `selectedTextForClipboard` into `clipboardPayload(): {text, perSelection: string[], isFromEmptySelection}` that, when every selection is collapsed and the (new) `emptySelectionClipboard` option is on, expands each caret to its full line via `lineRangeAtOffset` (editor/textRanges.ts, already used by triple-click at :1095-1101) with Monaco's same-line de-dup. `handleCut` writes that payload then routes through the existing edit machinery: `session.applyEdits` with the line ranges, mirroring `applyDeleteCommand` (:397-414) so undo/typing-run behaviour matches. Add the `pasteOnNewLine` branch to `handlePaste` using the metadata from the previous finding.

### Drag-and-drop of text is non-functional: no dragover, no dragstart, no move semantics

`partial` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco does not use HTML5 DnD at all for in-editor text movement; it drives it from its own mouse pipeline (onMouseDrag/onMouseDrop). On drag it remembers which existing selection contains the press position (`_dragSelection`), paints a caret-shaped `dnd-target` decoration at the hovered position, and switches the cursor to `copy` while the trigger modifier is held. On drop it pushes an undo stop, runs `DragAndDropCommand` as a single command — delete at source (skipped when copying) plus insert at target — and, crucially, `computeCursorState` returns a `targetSelection` computed for each case (drop below the selection, above it, same line before/after) so the moved text stays selected afterwards. Dropping inside the source selection is a no-op, except that dropping exactly on the selection's start or end with the copy modifier is allowed so you can duplicate a block beside itself.

**Why it matters here.** Two separate problems. (a) Dragging a selection to move it — a standard editor gesture — does not exist. (b) The existing external-drop handler is almost certainly dead code: the browser only fires `drop` on an element whose `dragover` was default-prevented, and nothing in this codebase does that, nor is the editor contenteditable. So dragging a file or text from another app onto the editor does nothing, and the handler that looks like it works has probably never fired in a real browser.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/contrib/dnd/browser/dnd.ts:104-203 (mouse down/drag/drop lifecycle, _dragSelection capture, trigger-modifier = Alt on mac / Ctrl elsewhere for copy, dnd-target decoration), :24-30 (hasTriggerModifier), :210-216 (showAt + revealPosition); src/vs/editor/contrib/dnd/browser/dragAndDropCommand.ts:27-107 (getEditOperations emits delete-source + insert-target as one command and computes the resulting selection for every geometric case)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:115 registers only `drop`; :1187-1212 `handleDrop` inserts `dropPlainText(event)` at the hit-tested offset with no source deletion. grep for `dragover|dragstart|draggable|contentEditable` across packages/editor/src returns nothing (the only `drag` hits are the internal `mouseSelectionDrag` variables). No drop-target decoration.

**Verifier.** Keep, but rank it last in this domain. Without a `dragover` handler that preventDefaults, the browser shows a no-drop cursor for most drags, so even the working `drop` path is reached inconsistently. The genuinely useful subset for us is small: (a) dragover → preventDefault + paint a caret-shaped target decoration (rangeDecorations.ts already gives us the decoration primitive), (b) mouse-driven move-vs-copy on the existing mouseSelection pipeline. Monaco deliberately avoids HTML5 DnD for in-editor moves — references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/dnd/browser/{dnd.ts,dragAndDropCommand.ts} — which is the right model for us since we already own mousedown/mousemove/mouseup. Effort: medium-high; value: moderate (most users move text with cut/paste).

**How to implement.** Add a `dragover` listener on `options.el` that calls `preventDefault()` and sets `dataTransfer.dropEffect`, plus a drop-caret indicator — the view already has a caret layer (`.editor-virtualized-caret-layer` in style.css) and `rangeDecorations.ts` for transient ranges. For in-editor move, do it in the existing mouse pipeline rather than HTML5 DnD: in `handleMouseDown`/`startMouseSelectionDrag` (:891-955), if the press offset falls inside an existing non-collapsed selection, don't start a new selection drag — arm a pending text-drag; on `mousemove` past a threshold, show the drop caret; on `mouseup` issue ONE `session.applyEdits([{from:srcStart,to:srcEnd,text:''},{from:target,to:target,text}], {selections:[...]})` (or omit the delete when Alt/Ctrl is held), reusing the batch-of-two-edits pattern already proven in `surroundSelection` (:325-355). Port `DragAndDropCommand.getEditOperations`' target-selection arithmetic — offsets make it much simpler than Monaco's line/column version.

### Diff-based input deduction as the robust fallback, instead of a keydown timing race

`partial` · value **high** · effort **M** · api-design · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco never guesses what a keystroke meant. It writes a known string+selection into the textarea, and on every `input` event diffs the new textarea state against the remembered one: `prefixLength = min(commonPrefixLength, prevSelStart, curSelStart)`, `suffixLength = min(commonSuffixLength, prevLen-prevSelEnd, curLen-curSelEnd)`, yielding `{text, replacePrevCharCnt, replaceNextCharCnt, positionDelta}`. Clamping the common prefix/suffix by the selection bounds is the subtle part — it prevents a repeated character adjacent to the caret from being absorbed into the prefix. The same primitive expresses ordinary typing, composition replacement, dead keys, autocorrect-style multi-character replacements and Android's word-level suggestion edits (which is why `deduceAndroidCompositionInput` exists as a variant that also computes `replaceNextCharCnt`). Guard rails: a lone high surrogate or 0x7f is dropped but deliberately left in the textarea so the next event completes it; a blur during composition synthesises a compositionend so the editor doesn't get stuck composing.

**Why it matters here.** Our current design instead races: on keydown it optimistically waits for a native `input` event and, if none arrives, replays the guessed character (`native-input-wait-started` -> `native-input-missing`). That produces double-insertion or dropped characters under exactly the conditions that are hardest to reproduce — autorepeat, dead keys, autocorrect, remote-desktop/synthetic key events, and any IME that doesn't fire compositionstart. A diff never double-counts because it compares against ground truth.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/textArea/textAreaEditContextState.ts:117-179 (TextAreaState.deduceInput — commonPrefix/commonSuffix clamped by the selection), :181-227 (deduceAndroidCompositionInput), :50-63 (readFromTextArea carrying newlineCountBeforeSelection forward); src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/textArea/textAreaEditContextInput.ts:322-364 (onInput: ignore lone high surrogates and 0x7f but keep them for next time; route insertFromPaste to onPaste), :221-262 (macOS long-press + Android compositionstart special cases), :445-462 (onBlur during composition fires an artificial compositionend)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/inputState.ts:156-164 `canWaitForNativeTextInput` (special-cases the space key!), :275-307 the fallback generation/timeout machinery; inputSelectionController.ts:1223-1241 `handleKeyDown` guesses from `event.key`, :1280-1318 `resolvePendingKeyboardTextFallback`/`applyKeyboardTextFallback` replay the guess. editor/input.ts:1-9 `keyboardFallbackText` is a 9-line heuristic (`event.key.length !== 1` -> null). The hidden textarea's value is always forced to `''` (:1307, :1330), so there is no previous state to diff against.

**Verifier.** Keep, and raise its value above the survey's framing. The win is not 'replace beforeinput' — beforeinput is fine and modern. The win is that a non-empty textarea with a known value+selection turns every one of the dropped inputTypes above into a uniform `{text, replacePrevCharCnt, replaceNextCharCnt}` triple derived by common-prefix/suffix diffing, with no per-inputType branch and no `event.key` guessing. That single change fixes autocorrect, dead keys, voice dictation and Android soft keyboards at once, and it is the same prerequisite as item 5 (both need the textarea to hold real content). Sequence 5 and 9 together — they are one refactor of the hidden-input contract, not two.

**How to implement.** Port `TextAreaState` verbatim into `packages/editor/src/editor/textAreaState.ts` — it is a pure, dependency-free class and lands directly in the existing unit-test style (see test/inputState.test.ts). Then stop clearing `inputElement.value`: keep a `previousTextAreaState`, listen to `input` on the hidden textarea (the capture listeners at inputSelectionController.ts:829-861 are already installed), and turn the deduced `{text, replacePrevCharCnt, replaceNextCharCnt}` into a `session.applyEdits` around the caret. The keydown-guess fallback and most of the `fallback-pending`/`native-input-*` half of the inputState machine can then be deleted, which is a net simplification. Bring across the lone-surrogate/0x7f guard and the blur-during-composition synthetic end.

### In-progress IME composition is invisible and the candidate window lands at the viewport corner

`missing` · value **high** · effort **L** · correctness · from **both**

**What it is.** Monaco's textArea path does NOT keep the textarea hidden during composition. On compositionstart it builds a VisibleTextAreaData (start line, distance to line start/end, width of hidden text before), reveals the range, sets class `ime-input`, and in _render() sizes and positions the textarea exactly over the composed span, scrolling the textarea internally (scrollLeft = widthOfHiddenLineTextBefore, scrollTop = lineCount*lineHeight) so only the composed text shows. It then copies the token's presentation (color/italic/bold/underline via definePresentation and TokenizationRegistry colour map) so the live composition is styled like the surrounding code. When the caret is off-screen it falls back to _renderAtTopLeft, and the textarea is 1x1 rather than 0x0 outside Firefox because a 0x0 textarea breaks Korean IME. On macOS (or accessibilitySupport=Enabled) the textarea is made a full lineHeight tall so the emoji/accent popup anchors correctly. The EditContext path solves the same problem differently: it never shows a textarea, but reports geometry to the OS — updateSelectionBounds/updateControlBounds from the primary selection rect, and per-character DOMRects on the `characterboundsupdate` event — and renders the IME's own underline styling by turning `textformatupdate` text formats into inline decorations (thin -> secondary, thick -> primary class).

**Why it matters here.** A CJK, Vietnamese or accent-key user currently types into a 1px fully-transparent textarea; they see nothing at all until compositionend, and the OS candidate list appears pinned at the top-left of the viewport instead of under the caret. This makes the editor effectively unusable for a large share of the world's developers, and it is invisible to anyone testing with a Latin keyboard.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/textArea/textAreaEditContext.ts:50-110 (VisibleTextAreaData, definePresentation), :396-425 (onCompositionStart makes the textarea visible), :703-795 (_render / _renderAtTopLeft), :112 (canUseZeroSizeTextarea); src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/native/nativeEditContext.ts:502-535 (_handleTextFormatUpdate), :547-575 (_updateSelectionAndControlBounds), :577-611 (_updateCharacterBounds); codemirror5/src/input/input.js:122-134 (hiddenTextarea kept near cursor)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/style.css:296-316 `.editor-virtualized-input` is `opacity:0; color:transparent; -webkit-text-fill-color:transparent; width:1px; height:1px`. packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:1901-1902 positions the input at `scrollTop`/`scrollLeft`, i.e. the viewport's top-left corner, never at the caret. packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:878-889 `handleCompositionUpdate` only stores the text in inputState; nothing is rendered, and the document is only touched at compositionend (applyCompositionText, :1320-1339). No EditContext usage anywhere (grep for `editContext` in packages/ returns nothing).

**Verifier.** Highest-value item in this domain. Today a CJK/Korean/Japanese/Vietnamese user sees nothing while composing and the OS candidate popup renders at the viewport's top-left, disconnected from the caret. Monaco's textarea path is verified at references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/textArea/textAreaEditContext.ts:50 (`class VisibleTextAreaData`), :396 (constructed on compositionstart), :411 (sets class `inputarea ... ime-input`) — it reveals the real textarea over the composed span rather than hiding it. Note the reference checkout also has a *native* EditContext path (src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/native/), which is the cheaper modern target for us: EditContext gives characterBoundsUpdate so we position the candidate window from our own geometry (offsetToX in virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts) without ever un-hiding the textarea. Effort: medium — geometry APIs and the row/chunk layer already exist; the work is a preedit decoration plus caret-rect-driven input placement.

**How to implement.** Two-stage. (1) Cheap fix first: in VirtualizedTextView.focusInput/positionInputInViewport, position `inputElement` at the primary caret's client rect (you already have `rangeClientRect(start,end)` in inputSelectionController.ts:791-803 and `offsetToX` in virtualizedTextViewGeometry.ts) instead of the viewport corner, and give it `height: lineHeight` on macOS. That alone fixes candidate-window placement. (2) Then make it visible during composition: on `compositionstart` add a class that drops opacity:0/transparent colours and sets the textarea's font/colour from the token under the caret (tokenIndex.ts/tokenProjection.ts already resolve token styles), size it to the composed span, and restore on `compositionend`. Longer term, add a `native-edit-context` strategy behind a capability check (`'EditContext' in window`) as a sibling of InputSelectionController's DOM listeners, feeding `textupdate` into the same `applyTypedText` path and `characterboundsupdate` from `virtualizedTextView.createRange`.

### Screen readers get an empty 1x1 textarea: no paged content window, no caret relationship

`missing` · value **high** · effort **L** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco keeps a hidden but non-empty element whose textContent is a *window* of the document around the primary selection: the page (accessibilityPageSize lines, default 10) containing the selection start, plus the selection, plus the page containing the selection end, with pretext/posttext each clamped to 500 characters (`LIMIT_CHARS`) because Chromium stalls on multi-thousand-character accessible text. The DOM selection inside that element is then set with `setBaseAndExtent` at the mapped offsets, and the element's `scrollTop` is nudged so the AT's notion of position matches. The flow is bidirectional: a `selectionchange` listener maps a caret the user moved with a Braille display or NVDA review cursor back to an editor Selection (`_getEditorSelectionFromDomRange`, including a CRLF offset correction). Self-inflicted selectionchange events are filtered by a timestamp guard: `_setIgnoreSelectionChangeTime` is stamped before every programmatic write and before cut/paste, and events within 100ms of that stamp — or within 5ms of the previous event — are dropped. `accessibilitySupport === Unknown` also switches on the aggressive text trimming.

**Why it matters here.** Right now the accessible name of the whole editor is the literal string 'Editor input' on an always-empty textarea. A screen-reader user gets no line content, no caret position, no selection feedback, and cannot use Braille review. The paged-window trick is also the only known way to do this without freezing on large files — a naive 'put the whole document in the textarea' implementation would hang on a 50k-line file.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/screenReaderUtils.ts:39-114 (SimplePagedScreenReaderStrategy.fromEditorSelection, LIMIT_CHARS=500, ellipsis U+2026 splicing), :116-136 (ariaLabelForScreenReaderContent); src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/native/screenReaderContentSimple.ts:40-73 (write textContent + setBaseAndExtent), :112-158 (selectionchange listener reading the caret back from the AT), :160-174 (trailing newline fix); src/vs/editor/common/config/editorOptions.ts:479-481 (accessibilityPageSize)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewHelpers.ts:161-169 `createInputElement` sets `aria-label: 'Editor input'` and nothing else; virtualizedTextView.ts:526-528 `focusInput` explicitly sets `inputElement.value = ''` and `setSelectionRange(0,0)`. inputSelectionController.ts:1307 and :1330 clear the value again on every keystroke and composition. No accessibilityPageSize-like option exists (grep for `accessibilit` across packages/ hits only diff/, panes/, find/ widget chrome).

**Verifier.** True and important: the editor is currently opaque to a screen reader — no text, no caret position, no context. Verified reference mechanism: references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/screenReaderUtils.ts:54 `const LIMIT_CHARS = 500` with pretext trimming at :63-64, posttext at :72-73, and a middle-ellipsis for oversized selections at :92-93; the page size lives at native/screenReaderContentRich.ts:33 `_accessibilityPageSize`. The 500-char clamp is the load-bearing trick (Chromium stalls computing accessible values on long strings), and pairing it with a paged window is what keeps arrow-key latency flat on huge files. Effort: high but bounded — we already have `readPieceTableTextRange` and resolved selections; the work is a per-selection-change content window plus dropping readOnly while focused. Item 6 is worthless without this one.

**How to implement.** New module `packages/editor/src/editor/screenReaderContent.ts` exporting a pure `pagedScreenReaderWindow(snapshot, resolvedSelection, linesPerPage, trim)` returning `{value, selectionStart, selectionEnd, startOffset}` — the piece table's `readPieceTableTextRange` + `offsetToPoint` make this straightforward and it is trivially unit-testable in the style of the existing inputState.test.ts. Wire it from `applySessionChange`/`syncDomSelection` to write `inputElement.value` and `setSelectionRange`, replacing the unconditional `value=''` clears (those clears exist to keep the fallback typing path clean, so gate the new content behind a `screenReaderMode` flag that starts off). Add the `ignoreSelectionChangeTime` stamp to the existing input-state machine (inputState.ts already has the phase/ownership vocabulary for this) and a `selectionchange`-on-input listener that maps back to offsets.

### Unicode ambiguous/invisible character highlighting

`missing` · value **high** · effort **L** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** The highlighter builds a candidate code point set (all invisible code points except space/tab/newline, plus all confusables for the enabled locales), compiles it to a single character-class regex, and scans line by line, extending each match to a full code point when it lands on a surrogate. The interesting part is the false-positive suppression: before flagging a character it looks at the whole word containing it (`getWordAtText` with DEFAULT_WORD_REGEXP); if that word contains no basic-ASCII characters *and* contains an obviously non-Latin non-confusable character, the character is left alone — that is what stops every Cyrillic or Greek identifier in a genuinely non-English file from lighting up, while still catching a lone Cyrillic 'а' inside `pаssword`. Results are capped at 1000 ranges with a `hasMore` flag, and the whole-document variant runs in the editor worker while the viewport-only variant runs inline.

**Why it matters here.** This is a supply-chain security feature: homoglyph and invisible-character attacks hide malicious identifiers in code that reads correctly. It also catches accidental non-breaking spaces and zero-width joiners pasted from docs, which produce baffling syntax errors. An editor without it silently renders the attack as legitimate code.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/common/services/unicodeTextModelHighlighter.ts:14-98 (computeUnicodeHighlights: candidate-set regex, surrogate-pair range extension, MAX_RESULT_LENGTH=1000), :183-241 (shouldHighlightNonBasicASCII word-context heuristic, isAllowedInvisibleCharacter), :99-126 (computeUnicodeHighlightReason -> confusableWith + notAmbiguousInLocales); src/vs/base/common/strings.ts:1243-1330 (AmbiguousCharacters: generated codepoint->ascii table per locale, intersected across allowed locales and merged with _common); src/vs/editor/contrib/unicodeHighlighter/browser/unicodeHighlighter.ts:39-215 (DocumentUnicodeHighlighter in the worker vs ViewportUnicodeHighlighter inline, banner + hover explaining the reason)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewHiddenCharacters.ts:112-116 `hiddenCharacterKind` recognises only `' '` and `'\t'` — the existing 'hidden characters' layer is a whitespace renderer, not a unicode analyser. grep for `2028|2029|confusable|ambiguous|invisible` across packages/editor/src returns nothing.

**Verifier.** Genuine gap with a security payload (trojan-source / homoglyph attacks in pasted code). Two things make it cheaper for us than it looks: (1) the marker/decoration plumbing already exists — virtualizedTextViewHiddenCharacters.ts computes per-offset markers with `offsetToX` geometry and a marker-key diff (:133-140), and packages/editor/src/editor/rangeDecorations.ts gives range decorations; the new work is the code-point classifier, not the paint path. (2) The false-positive suppression is the part worth copying verbatim: check the containing word's script before flagging, so Cyrillic prose is not flagged character-by-character. Merge item 8 into this as one 'suspicious characters' feature.

**How to implement.** The rendering hook already exists: `hiddenCharactersLayerElement` per row with absolutely-positioned markers computed from `offsetToX` (virtualizedTextViewHiddenCharacters.ts:89-110). Add `packages/editor/src/unicodeHighlight.ts` with a pure `computeUnicodeHighlights(text, options)` mirroring Monaco's scanner (port the `_common` + `_default` confusable table — it is a static JSON string, ~40KB, worth lazy-importing), then a plugin in the style of occurrenceHighlightPlugin.ts that computes ranges for the visible row range on each `notifyViewContributions('selection'|'edit')` and emits markers with a new `HiddenCharacterKind` ('ambiguous' | 'invisible'). Port the word-context heuristic — without it the feature is unusable on non-English codebases. For whole-document mode, packages/tree-sitter already proves the worker pattern.

### No aria-live announcement channel for editor actions

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **S** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Two subtleties make this non-obvious. First, screen readers ignore an aria-live region whose textContent is set to the same string twice in a row, so Monaco keeps *two* alert containers and *two* status containers and alternates between them, clearing the other. Second, several browser/AT combinations only announce a role=alert node after a visibility flip, so `insertMessage` sets `visibility:hidden` then `visible` immediately after writing. Messages are capped at 20000 characters because inserting a huge string into a live region freezes the browser. `alert()` is assertive (interrupts), `status()` is polite.

**Why it matters here.** Every non-visual state change in this editor — fold/unfold, 'added cursor above', 'selected N occurrences', rename applied, find wrapped, tab-focus toggled — is currently silent for a screen-reader user. This is the cheapest large accessibility win available, and the duplicate-message and visibility-flip workarounds are exactly the kind of thing a from-scratch implementation gets wrong and then can't debug.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/base/browser/ui/aria/aria.ts:16-42 (setARIAContainer builds two role=alert and two aria-live=polite containers), :44-75 (alert/status alternate between the pair), :77-87 (insertMessage: 20000-char cap, visibility hidden->visible toggle to force re-announcement); src/vs/editor/contrib/toggleTabFocusMode/browser/toggleTabFocusMode.ts:38-42 (alert on state change)

**Our code.** grep for `aria-live|role="alert"|role="status"` across all of packages/ returns zero hits. The only aria attributes in the editor package are the `aria-label` on the input (virtualizedTextViewHelpers.ts:168) and two `aria-hidden` layers (virtualizedTextViewRows.ts:174,177).

**Verifier.** Confirmed missing and cheap (a ~40-line module: two alert containers + two status containers, alternated so identical consecutive strings still fire, plus the visibility:hidden→visible flip that some AT combinations require before announcing role=alert). But its value is entirely derivative — announcing 'replaced 3 occurrences' is pointless while the document itself is unreadable (item 5). Sequence it after 5, or implement it alongside 11 whose toggle is the canonical first consumer.

**How to implement.** Add `packages/editor/src/editor/announce.ts` with a lazily-created container appended to `options.el.ownerDocument.body` (or the editor container so it disposes with the editor), exporting `announce(msg)` and `announceStatus(msg)` implementing the two-container alternation and visibility flip verbatim. Call it from the command handlers that already exist in inputSelectionController.ts: `applyInsertCursorCommand` (:498), `applySelectExactOccurrencesCommand` (:532), `applySelectAllCommand` (:470), plus fold/unfold in foldOperations.ts and the find widget.

### Backspace is code-point aware but not grapheme-cluster aware

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco walks backwards with a CodePointIterator: it first skips any trailing emoji modifiers (Fitzpatrick skin tones, U+FE0F variation selector, U+20E3 enclosing keycap), then requires the next code point back to be an emoji base, then optionally absorbs one preceding ZWJ so that a ZWJ sequence collapses one component per backspace rather than leaving orphaned joiners. If the sequence doesn't look like an emoji it falls back to a single code point. Note the deliberate scoping: it does not implement full UAX#29 grapheme clusters, only the emoji case, because that is where the visible breakage is.

**Why it matters here.** Backspacing over 👨‍👩‍👧 or 👍🏽 today leaves behind an orphaned zero-width joiner or a bare skin-tone modifier that renders as a stray box and silently corrupts the file. Users hit this whenever emoji appear in comments, commit-message editors, or markdown.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/base/common/strings.ts:1168-1183 (getLeftDeleteOffset), :1185-1219 (getOffsetBeforeLastEmojiComponent: skip skin-tone modifiers/variation selector/enclosing keycap, require an emoji base, then absorb one preceding ZWJ), :1221-1223 (isEmojiModifier 0x1F3FB-0x1F3FF); src/vs/editor/common/cursor/cursorDeleteOperations.ts:224-232 (the only caller — left delete position)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/documentSelectionEdits.ts:326-338 `previousCodePointOffset` handles surrogate pairs only (explicit 0xD800/0xDC00 range checks) and is the sole input to `backspaceRangeForSelection` (:340-350). No ZWJ (0x200D), variation-selector or skin-tone handling anywhere in packages/.

**Verifier.** Keep; effort is now clearly low because the hard part is already written and shipped. The fix is to expose a grapheme-boundary helper from the geometry module (or lift the segmenter into a shared util) and route `previousCodePointOffset` in documentSelectionEdits.ts:326 and textRanges.ts:43 through it. Monaco's hand-rolled emoji walk (skip Fitzpatrick/FE0F/20E3, require an emoji base, absorb one ZWJ) is worth reading for the *policy* — collapse one ZWJ component per backspace rather than the whole cluster — but we should implement it on Intl.Segmenter, which we already depend on, rather than porting the table walk. Note the current asymmetry is a live bug: geometry says a family emoji is one cell wide, backspace deletes half of it.

**How to implement.** Extend `previousCodePointOffset` in documentSelectionEdits.ts into `previousGraphemeOffset(snapshot, offset)`: read a short window backwards (`readPieceTableTextRange(snapshot, Math.max(0, offset-32), offset)`), run Monaco's `getLeftDeleteOffset` logic over that string, and map the result back to an absolute offset. Purely local change with no callers to update. Mirror it for forward delete if that path also steps by one code unit. Add cases to an existing edits test file covering 👍🏽, 👨‍👩‍👧 and a combining-accent sequence.

### Tab-focus mode: an accessibility escape hatch from the Tab key

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **S** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** A single global toggle, surfaced two ways: it becomes part of the computed editor options (so the editor recomputes on change) and it sets a context key, so the Tab/Shift+Tab keybindings simply stop matching and the browser's native focus traversal takes over. The toggle announces its new state through the aria alert channel, because the only users who need it cannot see the status bar indicator.

**Why it matters here.** A keyboard-only or screen-reader user who tabs into an editor is otherwise trapped: Tab inserts indentation and there is no way to reach the next control. WCAG 2.1.2 (No Keyboard Trap) makes this a conformance issue, not a nicety. It is also useful to sighted users filling in forms around an embedded editor.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/browser/config/tabFocus.ts:9-30 (global TabFocusImpl with an onDidChangeTabFocus event); src/vs/editor/contrib/toggleTabFocusMode/browser/toggleTabFocusMode.ts:13-44 (Ctrl+M / Ctrl+Shift+M on mac, and an aria alert stating the new behaviour); src/vs/editor/browser/config/editorConfiguration.ts:95,131 and src/vs/editor/browser/widget/codeEditor/codeEditorWidget.ts:2249,2262 (folds into the computed option and into an `editorTabMovesFocus` context key that keybinding `when` clauses consult)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/keymap.ts:429-430 binds Tab -> `indentSelection` and Shift+Tab -> `outdentSelection` unconditionally; there is no context/when mechanism and no toggle command (grep for `tabFocus` across packages/ returns nothing). The scroll element is `tabIndex = 0` (virtualizedTextViewHelpers.ts:155), so focus can enter but not leave via the keyboard.

**Verifier.** Keep — this is the cheapest item in the domain by a wide margin. Implementation is roughly: one editor option, plus `if (this.options.tabFocusMode) return false` at the top of applyIndentCommand (inputSelectionController.ts:416), which the existing shouldPreventDefault contract turns straight into native focus traversal. No context-key system needed; our handler-returns-boolean design already does what Monaco needs a `when` clause for. Reference for the shape: references/vscode/src/vs/editor/browser/config/tabFocus.ts and contrib/toggleTabFocusMode/browser/toggleTabFocusMode.ts. Pair the toggle with item 6 so the state change is actually announced.

**How to implement.** Add a `tabMovesFocus` boolean to `EditorSessionOptions`/runtime state plus a command `editor.action.toggleTabFocusMode` bound to Ctrl+M (Ctrl+Shift+M on mac) in keymap.ts. In `applyIndentCommand` (inputSelectionController.ts:416) return `false` when the flag is set so the keydown is not consumed and the browser moves focus; keymap.ts's dispatcher already distinguishes handled from unhandled commands. Announce the new state through the `announce()` helper from the aria-live finding. Also ensure Escape-then-Tab still works while a snippet session is active (:423 already gives snippets first claim on Tab).

### Copy with syntax highlighting, and the paste-provider (paste-as) pipeline

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **XL** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Two independent capabilities that share the same metadata channel. (a) Copy-with-syntax-highlighting renders the selected range's tokens to an inline-styled HTML div written as `text/html`, guarded by a 64KB size cap and skipped for plaintext or multi-selection; pasting into Word/Slack/Confluence then preserves colours. The font-family sanitisation (rejecting names containing `:;\/<>` and falling back to the default) is a CSS-injection guard worth copying. (b) Paste-as: on copy, providers registered for the language get to precompute a richer payload asynchronously; the clipboard carries only a uuid handle, and on paste the handle is matched against the still-running promises so the expensive work overlaps the user's think time. If the clipboard came from elsewhere the handle simply doesn't match and the providers fall back to whatever MIME types the DataTransfer actually has. Higher-priority providers overwrite lower ones by reversing the merge order.

**Why it matters here.** (a) is a small, self-contained win users notice immediately when pasting code into a doc or chat. (b) is the mechanism behind 'paste an image and get a markdown link', 'paste a URL over a selection and get a link', 'paste a TS symbol and get its import added' — the extension point that makes an editor feel language-aware on paste. There is already a markdown package and an LSP plugin here that would both want it.

**Reference.** vscode: src/vs/editor/common/viewModel/viewModelImpl.ts:1051-1107 (getRichTextToCopy: bail on plaintext/multi-selection, sanitise font-family for `:;\/<>` and quote it, emit an inline-styled div), src/vs/editor/browser/controller/editContext/clipboardUtils.ts:55-62 (only when sourceText.length < 65536); src/vs/editor/contrib/dropOrPasteInto/browser/copyPasteController.ts:176-230 (on copy: mint a uuid handle, stash it in the clipboard metadata, kick off every provider's prepareDocumentPaste as a cancelable promise), :561-594 (on paste: mergeInDataFromCopy matches the handle back to the in-flight operations and merges the resulting VSDataTransfer, highest-priority provider last), :232-320 (provider selection, pasteAs pick, post-paste widget)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:1214-1221 `handleCopy` writes only `text/plain`; :1163-1185 `handlePaste` reads only `text/plain` and ignores `clipboardData.types`, `files`, and `text/html` entirely. The plugin system (packages/editor/src/plugins.ts, viewContributions.ts) has no paste/copy hook — grep for `paste` in plugins.ts returns nothing.

**Verifier.** Keep, but split the value sharply. (a) Copy-as-HTML is a nice-to-have; moderate effort since we would render tokens from the syntax layer, and worth doing only behind the same 64KB cap and plaintext/multi-selection skips Monaco uses. (b) The paste-provider pipeline is the valuable half for this repo specifically: with packages/markdown in-tree, 'paste a URL over a selection → markdown link' and 'paste an image → placeholder + host upload' are exactly the behaviours users expect, and neither is expressible today because no extension point sees the ClipboardEvent. Recommend scoping this to (b) alone: add a `registerPasteHandler`-style contribution alongside the existing `registerEditContribution` (plugins.ts:1103-1110) so handlers can claim a paste by MIME, with our current text/plain path as the last-resort default. Depends on item 2's metadata channel for the internal-vs-external paste distinction.

**How to implement.** Split it. Ship (a) first: the token data needed is already materialised for rendering (editor/tokenProjection.ts + shiki theme colours in src/theme.ts), so `richTextForRange(snapshot, start, end)` producing an inline-styled `<div>` is maybe 60 lines; write it as `text/html` in `handleCopy` behind a `copyWithSyntaxHighlighting` option and the same 64KB cap. For (b), first widen `handlePaste`/`handleDrop` to expose the whole DataTransfer (types + files) rather than eagerly extracting `text/plain`, and add `onWillPaste`/`onWillCopy` hooks to the plugin lifecycle in plugins.ts — that alone lets packages/markdown and packages/lsp-plugin implement paste behaviours. The uuid-handle prefetch optimisation is only worth it once providers are actually async.
