## Folding, brackets & indentation

### Auto-unfold when the caret lands inside hidden lines, and selection clamping across them

`partial` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Three cooperating rules. (1) `revealCursor` (folding.ts:370-393), fired on a 200 ms `RunOnceScheduler` after cursor movement, checks `hiddenRangeModel.isHidden(line)` and, if so, collects `getAllRegionsAtLine(line, r => r.isCollapsed && line > r.startLineNumber)` and toggles them open, then reveals. So Go-to-line / find / go-to-definition into a folded body just works. (2) `HiddenRangeModel.adjustSelections` (96-125) clamps any selection endpoint that falls inside a hidden range to the end of the line *before* the range, so arrow keys and shift-selection cannot leave a caret in invisible text. (3) `FoldingModel._currentFoldedOrManualRanges` (171-173) refuses to re-collapse a region whose interior contains the current selection when provider ranges refresh — `selection?.startsInside(...)` sets `isCollapsed = false` — so a background syntax update never swallows the line you are editing. `updateHiddenRanges` also skips ranges nested inside an already-collapsed range (61-64), keeping the hidden-range list minimal and diffable (66-76 reuses old Range objects so the emitted event is identity-stable).

**Why it matters here.** Without (1), find-in-file or go-to-definition can move the caret into a collapsed region and the user sees nothing move. Without (2), the caret can sit invisible. Without (3), an async syntax refresh can collapse the block you are typing in. All three are the kind of bug that reads as 'the editor is broken' rather than 'a feature is missing'.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/folding/browser/folding.ts:247, 364-393 (cursorChangedScheduler + revealCursor); hiddenRangeModel.ts:44-125 (updateHiddenRanges, adjustSelections, isHidden); foldingModel.ts:162-186 (selection blocks re-collapse)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/Editor.ts — `foldState` is only touched by explicit fold/unfold/toggle/foldAll/unfoldAll (lines 620-661, 2587-2653); no cursor-movement path consults it. `grep -n 'foldMap|hidden' packages/editor/src/editor/selectionReveal.ts packages/editor/src/editor/navigation.ts` returns nothing. packages/editor/src/foldMap.ts has `bufferPointToFoldPoint` for geometry but no selection clamping and no unfold-on-reveal. `EditorFoldState.setFoldProjections` (foldState.ts:41-48) has no notion of the current selection.

**Verifier.** Downgraded to partial: the hidden-range coordinate mapping exists and is solid; what is missing is the *policy* layer on top of it. Two concrete behaviours to add, both small given foldMap: (1) after a programmatic reveal (go-to-definition, find-next, go-to-line — all of which already funnel through selectionReveal.ts), test the target offset against `foldMap.ranges` and unfold the enclosing collapsed ranges before revealing, otherwise the caret silently lands on a fold header with no feedback; (2) vertical navigation currently steps buffer rows, so ArrowDown into a collapsed body moves the caret through hidden text while it renders pinned to the header row — it should step *visible* rows via foldPointToBufferPoint. Do (2) even if (1) is deferred; the pinned-caret-that-does-not-move is the more confusing of the two.

**How to implement.** (1) In `Editor`, on the existing selection-change path that already calls into `selectionReveal.ts`, check whether the caret row is inside a collapsed `AnchorFoldRange` in the current `FoldMap` (`foldMap.ranges` already carries resolved start/end offsets) and, if so, call `this.unfold(fold)` for each enclosing collapsed fold before revealing. (2) Add `EditorFoldState.selectionBlocksFold(offset)` and consult it in `pruneCollapsedFolds`/`setFoldProjections` so a refresh cannot collapse over the caret — this pairs naturally with the anchor-keyed rewrite in the collapse-durability finding. (3) Clamp in `mouseSelection.ts`/`navigation.ts` via a `foldMap` lookup mirroring `HiddenRangeModel.adjustSelections`.

### Fold collapse state is keyed by content offsets, so any earlier edit silently expands folds

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco never identifies a folded region by its coordinates. `updatePost` writes one tracked model decoration per region (foldingModel.ts:146-157) whose range is `[startLine, maxCol] .. [endLine, maxCol+1]`. When new provider ranges arrive, `_currentFoldedOrManualRanges` (162-186) reads each decoration's *current* range back out of the text model — the decoration has already been moved by the edit — and `FoldingRegions.sanitizeAndMerge` (foldingRanges.ts:301-391) merges the fresh provider list (A) against that list of previously-collapsed/manual ranges (B) in one linear pass: equal start lines keep the provider's geometry but inherit B's collapsed flag *only if the line span is unchanged* (line 339), non-matching B entries survive as `FoldSource.recovered`, and a stack of `stackedRanges` discards anything that crosses rather than nests. Persistence across sessions uses a memento that stores a 6-digit hash of the region's first and last line content (241-245) and refuses to restore a region whose content hash changed.

**Why it matters here.** In our editor, collapsing a function at line 400 and then typing a single character at line 1 expands it. The fold's `startIndex`/`endIndex` shift by one, `foldRangeKey` produces a different string, and `pruneCollapsedFolds` drops the old key. This is the single most visible folding defect available to a user and it fires on ordinary typing.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/folding/browser/foldingModel.ts:132-186 (update / _currentFoldedOrManualRanges), 138-160 (updatePost delta-decorations), 191-245 (getMemento/applyMemento with line checksum); foldingRanges.ts:301-391 (sanitizeAndMerge)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/folds.ts:40-42 — `foldRangeKey` = `${languageId}:${type}:${startIndex}:${endIndex}` (byte offsets). packages/editor/src/editor/foldState.ts:135-141 `pruneCollapsedFolds` deletes any collapsed key not present in the new projection. packages/editor/src/editor/folds.ts:85-92 `projectSyntaxFoldsThroughLineEdit` returns null when `lineDelta === 0`, so a same-line insertion produces no key remap at all; the worker then reports shifted offsets and the collapse is lost. Note we already anchor the *geometry* correctly — packages/editor/src/foldMap.ts:19-63 stores `AnchorFoldRange` with piece-table anchors — so only the identity layer is wrong. No test covers collapse-survives-edit (packages/editor/test/ has foldMap.test.ts only).

**Verifier.** Highest-value item in this domain and the cheapest to fix: it is a user-visible correctness bug (type one character on line 1, every fold in the file pops open once the worker reparses), not a missing feature. The fix does not need Monaco's decoration machinery — foldMap.ts already proves we can key a fold by a pair of piece-table anchors. Move `collapsedFoldKeys` from an offset string to an anchor pair (or make EditorFoldState hold `AnchorFoldRange` identities and match incoming FoldRanges by resolved anchor offset), and delete the `lineDelta === 0` early return so offset-only shifts still remap. Add the missing regression test: collapse a fold, insert a char above it, deliver a reparse with shifted offsets, assert still collapsed.

**How to implement.** In `EditorFoldState` (foldState.ts) stop storing `Set<string>` of content keys. Store `{ start: Anchor, end: Anchor }` per collapsed fold using `anchorBefore`/`anchorAfter` from packages/textbuffer/src/anchors.ts — exactly what foldMap.ts already does at lines 19-28. On `setFoldProjections`, resolve each stored anchor against the current snapshot (`resolveAnchor`, dropping non-`live` ones) and match against incoming folds by resolved start offset, inheriting collapsed only when the span length is unchanged (Monaco's line-339 rule). This deletes `projectSyntaxFoldsThroughLineEdit`, `SyntaxFoldProjection.keyMap`, and `remapCollapsedFoldKeys` outright — a net simplification. Add a test: collapse a fold, insert a char at offset 0, assert `collapsedFoldCount` is unchanged.

### Two range providers (syntax + indentation fallback), with #region markers and off-side rule

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · feature · from **both**

**What it is.** `SyntaxRangeProvider.compute` awaits all folding-range providers; if *every* provider returns non-array (not just empty), it delegates to a `fallbackRangeProvider` — the `IndentRangeProvider` passed in at folding.ts:289. `computeRanges` (indentRangeProvider.ts:126-225) walks the document *bottom-up* keeping a stack of `{indent, endAbove, line}`; when the current line's indent drops below the stack top, every deeper entry pops and each pop emits a region `[line, previous.endAbove - 1]`. Two refinements matter: `offSide` (from language config `folding.offSide`, true for Python/YAML) attaches blank lines to the block *above* rather than terminating it (lines 150-158); and `folding.markers` (`#region`/`#endregion` regexes) are compiled into one alternation and handled with a sentinel indent of `-2` (lines 130-140, 178-203) so an explicit marker region overrides indentation entirely. Both collectors degrade gracefully past a limit by dropping the *deepest* levels first rather than truncating arbitrarily (indentRangeProvider.ts:82-107, syntaxRangeProvider.ts:140-165). `sanitizeRanges` (syntaxRangeProvider.ts:172-210) sorts by start then provider rank and uses a `previous` stack to compute each range's nesting level and discard crossings.

**Why it matters here.** Our folds exist only where a tree-sitter fold query matched. Open a .py, .yaml, .go, .rs, .sh, .css file or plain text and there is no folding at all, and even in TS there is no folding for a long `if` body or a `#region`. An indentation provider is ~100 lines and gives every language folding for free. The nesting level it computes is also the input for fold-by-level commands and for indent guides.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/folding/browser/syntaxRangeProvider.ts:48-59 (fallback), 172-210 (sanitizeRanges); indentRangeProvider.ts:126-225 (computeRanges), 66-111 (RangesCollector.toIndentRanges); references/codemirror5/addon/fold/indent-fold.js:14-46; references/codemirror5/addon/fold/comment-fold.js:14-57

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/syntax/session.ts:114 — folds come only from `EditorSyntaxResult.folds`; packages/tree-sitter-languages/src/queries/ has fold queries for javascript, typescript, jsx, markdown only. Editor.ts:1716-1758 registers exactly one fold projection owner, `editor.folds.syntax`. `grep -ri 'region|offSide|indentFold'` over packages/*/src returns nothing. Our FoldRange (session.ts:15-22) carries no nesting level.

**Verifier.** Real and worth doing: today a Python, Go, Rust, YAML, CSS or plain-text file gets zero folding, because folding is 100% grammar-gated on four queries. An indentation provider is ~120 lines and is the single change that makes folding universal. Effort is lower than the surveyor implies because the fan-in already exists — a second provider just needs its own owner string plus a public/plugin hook to register 'folds' projections (today only Editor.ts itself calls `displayProjections.set`, so the hook is the missing part, not the registry). `#region` markers are a cheap add-on to the same pass. Sequence it after the fold-identity fix (claim 2), otherwise indent folds inherit the same collapse-loss bug.

**How to implement.** Our `displayProjectionRegistry` already supports multiple owners for the `folds` kind (Editor.ts:1761 `foldProjections()` concatenates them), so this is additive: add `packages/editor/src/editor/indentFoldProvider.ts` implementing Monaco's bottom-up stack walk over a `LineMap` (the one in editActions.ts:497-506 already builds line starts), emitting `FoldRange` with `startIndex/endIndex/startLine/endLine`. Register it as owner `editor.folds.indent` and have `setSyntaxFolds` suppress it when the syntax projection is non-empty (Monaco's fallback rule). Also add a `nestingLevel` field to FoldRange while you are there — `rejectCrossingFoldRanges` (folds.ts:55-83) already maintains the `openAncestors` stack that computes it, so it is two lines.

### Language configuration as declarative data: onEnterRules and indentationRules

`partial` · value **high** · effort **M** · api-design · from **monaco**

**What it is.** One declarative record per language holds `comments`, `brackets`, `wordPattern`, `indentationRules {increaseIndentPattern, decreaseIndentPattern, indentNextLinePattern, unIndentedLinePattern}`, `onEnterRules [{beforeText, afterText, previousLineText, action}]`, `autoClosingPairs` (with `notIn`), `surroundingPairs`, `colorizedBracketPairs`, `autoCloseBefore`, and `folding {offSide, markers}`. `OnEnterSupport.onEnter` (onEnter.ts:52-107) evaluates in strict priority: user regex rules first, then `IndentAction.IndentOutdent` when the text before Enter matches an open-bracket regex *and* the text after matches a close-bracket regex, then plain `Indent` after a lone opener. `IndentOutdent` (languageConfiguration.ts:237) is the case that puts `}` on its own line and leaves the caret on a blank indented line. Separately, `getInheritIndentForLine` (autoIndent.ts:75-218) walks upward past lines matching `unIndentedLinePattern`, honours `indentNextLinePattern` for constructs like a bare `if (x)` whose indent is *temporary*, and returns the inherited indentation string. `IndentRulesSupport.getIndentMetadata` packs the four predicates into a 4-bit mask (indentRules.ts:8-13, 67-82) so callers branch on bits instead of re-running regexes.

**Why it matters here.** Our indentation is purely character-adjacency: `lineBreakIndent` looks at `charBefore`, `charAfter`, `betweenPair` and nothing else. That means Enter after `case 'a':`, after `if (x)` with no brace, inside a JSDoc block (` * ` continuation), after an unclosed JSX tag, or after a Python `def foo():` all produce the previous line's indentation verbatim. Declarative rules also move language knowledge out of three hardcoded `Record<string, ...>` tables into data an embedder can supply for a language we have never heard of.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/languages/languageConfiguration.ts:44-190 (LanguageConfiguration), 223-241 (IndentAction incl. IndentOutdent); supports/onEnter.ts:52-107; supports/indentRules.ts:8-83; autoIndent.ts:75-218 (getInheritIndentForLine), 304-358 (getIndentForEnter)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/indentation.ts:41-53 — `lineBreakIndent` has exactly three branches (betweenPair / afterOpener / continue), driven only by adjacent characters; there is no regex, no previous-line inspection, no language input at all. Language knowledge is hardcoded and split across three files: autoClose.ts:25-40 `PAIRS_BY_LANGUAGE`, editActions.ts:94-108 `COMMENT_TOKENS_BY_LANGUAGE`, and tree-sitter fold queries. `grep -riE 'onEnterRule|indentationRule|wordPattern|electricChar'` over packages/*/src returns nothing.

**Verifier.** Correctly partial. The value is not the abstraction for its own sake — it is that a single `LanguageConfiguration` record is the prerequisite that makes claims 8, 11 and 12 each a table entry instead of a new subsystem, and it kills the current failure mode where adding a language means editing three files and forgetting one. Concretely: today `PAIRS_BY_LANGUAGE` and `COMMENT_TOKENS_BY_LANGUAGE` list the same ~14 language ids independently, so any new language silently gets `[]` pairs (autoClose.ts:53-55 returns empty for unknown ids) and `//` comments (editActions.ts:1149 falls back to DEFAULT_COMMENT_TOKENS) — an inconsistent pair of defaults. Start by merging those two tables and adding `onEnterRules`; `indentationRules` can follow with claim 12.

**How to implement.** Add `packages/editor/src/editor/languageConfiguration.ts` exporting a `LanguageConfiguration` type and a registry keyed by languageId, seeded by merging today's `PAIRS_BY_LANGUAGE`, `COMMENT_TOKENS_BY_LANGUAGE`, and `AUTO_CLOSE_BEFORE`. Expose registration on the public API (packages/editor/src/public/) so embedders can add languages. Then extend `LineBreakIndentOptions` with `previousLineText`, `lineTextAfterCaret`, and the resolved config, and reimplement `lineBreakIndent` as Monaco's three-tier `onEnter`, returning an `{indentAction, appendText, removeText}` that the caller turns into `{insert, trailing}` — our existing `trailing` field is already exactly `IndentAction.IndentOutdent`. packages/editor/src/editor/indentation.test.ts gives a regression net.

### Auto-close gated on token type, with the 'neutral character' tokenizer probe for quotes

`partial` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Each `autoClosingPairs` entry declares `notIn: ['string','comment','regex']`. Before inserting a closer, `_getAutoClosingPairClose` resolves the *scoped* line tokens at the caret (handling embedded languages) and calls `pair.shouldAutoClose(scopedTokens, column)`, which maps the standard token type through `isOK` (languageConfiguration.ts:324-336). For quotes this is not enough, because the token type *at* the caret cannot distinguish 'this quote opens a string' from 'this quote closes the string I am in'. Monaco's answer is a probe: `findNeutralCharacter()` (348-372) picks any character in [0-9a-zA-Z] not appearing in the pair's open/close text, then `getTokenTypeIfInsertingCharacter(line, col, neutral)` asks the tokenizer what token type would result if that harmless character were typed there (cursorTypeEditOperations.ts:273-278). If the answer is String, we are inside a string and the quote should not auto-close. Two further rules: `autoCloseBefore` is resolved per *category* — quote / comment / bracket get different allowed-following-character sets (201-214) — and `_isBeforeClosingBrace` (345) permits auto-close directly before a closing brace even though a brace is not in the generic before-set.

**Why it matters here.** Today, typing `(` inside a `// comment` or inside a string literal inserts a stray `)`, and typing `"` to close a string you are in inserts a second `"`. Both are daily-friction bugs in exactly the situations where auto-close is most annoying. We already receive per-offset syntax captures that name `string`/`comment`, so the token gate is cheap; only the neutral-character probe needs new plumbing.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/languages/languageConfiguration.ts:297-320 (notIn parsing), 324-345 (isOK/shouldAutoClose), 348-372 (findNeutralCharacter); cursor/cursorTypeEditOperations.ts:240-280 (shouldAutoCloseBefore, scoped token check, neutral-character probe), 345 (_isBeforeClosingBrace)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/autoClose.ts:88-96 — `shouldAutoClose` consults only `charAfter`, `charBefore` and a word-character regex; there is no token, language-scope, or `notIn` input anywhere in the file. `AUTO_CLOSE_BEFORE` (line 48) is one global set, not per-category. packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:258-271 calls it with no syntax context. We do have the raw material: packages/editor/src/syntax/session.ts:8-13 `EditorSyntaxCapture {startIndex, endIndex, captureName, languageId}` and :117 `injections`.

**Verifier.** Correctly partial. The concrete user-visible bug today: typing an apostrophe inside a string or a comment inserts a second quote, because nothing consults the parse. Fix is small — thread the capture at the caret into AutoCloseContext and add `notIn: ['string','comment']` to the pair table from claim 5. Monaco's 'neutral character' probe (retokenize the line with the quote replaced by a neutral char, so the tokenizer's own state does not poison the answer) is a workaround for its line-based incremental tokenizer and does NOT transfer: tree-sitter re-parses the real text and gives us the node type directly, so we can skip that trick entirely. Note `AUTO_CLOSE_BEFORE` being one global set rather than per-category is a separate, smaller wart worth fixing in the same pass (quotes and brackets want different before-sets).

**How to implement.** Extend `AutoCloseContext` with `tokenTypeAtCaret: 'string' | 'comment' | 'regex' | 'other'` and `AutoClosingPair` with `notIn?: readonly string[]`, then add the `isOK` gate to `shouldAutoClose`. Resolve the token type by binary-searching the current `EditorSyntaxResult.captures` for the caret offset (`captureName` prefixed `string`/`comment`) — `getEditorTokenIndex` in editor/tokenIndex.ts already does monotonic-range lookups over a similar array. The neutral-character probe needs a `tokenTypeIfInserting(offset, char)` on the tree-sitter session; if that is too invasive, approximate it by checking whether the caret sits strictly inside a `string` capture (start < offset < end) versus at its boundary — that covers the common `"|"` case. Existing coverage: packages/editor/src/editor/autoClose.test.ts, packages/editor/test/autoClose.test.ts.

### Linked editing: mirrored ranges kept in sync with minimal prefix/suffix-trimmed edits

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · feature · from **both**

**What it is.** A provider returns a set of ranges that must stay identical (an HTML tag's open and close name). Each becomes a decoration with `TrackedRangeStickiness.AlwaysGrowsWhenTypingAtEdges` so typing at either boundary extends the range instead of escaping it. Two schedulers are separated on purpose (147-155): a slow `Delayer` re-asks the provider where the ranges *are*, and a fast one re-syncs their *contents*. `_syncRanges` (179-249) reads range 0 as the reference, then — this is the part that matters — for each mirror it strips the common prefix and common suffix between old and new text and emits an edit covering only the differing middle (218-236). That keeps undo granular, keeps other decorations and the caret stable inside the mirrors, and produces no edit at all when nothing changed. A guard breaks the link when the reference no longer matches `wordPattern` (193-200) — so typing `>` or a space in a tag name dissolves the linkage rather than corrupting the closing tag. Edits are wrapped in `popUndoStop` + preserved `prevEditOperationType` (242-247) so the sync merges into the user's own undo step. CM5 reaches the same UX from the other direction: `findMatchingTag` (xml-fold.js:151-178) scans with a token iterator and `matchtags.js` merely highlights, re-running on viewport change when the match failed because the partner was off-screen.

**Why it matters here.** Renaming a JSX/HTML tag and having the closing tag follow is one of the highest-perceived-value features in a web editor, and the same machinery gives 'highlight matching tag'. Our editor already has every prerequisite — anchors, range decorations, a snippet session that drives multiple synchronized regions — so this is assembly rather than invention.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/linkedEditing/browser/linkedEditing.ts:47-51 (AlwaysGrowsWhenTypingAtEdges decoration), 147-176 (two-stage debounce), 179-249 (_syncRanges), 276-365 (updateRanges + wordPattern capture); references/codemirror5/addon/edit/matchtags.js:34-52; references/codemirror5/addon/fold/xml-fold.js:91-182 (findMatchingOpen/Close)

**Our code.** `grep -riE 'linkedEditing|renameTag|matchTag'` over packages/*/src returns nothing. We have the parts: packages/editor/src/editor/rangeDecorations.ts, packages/editor/src/editor/occurrences.ts (document highlights, commit c23c2f9), packages/editor/src/editor/snippetSession.ts (multi-region tab stops), packages/textbuffer/src/anchors.ts, and packages/lsp (rename landed in 0b91f75). packages/tree-sitter-languages/src/queries/jsx-folds.scm shows we already parse JSX.

**Verifier.** Genuinely missing, moderate value, moderate effort — rank it below claims 2, 7, 11. The mechanism worth copying is specifically the two-scheduler split (a slow delayer re-asking *where* the mirrored ranges are, a fast one re-syncing *what* they contain): it is what stops a stale provider answer from corrupting text mid-keystroke, and it is not obvious. Our anchors give us stickiness for free — `anchorBefore`/`anchorAfter` is the direct analogue of TrackedRangeStickiness.AlwaysGrowsWhenTypingAtEdges, and foldMap.ts already demonstrates the growth-at-boundaries semantics. Cheapest first version: a tree-sitter query returning the two tag-name nodes of a JSX element, mirrored through a snippetSession-style multi-region edit. Skip the prefix/suffix trimming until it demonstrably matters.

**How to implement.** Add `packages/editor/src/editor/linkedEditing.ts`. Range source: either an LSP `textDocument/linkedEditingRange` request through packages/lsp, or purely local — walk the tree-sitter tree for the JSX/HTML element containing the caret and take its opening and closing name nodes (CM5's findMatchingTag is the fallback shape if no tree is available). Hold the ranges as piece-table anchors with right/left bias matching `AlwaysGrowsWhenTypingAtEdges`. On each committed edit inside range 0, run Monaco's prefix/suffix trim and apply the minimal mirror edits through the existing `editChain.ts` so they merge into one undo unit. Break the link when the reference text stops matching the language's `wordPattern` (which the language-configuration finding introduces). `snippetSession.test.ts` is a good template for the test shape.

### Bracket-pair guides and colorization derived from the AST, including min-indentation per pair

`partial` · value **high** · effort **L** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** `collectBrackets` walks the AST for a viewport range only, carrying two counters: `level` (absolute nesting) and `nestingLevelOfEqualBracketType` tracked in a `Map<bracketText, number>` so `(` and `[` colour independently. It emits `BracketInfo(range, level-1, typeLevel-1, parentPairIsIncomplete)` — the last flag lets an unclosed pair be rendered differently. Two structural optimisations: when a child extends past the requested end there is no sibling after it, so the loop *reassigns `node` and continues the outer `whileLoop`* instead of recursing (lines 280-290, 322-334), keeping stack depth at the AST depth rather than the child count; and `level > 200` bails (line 260). `collectBracketPairs` additionally computes `minIndentation` per pair by asking every contained `TextAstNode` for the minimum visible column of its lines (ast.ts:611-635), memoised in the node. That number is exactly where a bracket-scope guide should be drawn. The tokenizer deliberately caps text-node length (`lengthHeuristic > 1000` at end of line, `> 1500` hard, tokenizer.ts:255-268) so min-indentation recomputation after an edit stays bounded.

**Why it matters here.** Bracket-pair colorization is the single most-requested modern editor affordance and is impossible on our flat bracket list because nothing carries a nesting level. Separately, our scope guides are derived from fold markers, so a guide only exists where a tree-sitter fold query matched — a `for` body, an `if` block, or an object literal argument gets no guide at all, and no guide is drawn in a language without fold queries.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/common/model/bracketPairsTextModelPart/bracketPairsTree/bracketPairsTree.ts:247-374 (collectBrackets), 385-491 (collectBracketPairs); ast.ts:611-635 (TextAstNode.computeMinIndentation); tokenizer.ts:186-270 (lengthHeuristic); colorizedBracketPairsDecorationProvider.ts

**Our code.** packages/scope-lines/src/index.ts — `ScopeGuide` is built from `VirtualizedFoldMarker`, i.e. from fold ranges, with an `indentLevel` inferred rather than measured. packages/editor/src/syntax/session.ts:115 `BracketInfo` has `index`, `char`, `depth`; bracketMatching.ts:76-79 explicitly documents that the worker's `depth` is unreliable on unbalanced text and refuses to use it. packages/editor/src/bracketMatchPlugin.ts decorates one pair only. No colorization CSS or per-level class in packages/editor/src/style.css.

**Verifier.** Keep, but scope it to what is actually absent. Two separable pieces: (a) bracket-pair *colorization* of the bracket glyphs by nesting level — this is blocked on reliable per-bracket levels, i.e. it is the real payoff of the reduced version of claim 1, and once levels are trustworthy it is a `data-bracket-level` attribute plus six CSS vars, cloned straight from scope-lines/style.css which already defines that palette; (b) Monaco's `min-indentation over the whole pair` refinement — ours takes the *first* non-blank body line's indent (index.ts:386-397), which draws the guide at the wrong column when the first body line is a continuation or is indented deeper than the rest of the block. (b) is a ten-line change to that probe loop and is worth doing regardless of (a).

**How to implement.** Gated on the bracket AST finding. Once a tree exists, add `collectBracketsInRange(startOffset, endOffset)` returning `{range, level, typeLevel, incomplete}` and have the existing rendering path in packages/editor/src/virtualization/ attach a `bracket-level-{n % 6}` class — the highlight layer already paints ranges. For guides, port `computeMinIndentation` onto Text nodes and switch packages/scope-lines to consume bracket pairs when available, falling back to fold markers. Keep Monaco's `level > 200` and text-node length caps; they are what makes it safe on generated files.

### Comment tokens resolved at the caret's embedded language, plus insertion-point column normalization

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Every comment operation resolves the comment config from `model.getLanguageIdAtPosition(startLineNumber, 1)` after `tokenizeIfCheap`, not from the document's language — so `//` inside a `<script>` in HTML and `/* */` inside `<style>` are both correct, and a JS block inside Markdown comments as JS. `_analyzeLines` (115-190) then decides add-vs-remove with three careful rules: a line that is whitespace-only is ignored rather than counted as 'already commented'; if the whole selection is whitespace-only the decision flips to *add* and the ignore flags are cleared (175-185); and a trailing space after the token is removed on uncomment only when `insertSpace` is set (166-173). Finally `_normalizeInsertionPoint` (438-480) computes the minimum *visible* column across all target lines (tab-aware, via `nextVisibleColumn`), floors it to a multiple of the indent size, and inserts every `//` at that same visible column — so commenting a block of mixed-indent lines produces aligned markers rather than a ragged left edge.

**Why it matters here.** Two concrete defects for us: (a) commenting a JS region inside an HTML or Markdown file inserts `<!--` because our lookup is on the document language; (b) commenting a nested block inserts each `//` at that line's own first-non-whitespace column, so the markers stagger with the code instead of aligning — and uncommenting a block where one line was blank behaves inconsistently.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/comment/browser/lineCommentCommand.ts:87-89, 197, 291 (getLanguageIdAtPosition), 115-190 (_analyzeLines), 438-480 (_normalizeInsertionPoint); blockCommentCommand.ts:174

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/editActions.ts:442, 1145-1150 — `commentTokensForLanguage(options.languageId)` where `languageId` is threaded from `Editor.ts:1557 languageId: this.languageId`, the whole-document language. editActions.ts:566-578 `lineCommentEdits` inserts at `firstNonWhitespaceOffset(map, row)` per row, with no common-column normalization. `shouldUncommentLineComments` (581-588) does correctly filter blank lines but has no all-blank flip. We already carry `EditorSyntaxResult.injections` (syntax/session.ts:37-42, 117) giving `{parentLanguageId, languageId, startIndex, endIndex}`.

**Verifier.** Keep. Directly relevant to us rather than theoretical: we ship markdown injections (packages/tree-sitter-languages/src/queries/markdown-injections.scm) and markdown is mapped to HTML comment tokens (editActions.ts:572), so commenting a line inside a fenced ```ts block in a markdown file inserts `<!-- -->` today. Resolving tokens from the innermost injection covering the selection start is a lookup over an array we already have. Bundle three fixes in one pass: injection-scoped token lookup, common-column insertion (min firstNonWhitespace over the non-blank rows), and the blank-line skip on add.

**How to implement.** Change `EditorEditActionOptions.languageId` to `languageIdAt: (offset: number) => string | null`, implemented in Editor.ts by binary-searching the current result's `injections` for the offset and falling back to `this.languageId`. Call it with the selection's start offset in `commentLineAction`/`blockCommentAction` (editActions.ts:437-460). Port `_normalizeInsertionPoint` as a helper over the existing `LineMap`: compute min visible column with a tab-aware scan, floor to `tabSize`, and emit each insertion at that column. Tests exist at packages/editor/test/editor-helpers.test.ts and editor.test.ts.

### Continue-list on Enter, including empty-item termination and renumbering of following items

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **S** · feature · from **codemirror5**

**What it is.** One regex triple does the work: `listRE = /^(\s*)(>[> ]*|[*+-] \[[x ]\]\s|[*+-]\s|(\d+)([.)]))(\s*)/` captures indent, bullet, number, delimiter and trailing space; `emptyListRE` recognises a bullet with nothing after it. On Enter: if the line is an empty list item, the bullet is *deleted* and a bare newline inserted (43-51) — pressing Enter twice ends the list, which is the behaviour users expect and the reason a naive implementation feels broken. Otherwise the bullet is reproduced, with an ordered number incremented and a checkbox `[x]` reset to `[ ]` (line 55). It also declines to continue when the caret is before the bullet (`cursorBeforeBullet`, 38-39) or the selection is non-empty. `incrementRemainingMarkdownListNumbers` (67-100) then walks forward renumbering subsequent items at the same indent, skipping deeper-indented sublists and stopping when indentation decreases — so inserting an item in the middle of a numbered list renumbers the rest.

**Why it matters here.** We ship a Markdown package with inline replacements and a WYSIWYG-style view, which makes list editing a core interaction rather than a nicety. Today Enter inside `- item` yields a bare indented line and the user retypes the bullet every time.

**Reference.** references/codemirror5/addon/edit/continuelist.js:14-63 (newlineAndIndentContinueMarkdownList), 67-100 (incrementRemainingMarkdownListNumbers)

**Our code.** `grep -riE 'continueList|listRE|bullet'` over packages/*/src returns nothing. packages/editor/src/editor/indentation.ts:41-53 `lineBreakIndent` copies leading whitespace only. packages/markdown/src/replacements.ts exists (and is modified in the working tree) but handles inline replacement rendering, not Enter behaviour. autoClose.ts:18-23 already distinguishes prose languages via `PROSE_PAIRS`, so the language hook point exists.

**Verifier.** Best value-to-effort ratio of the twelve, and the one most specific to this project: markdown here is a live editing view over the same piece-table buffer, so Enter inside a list is a core interaction, not a nicety. Two mechanisms are worth taking verbatim from the CM5 addon and are the parts people get wrong when reimplementing: (1) Enter on an *empty* list item deletes the bullet and inserts a bare newline, so pressing Enter twice ends the list — without this the feature is actively annoying; (2) blockquote `>` and task-list `[x]`/`[ ]` markers are handled by the same regex as bullets, so they continue too. Renumbering following ordered items is optional and can land later. The `[-*+]` recognizer already exists in replacements.ts:38 and should be shared rather than duplicated. Do not gate this on the full LanguageConfiguration record — one markdown-scoped onEnter rule is enough to ship it.

**How to implement.** Implement as an `onEnterRules`-shaped rule for markdown once the language-configuration finding lands, or standalone first: in `inputSelectionController.insertLineBreak` (inputSelectionController.ts:191-215), before calling `lineBreakIndent`, match the current line against `listRE`; on `emptyListRE` emit a delete-of-bullet plus newline, else emit `\n + indent + nextBullet`. Renumbering is a second, optional edit batch over following lines — our `applyEdits` already takes an array. Keep CM5's guards (collapsed caret only, caret not before the bullet) or the feature will fight multi-cursor and mid-bullet edits.

### Fold by level, fold recursively, fold all regions, manual folds from selection

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **M** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** `FoldingRegions` stores regions in two `Uint32Array`s and packs the *parent index* into the high 16 bits, split across the two arrays (`_startIndexes[i] |= (parent & 0xFF) << 24`, `_endIndexes[i] |= (parent & 0xFF00) << 16`, foldingRanges.ts:105-106, read back at 194-201). Computing parents is one linear pass with a stack (86-109) and is done lazily on first use. `findRange(line)` is a binary search plus a walk up the parent chain (207-239). On top of that, one filter-driven primitive `getRegionsInside(region, filter)` — which maintains a `levelStack` to supply the nesting level to a two-arg filter (284-299) — expresses every command: fold level N is `level === foldLevel && !containsBlockedLine`; fold recursively is `levels = MAX_VALUE`; fold-all-block-comments is `setCollapseStateForType('comment')`; fold-all-except is `setCollapseStateForRest`. `toggleCollapseState` (54-96) batches all toggles into a single `changeDecorations` transaction and updates only decorations whose end line falls inside the dirty span. Manual folds are first-class: `FoldRangeFromSelectionAction` (folding.ts:1152-1186) creates a `FoldSource.userDefined` range from the selection and merges it via `sanitizeAndMerge`, where user-defined entries win over provider entries (foldingRanges.ts:330-341).

**Why it matters here.** Fold-level-1..7 and fold-recursively are how people actually navigate a large unfamiliar file. Manual folds are how people collapse things the grammar has no node for (a config block, a data table, a licence header). We expose only fold / unfold / toggle / foldAll / unfoldAll, and foldAll collapses *every* region including nested ones, which is not what any editor's Fold All does.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/folding/browser/foldingModel.ts:279-313 (getRegionsInside with level stack), 326-463 (toggleCollapseState, setCollapseStateLevelsDown/Up, setCollapseStateAtLevel, setCollapseStateForRest, ForMatchingLines, ForType), 472-603 (getParentFoldLine/Previous/Next); foldingRanges.ts:86-109 (parent index packing), 207-239 (findIndex/findRange); folding.ts:1034-1045 (FoldLevelAction), 1137-1186 (FoldRangeFromSelectionAction)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/Editor.ts:625-661 — the complete fold API is toggleFold / fold / unfold / foldAll / unfoldAll. `EditorFoldState.foldAll` (foldState.ts:91-98) sets every fold's key, with no level filter. `FoldRange` (syntax/session.ts:15-22) has no parent or level field and no `source`. packages/editor/src/editor/commands.ts contains no fold command ids at all. packages/editor/src/editor/foldOperations.ts:5-23 picks a single candidate at a location; there is no 'regions inside' query.

**Verifier.** Keep — best value-per-line in the domain, and note the correction: the missing keybindings are a bigger practical gap than the missing level operations. Ctrl+Shift+[ / ] and Ctrl+K Ctrl+0..9 are muscle memory; today no key folds anything. Nesting level does not need Monaco's Uint32Array packing: our fold list is already sorted start-asc/end-desc (`sortedFoldRangesForIngestion`, packages/editor/src/editor/folds.ts:104-117) and guaranteed non-crossing (`rejectCrossingFoldRanges`, folds.ts:55-83), so parent/level fall out of one containment-stack pass over the existing array — the same stack `innermostOpenAncestor` (folds.ts:143-151) already maintains. Manual folds want the plugin-facing 'folds' projection hook from claim 3, so the two share a prerequisite.

**How to implement.** Add a `parentIndex` array computed once per projection in `EditorFoldState` using the same stack `rejectCrossingFoldRanges` (folds.ts:143-151) already walks — no bit packing needed at our scale, a plain `Int32Array` is fine. Then add one primitive `regionsInside(fold | null, filter: (fold, level) => boolean)` and express `foldLevel(n)`, `foldRecursively`, `unfoldRecursively`, `foldAllExcept` on top, mirroring foldingModel.ts:326-429. Register the command ids in commands.ts/keymap.ts next to the existing `editor.action.*` list. Manual folds need a `source` field on FoldRange plus a second projection owner `editor.folds.manual` — the registry already supports it.

### Reindent lines / reindent selection driven by indentation rules

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **M** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** `getReindentEditOperations` recomputes each line's indentation from `getGoodIndentForLine`, which combines the inherited indent from above with the current line's `decreaseIndentPattern`, and emits one replace-edit per line whose indentation actually changes. The correctness detail worth stealing is `IndentationLineProcessor.getProcessedTokens` (indentationLineProcessor.ts:198-225): before any indentation regex runs, it walks the line's tokens and *deletes the language's bracket characters from String, RegEx and Comment tokens*, then rebuilds the line. So `const s = "}"` does not trigger `decreaseIndentPattern`, and a `{` inside a comment does not increase indent. `ProcessedIndentRulesSupport` (22-67) wraps every predicate behind that filter, so no caller can forget it. `isLanguageDifferentFromLineStart` (227-235) additionally detects when the caret's scoped language differs from the line's starting language, which is how embedded-language indentation avoids using the host language's rules.

**Why it matters here.** Reindent-selection is the standard cleanup gesture after a paste, and paste-with-correct-indentation depends on the same primitive. Beyond the command itself, the token-masking idea is a correctness fix that applies to any indentation heuristic we ever write, including the auto-indent-on-Enter improvements: without it, brackets inside strings and comments corrupt the answer.

**Reference.** references/vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/indentation/browser/indentation.ts:209-235 (DetectIndentation), 237-264 (ReindentLinesAction), 266-320 (ReindentSelectedLinesAction); common/indentation.ts getReindentEditOperations; common/languages/autoIndent.ts:219-302 (getGoodIndentForLine); supports/indentationLineProcessor.ts:172-225 (getProcessedTokens)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/commands.ts:44-45 has only `editor.action.indentLines` / `outdentLines`, which shift by a fixed unit (editActions.ts:462-476) with no rule awareness. packages/editor/src/editor/indentation.ts:67-71 `indentUnit` infers tab-vs-spaces from the current line's leading whitespace, per line, with no document-wide detection. `grep -riE 'reindent|detectIndentation'` over packages/*/src returns nothing.

**Verifier.** Keep, but rank it last and flag the dependency: `getReindentEditOperations` is meaningless without `indentationRules` (increase/decrease patterns), so this cannot ship before claim 5 — implemented alone it would be a no-op or a guess. Two things here are worth doing independently and sooner, and both are cheap: (a) content-based indent detection at load, replacing the per-line guess in indentation.ts:67-71, which today makes a mixed-indentation file drift line by line; (b) Monaco's `IndentationLineProcessor.getProcessedTokens` idea — strip string and comment content before matching any indent pattern — which we can do for free from the captures we already retain (Editor.ts:591-592), and which is the difference between a reindent that is safe on a file containing `// }` and one that is not. If effort is constrained, take (a) and drop the rest.

**How to implement.** Depends on `indentationRules` from the language-configuration finding. Add `editor.action.reindentlines` / `editor.action.reindentselectedlines` to commands.ts and implement in editActions.ts over the existing `LineMap`: for each row compute the target indentation from the previous non-ignored row plus the current row's decrease pattern, and emit a replace edit for the leading-whitespace range only when it differs. Implement the token mask by intersecting each row with the `EditorSyntaxCapture[]` for `string`/`comment` captures and blanking bracket characters inside them before matching — the same capture lookup the auto-close finding needs. Also worth adding `detectIndentation` over the first N lines to replace the per-line inference in indentation.ts:67-71.
