## Find & replace

### Case-insensitive plain search folds the whole haystack, which both wastes memory and corrupts offsets

`missing` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **both**

**What it is.** Monaco refuses to take the fast indexOf path whenever folding could matter: `let canUseSimpleSearch = (!this.isRegex && !multiline); if (canUseSimpleSearch && this.searchString.toLowerCase() !== this.searchString.toUpperCase()) { canUseSimpleSearch = this.matchCase; }` — i.e. a case-insensitive query containing any cased character falls back to the `i`-flagged regex, which reports indices in the *original* string. CM5 instead does fold the text (`str.normalize("NFD").toLowerCase()`) but then maps every folded index back through `adjustPos(orig, folded, pos, foldFunc)`, a binary search that re-folds prefixes of the original until the folded length equals the target index — with the explicit comment 'compensating for codepoints increasing in number during folding'.

**Why it matters here.** Case folding is not length-preserving. Verified against our exact code path: in the document `aİstanbul stanbul`, `text.toLocaleLowerCase()` turns U+0130 into two code units, so a case-insensitive search for `stanbul` reports index 3, where the original text reads `tanbul ` — the highlight is off by one and Replace One writes the replacement over the wrong characters, silently corrupting the user's file. Separately, the fold allocates a full copy of the document per search *range* per research pass.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/common/model/textModelSearch.ts:59-63 (canUseSimpleSearch guard); codemirror5/addon/search/searchcursor.js:125-146 (doFold/noFold + adjustPos), :148-174 (searchStringForward)

**Our code.** packages/find/src/search.ts:138 `const haystack = query.regex.ignoreCase ? text.toLocaleLowerCase() : text` — computed inside `appendSimpleMatches`, i.e. once per search range; lines 141-146 then use the folded index against the unfolded `text` for both the whole-word check and the emitted `{start, end}`. `compileFindQuery` (search.ts:94-95) sets `simpleSearch` for any non-regex query regardless of matchCase, so the folded path is the default for case-insensitive find.

**Verifier.** Cheapest high-value fix in the list: three lines in `compileFindQuery` — refuse `simpleSearch` when `!matchCase && searchString.toLowerCase() !== searchString.toUpperCase()`, letting the already-correct `i`-flagged regex path (search.ts:150) handle it. That also deletes the full-document `toLocaleLowerCase()` allocation. This is a data-corrupting bug, not a perf nit; it should be ranked above claim 1.

**How to implement.** Cheapest correct fix is Monaco's: in `compileFindQuery`, set `simpleSearch` to null when `!query.matchCase && searchString.toLowerCase() !== searchString.toUpperCase()`, letting the existing `gimu` regex path handle it — that also deletes the whole-document fold allocation. Add a regression test with `İ`/`ẞ` in the haystack asserting the returned range slices back to the query. If we later want the indexOf speed for case-insensitive ASCII, gate it on the query and the scanned window both being ASCII-only rather than reintroducing folding.

### Replace All silently stops at 19,999 matches; Monaco has a dedicated large-replace path

`missing` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco keeps two distinct limits. `MATCHES_LIMIT = 19999` bounds only the *decorations* used for highlighting and position display. Every mutating operation re-queries with `Constants.MAX_SAFE_SMALL_INTEGER` as the limit: `_regularReplaceAll` and `selectAllMatches` both pass it explicitly with the comment 'Get all the ranges (even more than the highlighted ones)'. And when the whole file is in scope and the count is at the limit, `replaceAll` routes to `_largeReplaceAll`, which skips match enumeration entirely: it takes `model.getValue(EndOfLinePreference.LF)`, forces the regex to `mu` flags, runs a single native `modelText.replace(searchRegex, fn)` (passing `arguments` straight into `buildReplaceString` so capture groups and preserveCase still work), and applies the result as one full-range edit via `ReplaceCommandThatPreservesSelection`. The find widget separately renders '19999+' and a tooltip saying 'Only the first N results are highlighted, but all find operations work on the entire text' (findWidget.ts:76, :457-483).

**Why it matters here.** A user replacing a common token across a large file gets a partial replacement with no warning — the count reads 19999, the operation reports success, and thousands of occurrences are left behind. This is the single worst kind of editor bug: silent data divergence from what the user asked for. Same for Select All Matches, which caps our multi-cursor set at the display limit.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findModel.ts:515-530 (replaceAll dispatch), :532-570 (_largeReplaceAll), :572-584 (_regularReplaceAll), :586-608 (selectAllMatches); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findController.ts:424-430 (isTooLargeForHeapOperation guard)

**Our code.** packages/find/src/findController.ts:210-228 `replaceAll()` calls `this.findAll(...)` which hard-codes `FIND_MATCHES_LIMIT` (findController.ts:379, search.ts:8 = 19_999); there is no unlimited variant and no large-file path. `selectAllMatches()` (findController.ts:230-239) uses the already-truncated `this.matches`. Our edit merging (findController.ts:555-573 `mergeAdjacentReplaceEdits`) is a faithful equivalent of Monaco's ReplaceAllCommand merge (replaceAllCommand.ts:46-59), so only the limit is wrong. Widget shows a bare count with no '+' (packages/find/src/findWidget.ts:276-279).

**Verifier.** Low effort given our architecture: add a `limit` argument to `findAll` and pass `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` from `replaceAll`/`selectAllMatches`. Monaco's `_largeReplaceAll` (a single regex replace over the whole text, bypassing enumeration) is a separate optimization we do not need until we care about >100k matches; the correctness half — Replace All actually replacing all — is the part worth taking. Also add the `19999+` overflow rendering in `resultCountText` so the cap is visible.

**How to implement.** Add a `limit` parameter to `findAll(captureMatches, limit)` and pass `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` from `replaceAll` and `selectAllMatches`; keep FIND_MATCHES_LIMIT for `research()` only. Then add the large path: when `this.scopes === null && this.matches.length >= FIND_MATCHES_LIMIT`, build the replacement with a single `String.prototype.replace` over `host.materializeFullText()` and emit one `{from: 0, to: text.length, text: result}` edit. Surface the limit in the widget: `${count}+` plus a title attribute, in `resultCountText` (findWidget.ts:276).

### Zero-width matches deadlock navigation; both references have an explicit escape and Monaco adds a line-stop rule

`partial` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **both**

**What it is.** CM5's cursor records `afterEmptyMatch` after each find and, on the next call, manually advances the head by one character (wrapping across the line boundary and re-clipping) before searching, 'do not return the same 0 width match twice'. Monaco detects the same condition post-hoc — `nextMatchRange.isEmpty() && nextMatchRange.getStartPosition().equals(after)` — and retries from `_nextSearchPosition(after)`. The clever part is that `_nextSearchPosition` is *pattern-aware*: `const isUsingLineStops = this._state.isRegex && (searchString.indexOf('^') >= 0 || searchString.indexOf('$') >= 0)`; when the pattern is anchored, advancing one column is useless (the next `^` is on the next line), so it jumps to line+1, column 1. Monaco's `Searcher.next` additionally dedupes identical consecutive matches and, for empty ones, bumps `lastIndex` by 2 when `getNextCodePoint(...) > 0xFFFF` so surrogate pairs aren't split.

**Why it matters here.** Search `$` or `^` or `\b` in regex mode, press Enter: the caret lands on a zero-width match, and every subsequent Enter re-selects the same zero-width range because `match.start >= selection.endOffset` is satisfied by the match itself. Find Previous deadlocks identically via `match.end <= selection.startOffset`. Anchored regex navigation — a very common power-user flow — is simply broken.

**Reference.** codemirror5/addon/search/searchcursor.js:236-274 (SearchCursor.find with afterEmptyMatch), :203-234 (constructor state); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findModel.ts:361-382 (_nextSearchPosition), :267-287 (_prevSearchPosition), :396-405 and :441-445 (the 'Looks like we're stuck at this position, unacceptable!' retries); vscode/src/vs/editor/common/model/textModelSearch.ts:513-554 (Searcher.next dedupe + surrogate-aware lastIndex bump)

**Our code.** We handle the *enumeration* side: packages/find/src/search.ts:190-196 `advancePastEmptyMatch` bumps lastIndex by the code-point size, and search.test.ts:83-90 asserts `(?=)` yields 3 matches on 'ab'. But navigation has no equivalent: packages/find/src/search.ts:50-60 `nextMatchAfter` uses `match.start >= offset` with `offset = selection.endOffset` (findController.ts:175-177), and :62-75 `previousMatchBefore` uses `match.end <= offset` with `offset = selection.startOffset` (findController.ts:184-186). Both re-return a zero-width match sitting exactly at the cursor. There is no line-stop concept anywhere.

**Verifier.** Genuinely reachable — `^`, `\b`, `(?=x)`, `$` are ordinary user queries and F3 currently sticks in place forever. Small fix contained to `nextMatchAfter`/`previousMatchBefore` plus their two call sites: when the returned match is empty and sits exactly at the cursor, advance the probe offset by one code point (clamped/wrapped) and retry once.

**How to implement.** Give `nextMatchAfter`/`previousMatchBefore` an `excludeEmptyAt: number | null` argument, or simpler: in `EditorFindController.findNext`, if the returned match is empty and `match.start === startOffset`, recompute from `nextSearchOffset(text, startOffset, isRegex && /[\^$]/.test(searchString))` — advancing to the next line start when anchored, otherwise by one code point via the existing `codePointSizeAt` (already imported in search.ts:2). Mirror for findPrevious using `lineRangeAtOffset` (packages/editor/src/textRanges.ts:35-41) for the line-stop case.

### Find Next past the match limit jumps to the top of the file instead of to the next match

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco treats the decoration list as a cache that is only valid below the limit: `if (this._decorations.getCount() < MATCHES_LIMIT) { ...use matchAfterPosition... return; }` and otherwise falls through to `_getNextMatch`, which runs a live incremental `model.findNextMatch(searchString, position, ...)` from the cursor. That live search is inherently unbounded — `_doFindNextMatchLineByLine` iterates `(startLineNumber + i - 1) % lineCount` so wraparound is a modular walk, and the multiline variant recurses once from Position(1,1). `_getNextMatch` also clamps the start position into the find scope and re-recurses once if the found match escaped the scope. `FindReplaceState.canNavigateInLoop()` (findState.ts:329-331) deliberately returns true when `matchesCount >= MATCHES_LIMIT` so the bounded-navigation guards don't misfire on truncated data.

**Why it matters here.** In any file where a token occurs more than 19,999 times (a minified bundle, a log, a large JSON), pressing Enter/F3 anywhere past the truncation point teleports the cursor to the first match at the top of the document, and keeps doing so — find becomes unusable exactly where the user most needs it.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findModel.ts:384-414 (_moveToNextMatch), :289-355 (_moveToPrevMatch), :416-457 (_getNextMatch); vscode/src/vs/editor/common/model/textModelSearch.ts:290-302 (findNextMatch), :331-352 (_doFindNextMatchLineByLine with modular wraparound), :368-395 (findPreviousMatch)

**Our code.** packages/find/src/search.ts:50-60 `nextMatchAfter` does `matches.find(m => m.start >= offset)` over the truncated array and falls back to `matches[0]` when nothing is found; `previousMatchBefore` (search.ts:62-75) mirrors it with `matches.at(-1)`. packages/find/src/findController.ts:172-188 feeds it `this.matches`, which `research()` (line 363) always fills via the capped `findAll(false)`. There is no incremental single-match search anywhere in packages/find.

**Verifier.** Correct, but rank it below claims 2/3/6 — it only bites past 19,999 matches. Worth noting we do already have a 'next exact occurrence from offset' primitive in packages/editor/src/editor/occurrences.ts:78 `findNextExactOccurrenceFromRange` / :99 `findExactOccurrenceFrom` (used by the Cmd+D-style multi-cursor commands), so the shape of an incremental search is not foreign to the codebase — it is just plain-string-only and full-text-based. A proper fix rides on claim 1's line-by-line searcher; a cheap interim fix is to detect `matches.length === FIND_MATCHES_LIMIT` and re-run `findMatches` over `[cursorOffset, textLength]` with `limit = 1`.

**How to implement.** Add `findNextMatchFrom(source, query, offset, captureMatches)` and `findPreviousMatchFrom(...)` to packages/find/src/search.ts — with the line-scoped source from finding #1 these are cheap and naturally unbounded. In `EditorFindController.findNext/findPrevious`, branch: if `this.matches.length >= FIND_MATCHES_LIMIT`, call the incremental search from the selection edge (clamped into `this.scopes` when set) instead of consulting `this.matches`. Keep the array path below the limit.

### Find-in-selection scope is recomputed from the live selection, so it is destroyed by the first replace and silently widens to the whole document

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco stores the find scope as a *tracked decoration* on the model, not as a selection. `getFindScopes()` reads the current ranges back out of the decoration ids, so every edit — including the find widget's own replaces — shifts the scope automatically and it survives arbitrary cursor movement. On top of that, both `toggleSearchScope` and `research` normalize a multi-line scope to whole lines: if `findScope.endColumn === 1` the last line is dropped, then the scope is rewritten as `(startLineNumber, 1) → (endLineNumber, getLineMaxColumn)`. So dragging a selection down to the start of line 4 gives a 3-line scope, and the scope highlight is whole-line.

**Why it matters here.** Two concrete failures in ours. (1) Replace One inside a selection scope: `replaceOne` sets a collapsed selection at the replacement point, then the resulting content update recomputes scopes from selections, gets an empty list, and `normalizedSearchRanges` treats an empty range list as 'search everything' — so the second Replace/Replace All escapes the scope and rewrites the whole file. (2) Even before that, `research()` after an edit uses the *pre-edit* scope offsets, so matches are computed against a window that has already shifted.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findDecorations.ts:65-75 (getFindScopes reads back tracked decoration ranges), :219-225 (scopes stored via accessor.addDecoration), :343-347 (_FIND_SCOPE_DECORATION); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findModel.ts:199-214 (research normalizes multi-line scopes to whole lines); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findController.ts:281-303 (toggleSearchScope trims a scope ending at column 1)

**Our code.** packages/find/src/findController.ts:311-313 in `handleViewUpdate`: `if (this.state.inSelection) this.scopes = nonEmptySelectionRanges(this.host?.getSelections() ?? [])` — and `nonEmptySelectionRanges` (findController.ts:521-527) filters out collapsed selections. packages/find/src/search.ts:107 `if (!ranges || ranges.length === 0) return [{ start: 0, end: text.length }]` is the widening step. `replaceOne` (findController.ts:201-206) hands the edit host a collapsed selection, then calls `research(true)` with the stale `this.scopes`. `toggleFindInSelection` (findController.ts:271-286) stores raw selection offsets with no whole-line normalization.

**Verifier.** Highest-severity item here: 'Replace All in selection' can rewrite the whole file once the first replace lands. Two independent fixes, both cheap. (1) Never conflate empty with null — an explicitly-empty scope array should yield zero matches, not the whole document (search.ts:107). (2) Track the scope through edits: we already ship the machinery — `Anchor`/`AnchorBias`/`resolveAnchor` in packages/textbuffer/src/anchors.ts, exported from the public document API — so a scope held as an anchor pair survives replaces exactly like Monaco's tracked decoration. Whole-line normalization is a nice-to-have on top.

**How to implement.** We already have the primitive: `anchorBefore`/`anchorAfter` with bias (packages/textbuffer/src/anchors.ts:191-212) plus `resolveAnchor` at :235. Store scopes as anchor pairs on the controller when `toggleFindInSelection`/`applyAutoFindInSelection` run, resolve them against the current snapshot inside `findAll`, and delete the recompute-from-selection in `handleViewUpdate`. Separately, make `normalizedSearchRanges` distinguish 'no scope' (null) from 'empty scope' (a scope that collapsed to nothing must yield zero matches, never the full document). Add the whole-line normalization from findModel.ts:202-213 when the scope spans multiple lines.

### Highlight painting is O(mounted rows × total matches) per frame, with no visible-window index

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco hands the match ranges to the model's decoration store (an interval tree). Rendering then asks only for decorations intersecting the rendered line range, so the per-frame cost scales with matches *in view*, not with total matches. Even the 'which match am I on' query is a range query: `getCurrentMatchesPosition` calls `getDecorationsInRange(desiredRange)` and matches on decoration identity, rather than scanning the array. Monaco explicitly caps the *other* consumer instead: when `findMatches.length > 1000`, it switches to `_FIND_MATCH_NO_OVERVIEW_DECORATION` and emits merged coarse bands for the overview ruler, 'because the overview ruler paints all the decorations in the file and we don't want to cause freezes' (findDecorations.ts:163-197).

**Why it matters here.** Search for `e` in a big file, get ~19,999 matches, and every scroll frame walks 19,999 ranges for each of ~60 mounted rows — over a million interval tests per frame — before any DOM range is created. This is a hard scroll stall triggered by an entirely ordinary search.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findDecorations.ts:157-207 (deltaDecorations into the model's interval tree), :229-271 (matchBeforePosition/matchAfterPosition), :102-112 (getCurrentMatchesPosition via getDecorationsInRange)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/virtualization/virtualizedTextViewHighlights.ts:1254-1260 `addMountedRangeHighlightRanges` loops `getMountedRows(view)`, and :1263-1270 `addMountedRangeHighlightRangesForRow` loops `for (const range of group.ranges)` — a full pass over every range for every row, with the cheap rejection at :1277 happening only after the inner loop is entered. The find controller pushes the whole match array in one call: packages/find/src/findController.ts:405 `host.setRangeHighlight(this.matchHighlightName, this.matches, FIND_MATCH_STYLE)`. Similarly `findMatchIndex` (search.ts:77-79) and `currentMatchIndex` (findController.ts:533-541) are linear scans run on every navigation.

**Verifier.** Real, and the fix is local and does not need an interval tree. `findMatches` already emits ranges in ascending order, so `addMountedRangeHighlightRanges` and `rangeHighlightSignature` can binary-search the first range with `end > row.startOffset` and break once `range.start >= row.endOffset` — we already have a `lowerBound` helper at packages/editor/src/virtualization/rowHeightIndex.ts:46 to copy. Worth doing independent of find: this path also serves LSP diagnostics and document highlights. Kill the per-frame per-range string allocation in the signature at the same time (hash or version counter instead).

**How to implement.** Ranges arrive sorted (search.ts appends in document order per range, and `normalizeTextOffsetRanges` sorts the scopes). Store `group.ranges` as a sorted array plus a binary search, and in `addMountedRangeHighlightRangesForRow` seek to the first range with `end > row.startOffset` and stop at the first with `start >= row.endOffset` — that alone turns the inner loop from O(all) into O(in-view). Use the same binary search for `findMatchIndex`/`currentMatchIndex`.

### Search materializes the entire document as one string; neither reference ever does

`missing` · value **high** · effort **L** · performance · from **both**

**What it is.** Monaco classifies the query first: `parseSearchRequest` decides `multiline` from the *pattern source* (`isMultilineRegexSource` returns true only for a literal \n, or an escape \n/\r/\W; a non-regex query is multiline only if it contains \n). If not multiline, `_doFindMatchesLineByLine` walks `model.getLineContent(lineNumber)` line by line and never concatenates — the piece tree is read one line at a time, and matches are produced as (line, column) ranges directly. Only when the pattern truly needs to cross lines does `_doFindMatchesMultiline` call `getValueInRange(range, EndOfLinePreference.LF)`. CM5 goes further: `searchRegexpForwardMultiline` grows its search buffer in *exponentially sized chunks* (`chunk = chunk * 2`, starting at 1 line) between retries, with the comment that this makes nearby matches fast "and don't require concatenating the whole document". The regex is re-run against a 1-line, then 2-line, 4-line... window until it matches.

**Why it matters here.** On a 20MB file our Cmd+F does a full red-black-tree walk plus a chunks.join('') allocation of the whole document before a single character is compared — and it does it again after every edit and (via findOnType) on every keystroke in the find box. Line-scoped search makes the common case (plain string, single-line regex) proportional to the number of lines actually scanned rather than to document size, and the CM5 chunking makes even multiline regex 'find next' near the cursor cheap.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/common/model/textModelSearch.ts:226-288 (_doFindMatchesLineByLine / _findMatchesInLine), :169-173 (multiline vs line-by-line dispatch), :29-66 (SearchParams.parseSearchRequest), :69-99 (isMultilineRegexSource); codemirror5/addon/search/searchcursor.js:33-43 (searchRegexpForward), :45-73 (searchRegexpForwardMultiline)

**Our code.** packages/find/src/search.ts:30-48 `findMatches(text: string, ...)` — the whole API takes a materialized string. packages/find/src/findController.ts:370-381 `findAll()` calls `host.materializeFullText()`; packages/find/src/plugin.ts:243 wires that to `getSnapshot().fullText`. packages/editor/src/editor/Editor.ts:2152 builds a *fresh* snapshot object per `getSnapshot()` call, so `defineLazyFullTextProperty`'s cache (documentTextSnapshot.ts:23-36) does not survive between calls; packages/editor/src/documentSession.ts:414,435,517,958 create post-edit snapshots with no `retainedText`, so `materializePieceTableFullText` (pieceTable/reads.ts:30-31) really re-walks the tree. Our compileFindQuery (search.ts:89) unconditionally sets the `m` flag and always scans the full text — there is no multiline/single-line classification at all.

**Verifier.** Real and the highest-leverage item in this domain, but the fix is bigger than swapping an algorithm: `EditorFindHost` (findController.ts:38-55) only offers `materializeFullText()`. We already own the primitives — `TextSnapshot.readRange`/`forEachTextChunk` (documentTextSnapshot.ts:12-17), `LineStartsView` (virtualization/lineStartIndex.ts:82), `streamPieceTableTextChunks` (pieceTable/reads.ts:33) — so the work is (a) widen the host to expose line/range reads, (b) port `isMultilineRegexSource`, (c) a line-by-line loop. Note our whole codebase leans on full-text materialization (occurrences.ts, seedSearchString, LSP), so find alone won't remove the cost; it will remove the per-keystroke cost, which is the one users feel.

**How to implement.** Change `findMatches` to take a text *source* rather than a string: `{ length, readRange(start,end), lineStartsView }`. We already have both — `TextSnapshot.readRange` (packages/editor/src/documentTextSnapshot.ts:14) and `EditorLineStartsView.at/indexForOffset/firstIndexAtOrAfter` (packages/editor/src/plugins.ts:171-177) — and `EditorViewSnapshot` already exposes `textSnapshot` and `lineStartsView`. Add a `isMultilineQuery(query)` port of isMultilineRegexSource; when false, loop lines via `lineStartsView.at(i)`/`at(i+1)` and `readRange` per line, drop the `m` flag (a single-line haystack makes ^/$ correct for free), and offset match indices by the line start. When true, use CM5's doubling-window strategy over `readRange`. Keep the current whole-string path as a fallback for the string-based public API and tests.

### Every content change triggers a synchronous full re-search; both references debounce it and repair incrementally

`missing` · value **high** · effort **L** · performance · from **both**

**What it is.** Monaco never re-searches synchronously on typing: content changes only `schedule()` a 100ms RunOnceScheduler, and query/option changes on a model over 50MB go through a 240ms `TimeoutTimer` instead of running immediately. Between re-searches, the match ranges stay correct because they are model decorations with `NeverGrowsWhenTypingAtEdges` stickiness — the model shifts them for free. CM5's scrollbar annotation does true incremental repair: `onChange` shifts every cached match's line by `sizeChange` via `offsetLine`, records the edited region as a `gap`, and 250ms later `findMatches()` splices out only the matches inside the gap and re-runs a SearchCursor over just that region.

**Why it matters here.** With the find widget open, every keystroke in the document currently costs a full piece-tree materialization plus a full-document scan plus (for case-insensitive search) a full-document `toLocaleLowerCase()`, all on the main thread before the frame can paint. That is the difference between typing smoothly and typing at 4fps in a large file with find open.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findModel.ts:105-134 (RunOnceScheduler(…, 100) on model content change), :156-177 (_onStateChanged with RESEARCH_DELAY=240 gated on isTooLargeForSyncing), :81-82 (constants); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findDecorations.ts:157-227 (matches held as deltaDecorations, so ranges shift without re-searching); codemirror5/addon/search/matchesonscrollbar.js:60-91 (gap-based incremental repair)

**Our code.** packages/find/src/findController.ts:298-314 `handleViewUpdate` calls `this.research(false)` directly for every `document`/`content` update, with no scheduler. `research()` (findController.ts:360-368) calls `findAll` → `materializeFullText` → full scan, then `updateHighlights` + `updateWidget`. `setSearchString` (findController.ts:288-291) also researches synchronously per keystroke when `findOnType` is on (default true, findController.ts:505). We do have schedulers available (packages/editor/src/editor/workScheduler.ts:115 `EditorWorkScheduler`, secondaryWorkScheduler.ts:21) but find uses neither.

**Verifier.** Correct, but the survey understates a dependency: debouncing alone would be a regression for us. Monaco can defer re-search because its matches are model decorations with `NeverGrowsWhenTypingAtEdges` stickiness that the model shifts for free; our matches are raw `{start,end}` offsets pushed straight into `setRangeHighlight` (findController.ts:405), so a deferred re-search means highlights painted at stale offsets while the user types. Sequence this after claim 6's anchor-tracked ranges (or shift pending match offsets by the applied edit's delta before deferring). With that in place, `EditorWorkScheduler.schedule({key: 'find.research', ...})` is a few lines.

**How to implement.** Two stages. Cheap first: route `handleViewUpdate`'s research through a coalescing timer (100ms) and `setSearchString` through a short debounce, keeping the current match list visible in between. Then the real win: keep matches as anchor-backed ranges (pieceTable/anchors.ts) so they shift with edits and only the edited region needs rescanning — CM5's `gap` model maps directly onto the `DocumentSessionChange` we already receive in `handleViewUpdate`, since we know the edited offset range and the length delta.

### Seeding the search string from the selection does not escape regex metacharacters when regex mode is on

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco funnels every seeded search string through `strings.escapeRegExpCharacters` when the state is in regex mode — in `setSearchString`, in `_start`'s selection seeding, and therefore in Cmd+E / Find-Next-Selection as well. It also bounds the seed: a selection is only used if `getValueLengthInRange(selection) < SEARCH_STRING_MAX_LENGTH` (512KB), and a *collapsed* selection seeds from `getConfiguredWordAtPosition` — the language-configured word definition, not a hard-coded one. The 'seedSearchStringFromSelection: selection' mode is expressed as `seedSearchStringFromNonEmptySelection`, which suppresses the word-at-cursor fallback entirely.

**Why it matters here.** With regex mode on (a sticky, persisted setting in Monaco and a plausible one for us), selecting `foo(bar)` and hitting Cmd+F yields a search for a pattern with a capture group instead of the literal text — the user sees wrong or zero results with no indication why. Also, selecting a huge blob and hitting Cmd+F currently stuffs the entire selection into the input and searches for it.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findController.ts:305-311 (setSearchString escapes when isRegex), :327-338 (_start seeding path escapes), :42-66 (getSelectionSearchString with SEARCH_STRING_MAX_LENGTH=524288 and the word-at-position fallback), :934-960 (SelectionMatchFindAction routes through setSearchString so it is escaped too)

**Our code.** packages/find/src/findController.ts:446-457 `seedSearchString` returns `selectedSingleLineText(...)` or the word at the cursor verbatim; nothing escapes it, and `this.state.isRegex` is not consulted. We do implement the multi-line suppression (`selectedSingleLineText`, findController.ts:515-519) and the 'never'/'always'/'selection' option (types.ts:3), so the option surface is right — only the escaping and the length bound are absent. We do already have `escapeRegExpCharacters` locally (search.ts:81-83) with the same character class as Monaco's strings.ts:89-91.

**Verifier.** Trivial and worth taking: export `escapeRegExpCharacters` from search.ts and wrap the seed when `state.isRegex`; add a length guard on the selection before slicing. Note our Cmd+D-family commands are a separate code path (packages/editor/src/editor/inputSelectionController.ts:558 `applyMoveSelectionToNextOccurrenceCommand` → occurrences.ts exact-string matching) and never touch find state, so they carry no escaping bug — the fix is genuinely one call site. The 512KB bound matters less for us since we currently materialize the whole text anyway.

**How to implement.** In `seedSearchString` (and any future setSearchString-from-selection entry point), wrap the result in `escapeRegExpCharacters` when `this.state.isRegex`. Export that helper from search.ts. Add a `SEARCH_STRING_MAX_LENGTH` guard before `text.slice(...)` in `selectedSingleLineText`.

### Replace One re-scans the whole document with capture groups enabled just to replace one match

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **S** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco's `replace()` computes exactly one match: `this._getNextMatch(selection.getStartPosition(), /*captureMatches*/ true, /*forceMove*/ false)`, which bottoms out in `TextModelSearch.findNextMatch` — a single incremental search from the cursor, not an enumeration. It then compares `selection.equalsRange(nextMatch.range)`: if the selection is already on the match, replace it; if not, just move to that match (the classic 'first Enter selects, second Enter replaces' behavior). Note the ordering — captures are only ever computed for the one match being replaced.

**Why it matters here.** Holding down Replace on a large file currently costs a full materialize + full regex scan *with capture arrays allocated for every match in the document* per replacement. Capture-enabled scanning is the expensive mode (it forces the regex path even for plain strings and allocates a `matches` array per hit), so this is the worst possible way to get one match's captures.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findModel.ts:481-505 (replace), :416-457 (_getNextMatch with captureMatches=true); vscode/src/vs/editor/common/model/textModelSearch.ts:290-302 (findNextMatch)

**Our code.** packages/find/src/findController.ts:190-208 `replaceOne` calls `this.currentOrSelectionMatch(true)`, and that (findController.ts:475-485) does `const matches = captureMatches ? this.findAll(true) : this.matches` — a full re-enumeration with `captureMatches = true`, then a linear `.find` for the selection's range. It then calls `this.research(true)` afterwards, which is a *third* full scan.

**Verifier.** Fixable today without claim 1's incremental searcher: locate the match in the already-computed `this.matches` (offsets only), then re-run the compiled regex against just `text.slice(match.start, match.end)` to recover the capture array. That removes scan #1 outright. The duplicate research (#2/#3) is removable by letting `handleViewUpdate` own the re-search after `applyEdits` instead of calling it again in `replaceOne`. Holding Cmd+Alt+Enter on a large file currently does three full-document scans per repeat.

**How to implement.** Once `findNextMatchFrom` from finding #4 exists, `replaceOne` becomes: search once from `selection.startOffset` with captures, compare against the selection, replace or navigate. Even without it, a cheap intermediate is to re-run the compiled regex over just `[match.start, match.end]` to recover the capture groups for the already-known range, instead of scanning the document.

### Replace-pattern edge cases: out-of-range $nn should degrade digit-by-digit, and a backslash should swallow the next character

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco's `_substitute` for an index beyond the capture count peels digits off the right: `while (matchIndex > 0) { if (matchIndex < matches.length) return (matches[matchIndex] || '') + remainder; remainder = String(matchIndex % 10) + remainder; matchIndex = Math.floor(matchIndex / 10); }`. So with one capture group, `$12` renders as `<group 1>` followed by a literal `2` — matching what users expect from sed/JS semantics — and only falls back to a literal `$…` when even the first digit is out of range. Separately, the parser's backslash branch does `i++` before the switch and has *no* default case, so an unrecognized escape like `\$` consumes both characters: `\$1` stays the literal three characters and is never treated as a capture reference.

**Why it matters here.** Both are small, but both are the kind of thing a user hits once and then stops trusting regex replace. `$12` silently emitting the literal text `$12` into their code, or `\$1` unexpectedly expanding to a capture, are the sort of quiet wrong-output bugs that are hard to notice in a bulk replace.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/replacePattern.ts:108-127 (_substitute), :238-287 (the backslash branch consumes the next char unconditionally, with no default case), :215-229 (the documented grammar); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/test/browser/replacePattern.test.ts

**Our code.** packages/find/src/replacePattern.ts:185-190 `substituteMatch` returns `` `$${index}` `` verbatim for out-of-range indices — no digit peeling. packages/find/src/replacePattern.ts:108-131 `consumeReplaceEscape` returns the unchanged index for an unrecognized escape, and the caller (parseReplaceString, :35-43) then advances only one character, so the `$` in `\$1` is re-examined by `consumeReplaceDollar` and *is* parsed as a capture reference — the opposite of Monaco. Everything else matches closely: our `\u/\U/\l/\L` handling (replacePattern.ts:192-208) and the whole preserve-case algorithm (replacePattern.ts:48-64, 227-259) are faithful ports of Monaco's replacePattern.ts:72-101 and base/common/search.ts:8-48.

**Verifier.** Low value but near-zero cost — roughly 15 lines across two functions, plus unit tests in the existing packages/find/test/search.test.ts. Worth folding into whatever other find work happens rather than scheduling on its own. Of the two, the backslash one is the more defensible bug: a user typing `\$1` to get a literal `$1` currently gets the capture group instead, i.e. there is no way to escape a dollar except `$$`.

**How to implement.** Rewrite `substituteMatch` (replacePattern.ts:185-190) as Monaco's peeling loop. In `consumeReplaceEscape`, when the character after `\` is not a recognized escape, still advance past it (return `index + 1` after `builder.emitUnchanged`-free passthrough) so the escaped character is never re-scanned. Port Monaco's replacePattern.test.ts cases wholesale — the file is 254 lines and covers exactly these corners.

### Monaco runs every find test twice, the second time over a piece tree with deliberately misaligned chunk boundaries

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **S** · testing · from **monaco**

**What it is.** `findTest(name, callback)` runs the same callback twice: once against a model built from a line array, and once against a `PieceTreeTextBufferBuilder` fed three chunks split at byte offsets 94/101/59 — boundaries chosen to fall in the middle of lines. Both runs assert the same thing via `assertFindState(editor, cursor, highlighted, findDecorations)`, which snapshots the cursor selection, the currently-highlighted match, *and* the full decoration range list in one deepStrictEqual. That single helper is why the suite can afford 2405 lines of cases: each test is three arrays.

**Why it matters here.** Any search implementation that reads text through a chunked buffer has bugs that only appear when a match straddles a chunk boundary — and those are exactly the bugs that a string-based test suite can never catch. If we move search onto the piece tree (findings #1 and #7), this harness is the thing that makes that safe. Our current suite tests `findMatches(string, ...)` and therefore cannot see chunk-boundary bugs at all.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/test/browser/findModel.test.ts:34-68 (findTest helper), :69-99 (_getFindState / assertFindState); the suite is 2405 lines and every case goes through findTest

**Our code.** packages/find/test/search.test.ts is 101 lines and calls `findMatches` with plain string literals only; packages/find/test/plugin.test.ts (181 lines) exercises the plugin wiring. Neither constructs a piece table. Our piece table does have the machinery — packages/textbuffer/src/reads.ts:33-49 `streamPieceTableTextChunks` and pieceTable.test.ts exist — it is just not used by find's tests. Note pieceTableSnapshotsHaveSameText (reads.ts:72-101) already walks two chunk streams with misaligned boundaries, so the pattern is familiar in this codebase.

**Verifier.** Keep, but reframe: the chunk-boundary half only pays off once claim 1's line/chunk-based searcher exists (today `findMatches` takes a string, so buffer chunking is invisible to it). The half that pays off immediately is the `assertFindState` shape — a controller-level test driving `EditorFindController` against a real `DocumentSession` and asserting (selection, current index, highlight ranges) after each command. Claims 4, 5, 6 and 10 are all behavioural bugs that such a test would have caught and that no unit test on `findMatches` ever can. `pieceTableSnapshotsHaveSameText` (packages/textbuffer/src/reads.ts:72-101) shows the misaligned-chunk-walking pattern is already idiomatic here for when it is needed.

**How to implement.** Add a `findTest(name, fn)` helper in packages/find/test that runs each assertion against (a) a plain string and (b) a `DocumentSession` built by feeding the same text in three arbitrary chunks that split lines, reading through `TextSnapshot.readRange`. Add an `assertFindState(controller, {cursor, current, matches})` helper so cases stay one-liners. Do this *before* the line-scoped search change, so it acts as the safety net for it.

### Find matches are not surfaced on the minimap or scrollbar, and Monaco's >1000-match merge is the reason that is not trivial

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **M** · feature · from **both**

**What it is.** Both give the user an at-a-glance map of where matches are. Monaco attaches `overviewRuler` and `minimap` color options to the find-match decoration itself — but only up to 1000 matches. Beyond that it swaps to a no-overview decoration and computes *merged* bands: it derives `mergeLinesDelta = Math.max(2, Math.ceil(3 / (height / lineCount)))` — the number of document lines that collapse into ~3 ruler pixels — then coalesces consecutive matches within that distance into a single band. CM5's `showMatchesOnScrollbar` is a separate lightweight annotation capped at 1000, refreshed incrementally. CM5 also highlights matches *in the text* via a tokenizer overlay (`searchOverlay`) that runs per rendered line, so text highlighting costs nothing for off-screen matches.

**Why it matters here.** 'Where are the 40 matches in this 3000-line file' is answered instantly by a scrollbar/minimap map and not at all by a count label. We already ship a minimap with a decoration API used by diagnostics, so this is close to free — except for the density problem, which Monaco has already solved with a concrete formula worth copying rather than rediscovering.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/find/browser/findDecorations.ts:161-197 (approximate overview-ruler decorations), :286-334 (decoration options carrying minimap + overviewRuler color); codemirror5/addon/search/matchesonscrollbar.js:20-58 (SearchAnnotation with MAX_MATCHES=1000), :14-18 (showMatchesOnScrollbar extension); codemirror5/addon/search/search.js:113-116 (startSearch wires the annotation), :25-43 (searchOverlay: per-visible-line lazy highlighting)

**Our code.** packages/minimap/src/plugin.ts:295-311 `MinimapDecorationRegistry.setDecorations(sourceId, ...)` exists and is consumed by packages/lsp-plugin/src/diagnosticsPresenter.ts:129, but nothing in packages/find references the minimap — grep for 'minimap' across packages/find/src returns nothing. Find's only visual output is `setRangeHighlight` on the text layer (findController.ts:401-413).

**Verifier.** Keep, but scope it to the minimap only — we have no overview ruler, and building one is a much larger job than the survey implies. Half the work is already done: register find as a minimap decoration source and convert match offsets to line/column via the existing `LineStartsView`. The >1000 merge matters for us for a different reason than Monaco's: our minimap decoration list is a plain flattened array (`getDecorations()` at plugin.ts:312 does `Array.from(...).flat()`), so 19,999 unmerged entries would be re-flattened and re-rendered on every notify.

**How to implement.** Have `EditorFindController.updateHighlights` also push to the minimap feature via a new optional host method `setMinimapDecorations(sourceId, decorations)` mirroring `setRangeHighlight`. Port the merge: convert match offsets to rows via `lineStartsView.indexForOffset`, and when `matches.length > 1000` coalesce rows within `mergeLinesDelta` computed from the minimap's own height/lineCount before handing them over.
