## Language features / IntelliSense

### Re-diffing formatter output into minimal edits before applying it

`missing` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Formatter results are never applied as given. The worker sorts the edits, merges adjacent ones (:238-248), drops no-ops (`original === text`, :264-267), normalizes EOLs to the model's, and then — for each remaining edit under a `_diffLimit` size cap — runs a character-level `stringDiff` between the *current* text in the range and the replacement, emitting one small edit per change region mapped back through `model.offsetAt/positionAt` (:276-290). A single whole-document replacement from `prettier`/`tsserver` therefore becomes a handful of tiny edits. Each produced edit is checked once more against the model before being kept (:287).

**Why it matters here.** Directly determines whether formatting destroys editor state. A whole-buffer replace invalidates every anchor, decoration, fold, and selection, blows out the undo stack as one giant entry, and makes the whole viewport repaint. With minimal edits, the caret stays put, folds survive, and a no-op format is genuinely a no-op. It matters more for us than for Monaco because our anchors and fold map are offset-tracked.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/common/services/editorWebWorker.ts:218-298 ($computeMoreMinimalEdits); called from vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/format/browser/format.ts:245-247 (range formatting) and the document-formatting path around :290-330

**Our code.** packages/lsp-plugin/src/formatting.ts:13-38 — `formattingEdits()` converts LSP edits, sorts, drops overlaps, reverses, and returns them raw; there is no diffing step. `formattingChangesText` (:42-44) only decides whether *anything* changed, it does not minimize. We already have the diff primitive: packages/textbuffer/src/diff.ts.

**Verifier.** Strong keep — arguably better value/effort than anything else here, because our own source comment names the symptom. Minimizing formatter output is what makes the caret, the selection, folds, diagnostics anchors and inline-replacement anchors survive a format instead of being reset; with a whole-document edit every anchor in packages/textbuffer/src/anchors.ts dies at once. Correction to the claim's evidence: packages/textbuffer/src/diff.ts is NOT the right primitive (it returns a single prefix/suffix edit); use the `diff` package already vendored for packages/diff, and apply Monaco's size cap so a 5 MB minified file does not run a quadratic diff. Also worth stealing the cheap parts of editorWebWorker.ts:238-267 — merging adjacent edits and dropping `original === text` no-ops — which are ~10 lines and independent of the diff.

**How to implement.** Add a `minimizeFormattingEdits(text, edits)` step inside `packages/lsp-plugin/src/formatting.ts`, between the overlap filter and the reverse: for each edit whose `to - from` and `text.length` are both under a cap (say 100k), run the existing `packages/textbuffer/src/diff.ts` routine over `text.slice(from, to)` vs `edit.text`, and expand into per-change-region edits offset by `from`; skip edits whose diff is empty. Merge adjacent edits first, exactly as the reference does, so a formatter that emits per-line edits does not defeat the diff. Existing tests in packages/lsp-plugin should be extended with a 'whole-document replace that changes one line' case asserting a single small edit comes out.

### Completion insert-vs-replace ranges: we silently drop the replace range

`partial` · value **high** · effort **S** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** An LSP/Monaco completion carries two end positions: `insert` (replace only up to the caret) and `replace` (replace the whole word the caret sits in). Monaco keeps both, validates that neither spans lines and that they share a start column, and at acceptance computes `overwriteBefore = caretColumn - editStart.column` and `overwriteAfter = (replace ? editReplaceEnd : editInsertEnd).column - caretColumn`, then *adds* `columnDelta` — the number of characters typed between the request and the acceptance — so a completion accepted after further typing still overwrites the right span. `suffixDelta` restores text the user overtyped over a selection. Shift+Enter flips insert/replace for a single acceptance.

**Why it matters here.** With `foo|Bar` in the buffer and accepting `foobaz`, insert mode gives `foobazBar` and replace mode gives `foobaz`. We always take the insert range, so accepting a completion in the middle of an existing identifier leaves the tail behind — a visible, frequently-hit bug. The `columnDelta` correction matters too: our 80 ms debounce guarantees the user has often typed more since the request.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/suggest/browser/suggest.ts:100-120 (CompletionItem normalizes range into editStart/editInsertEnd/editReplaceEnd and validates single-line-ness); suggestController.ts:573-589 (getOverwriteInfo: picks insert or replace end per `suggest.insertMode`, adds `columnDelta` for characters typed since the request went out, and `suffixDelta` from the OvertypingCapturer); :209 (ctxHasInsertAndReplace) and :684 (AlternativeOverwriteConfig — shift+Enter inverts the mode for one acceptance); suggestOvertypingCapturer.ts:10-74

**Our code.** packages/lsp-plugin/src/completion.ts:388-393 — `completionTextEdit()` for an `InsertReplaceEdit` returns `{ range: textEdit.insert, newText }`, discarding `.replace` unconditionally. `completionApplication` (:174-194) uses the offset captured at request time (`session.offset` in completionController.ts:213, :266) with no delta for characters typed since. No validation that the edit range is single-line. No insert/replace toggle key.

**Verifier.** Keep, but re-prioritise inside the claim. The InsertReplaceEdit drop is a two-line fix and low impact given our default path already replaces the whole word. The *stale offset* is the real bug and is worth calling out separately: accept the completion after typing two more characters and the edit is computed against an offset that no longer matches the buffer. Monaco's `overwriteBefore = caretColumn - editStart.column` / `overwriteAfter` reformulation is the fix worth copying — it re-derives the range from the *current* caret rather than the request-time offset, which makes the whole class of races go away. The user-facing insert/replace toggle key is polish; skip it.

**How to implement.** Change `completionTextEdit` to return both ends, and add an `insertMode: 'insert' | 'replace'` option (default 'replace', which is what most users expect and what TypeScript assumes) threaded through `CompletionControllerOptions`. In `completionApplication`, compute the replacement span from the chosen end and add `currentOffset - session.offset` to it, validating the LSP range is on one line and rejecting the item otherwise (Monaco's `isInvalid`). Bind Shift+Enter in `handleCompletionKeyDown` (completionController.ts:293-330) to accept with the mode inverted.

### Incomplete completion lists: per-provider re-query with item reuse, instead of hide-and-refetch

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · correctness · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Monaco keeps the completion list alive across typing and only re-queries what must be re-queried. On each cursor/content change `_onNewContext` classifies the delta: cursor moved left → retrigger if still on a word, else cancel; leading whitespace changed → cancel; a *new* word started → retrigger but pass the entire current item map as `providerItemsToReuse`; cursor moved right and at least one provider marked its list `incomplete` → build a `providerFilter` of *only* the incomplete providers and a `providerItemsToReuse` of all the complete ones, and retrigger with `CompletionTriggerKind.TriggerForIncompleteCompletions`; otherwise → no request at all, just update `completionModel.lineContext` and re-filter locally. Trigger characters get the same treatment (:280-296): typing `.` re-asks only the providers that declare `.` and keeps everyone else's items. There is also a 'freeze' behaviour (:800-822): when the user *manually* invoked suggest and filtering has emptied the list, the old lineContext is restored and the widget stays up rather than vanishing mid-word.

**Why it matters here.** This is the difference between a suggest widget that feels continuous and one that flickers. It also fixes a real correctness hole: `CompletionList.isIncomplete` from a server means 'ask me again as the user types', and a client that ignores it shows a stale, wrongly-filtered list for large member sets (TS auto-import completions are always incomplete).

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/suggest/browser/suggestModel.ts:717-838 (_onNewContext), specifically :770-790 (incomplete re-query with providerFilter/providerItemsToReuse), :752-767 (new-word retrigger reusing all items), :253-297 (trigger-character retrigger reusing non-triggering providers); completionModel.ts:101-110 (getIncompleteProvider), :96-99 (getItemsByProvider); suggest.ts:296-330 (providerItemsToReuse honoured per provider)

**Our code.** packages/lsp-plugin/src/completionController.ts:69-91 — `update()` hides the widget on `selection`, `viewport`, `layout`, `document` and on any `content` change that is not itself a trigger; every accepted trigger schedules a fresh full `textDocument/completion` after an 80 ms debounce (COMPLETION_REQUEST_DEBOUNCE_MS, completion.ts:10). packages/lsp-plugin/src/completion.ts:149-155 `completionItems()` discards `CompletionList.isIncomplete` entirely — the flag is never read anywhere in packages/.

**Verifier.** Confirmed and worth fixing, but the framing should be inverted for us: the acute bug is not 'we do not reuse items', it is that `update()` hides the widget on *selection* changes — and typing a character produces a selection change as well as a content change, so the widget lifetime is fragile in ways a user feels immediately (backspace kills the list). Step 1: keep the session alive and re-filter locally against the current prefix (we already re-filter at completionController.ts:209 with `completionPrefix`, just at the wrong moment). Step 2: read `isIncomplete` in `completionItems()` and only re-request when it is set. Step 3 (Monaco's `providerItemsToReuse`) is only meaningful after claim 1's multi-provider aggregation exists — skip it until then.

**How to implement.** Give `CompletionController` a session object holding `{ items, isIncomplete, requestOffset, requestPrefix }` that survives content changes. On a `content` update, compute the new prefix via `completionPrefix()`; if the caret is still inside the same word and the prefix *extends* the request prefix, re-filter locally (no request) unless `isIncomplete`, in which case re-request and replace. Cancel only on the conditions Monaco cancels on: line changed, leading-whitespace changed, caret left the word. Stop hiding on `selection` updates that are the consequence of typing. Since we currently have a single provider, `providerItemsToReuse` collapses to 'keep or replace the whole list' until the registry from finding #1 lands, at which point the per-provider maps become necessary.

### Code actions: hierarchical kind filtering, isPreferred ordering, and 'auto fix'

`missing` · value **high** · effort **M** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Code actions are kinds in a dotted hierarchy (`quickfix`, `refactor.extract.function`, `source.organizeImports`), and filtering uses prefix containment on that hierarchy, not equality — so a `refactor` request matches `refactor.extract.function`. The lightbulb is driven by an oracle debounced at 250 ms that listens to *both* cursor movement and diagnostic changes, and refuses to trigger when the selection is enclosed in whitespace (which is why the bulb does not flicker while you type). Ranking puts actions that reference a diagnostic first, then `isPreferred` ones — and `hasAutoFix` (a preferred quickfix) is what lets Cmd+. apply a single obvious fix with no menu at all, and what changes the bulb's icon.

**Why it matters here.** Quick fix is the highest-value LSP feature we do not have; it is how you add a missing import, install a missing type, or apply an eslint fix. We already have diagnostics rendered and `workspaceEdit.ts` to apply the results, so this is mostly the trigger/selection/UI layer.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/codeAction/browser/codeAction.ts:43-96 (ManagedCodeActionSet: comparator ranks diagnostic-bearing actions above others, then isPreferred; `hasAutoFix` = a valid action whose kind is under `quickfix` AND isPreferred), :98-160 (getCodeActions: builds a `CodeActionContext` with `only`, queries every matching provider in parallel, filters each result with `filtersAction`, reports slow providers after 1250 ms); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/codeAction/common/types.ts (HierarchicalKind `contains`); codeActionModel.ts:35-80 (CodeActionOracle: 250 ms debounce on cursor position AND on marker changes, suppressed when the selection is whitespace-enclosed); codeActionCommands.ts:274-301 (AutoFixAction → CodeActionAutoApply.IfSingle); lightBulbWidget.ts:103-250 (state machine, distinct icon when hasAutoFix)

**Our code.** No `codeAction` anywhere in packages/ (grep). We do have diagnostics end-to-end (packages/lsp-plugin/src/diagnostics.ts, diagnosticProjection.ts, diagnosticsPresenter.ts), a workspace-edit applier (packages/lsp-plugin/src/workspaceEdit.ts:1-85, used by rename), and a floating widget pattern to copy (renameWidget.ts:1-110). `textDocument/codeAction` is not in the request list and `codeAction` is absent from packages/lsp/src/capabilities.ts.

**Verifier.** Keep — highest feature-value item, and unusually cheap for us because every prerequisite exists (workspace-edit applier, diagnostics with ranges, a floating widget pattern, an edit-feature transaction boundary). The two mechanisms worth copying precisely are (a) prefix containment on the dotted kind hierarchy rather than string equality, so a `refactor` request matches `refactor.extract.function` — get this wrong and every filtered request returns nothing, and (b) the 250 ms oracle debounced on *both* cursor movement and diagnostic changes, since a naive cursor-only trigger misses the case where diagnostics arrive after the cursor settled. Skip VS Code's lightbulb-in-the-gutter UI decisions; our gutters package (packages/gutters) has its own model.

**How to implement.** Add `packages/lsp-plugin/src/codeActionController.ts`: advertise `textDocument.codeAction` with `codeActionLiteralSupport` + `isPreferredSupport` + `resolveSupport` in capabilities.ts; an oracle debounced at 250 ms on selection change and on the diagnostics-updated event that diagnosticsPresenter already emits, passing the diagnostics overlapping the selection as `context.diagnostics`. Sort with the two-level comparator (has-diagnostics, then isPreferred) and implement `HierarchicalKind.contains` as dotted-prefix matching. Render the menu with the renameWidget.ts pattern, and put the lightbulb in `packages/gutters`. Apply results through `workspaceEdit.ts` (resolve the action first when it arrives with only a `command`/no `edit`). Wire `editor.action.autoFix` to apply the single preferred quickfix directly, no menu.

### LanguageFeatureRegistry: score-based, multi-provider language feature dispatch

`partial` · value **high** · effort **L** · api-design · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Every language feature (completion, hover, definition, code action, formatting, semantic tokens, …) is one generic `LanguageFeatureRegistry<T>` instance. Providers register with a `LanguageSelector` (language id, uri scheme, glob pattern, or an array of those). On each query `_updateScores` (:163) scores every entry against the model's uri+languageId, memoized on a `MatchCandidate` (:38-54) so repeated queries for the same model do zero work; entries are then sorted by score desc, built-in selectors deprioritized, and newer registrations winning ties (`_time`, :202-223). `ordered()` returns a flat best-first list; `orderedGroups()` (:132-148) buckets providers of *equal* score, and consumers like `provideSuggestionItems` walk group by group and **stop at the first group that produced any result** (suggest.ts:293-330) — so a specific provider shadows a generic one, but two equally-specific providers are merged. `exclusive: true` (:19-27, :183-195) lets one provider blank the whole registry (score 1000).

**Why it matters here.** It is the single extensibility primitive that makes 'multiple language sources' work: a tree-sitter word-completer, an LSP, a snippet source and a path-completer can all answer the same request and get merged deterministically, and a markdown-in-typescript embedded region can route to a different provider. Without it every feature is hard-wired to one backend.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/common/languageFeatureRegistry.ts:56-236 (register/all/ordered/orderedGroups/_updateScores/_compareByScoreAndTime); vscode/src/vs/editor/common/languageSelector.ts score() (string '*' → 5, exact language → 10, scheme/pattern/notebook each 0/5/10, array → max); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/suggest/browser/suggest.ts:293-330 (consumes registry.orderedGroups)

**Our code.** Our capability system (packages/editor/src/plugins.ts:31-41, Editor.ts:2256-2277) is single-owner: `registerFeature` throws `Editor feature already registered` on a duplicate token id and `getFeature` returns one value. Language features are hard-wired to exactly one LspClient: packages/lsp-plugin/src/completionController.ts:46-51, hoverDefinitionController.ts, documentHighlightController.ts, signatureHelpController.ts all take a single `client` in their options. No file in packages/ contains a registry keyed by language selector (grep for `registry|Registry` in packages/editor/src + packages/lsp-plugin/src only hits displayProjectionRegistry).

**Verifier.** Real gap, but narrower than stated. The plugin plumbing for N providers already exists (contribution arrays, owner/layer/priority merge in displayProjectionRegistry). What is missing is (1) an aggregation layer per language feature so two LspClients (e.g. tsserver + eslint + a Copilot-style provider) can both answer completion/hover into one widget, and (2) ordered/first-non-empty vs merge semantics per feature. The VS Code half worth *skipping* is the selector scoring itself — uri scheme globs, notebook cell types, `shouldSynchronizeModel` — that is workbench multi-scheme machinery we have no analogue for; a plain languageId match plus a priority number gets us the same behaviour. Prerequisite for claims 3, 9, 10 landing cleanly (inline completions and code actions are the features that actually have multiple providers in practice).

**How to implement.** Add `packages/editor/src/editor/languageFeatureRegistry.ts` exporting `createLanguageFeatureRegistry<T>()` with `register(selector, provider)`, `ordered(doc)`, `orderedGroups(doc)`, using our existing `EditorSyntaxLanguageId` + document uri/path as the match candidate and the same 0/5/10 scoring. Keep the `MatchCandidate` memo — our controllers query on every keystroke. Then change `packages/editor/src/plugins.ts` to add a second, multi-valued registration channel (`registerProvider(token, selector, provider)`) alongside the existing single-owner `registerFeature`, so existing capability tokens keep their semantics. Finally re-express `packages/lsp-plugin/src/*Controller.ts` as providers registered into that registry rather than direct client callers; `packages/typescript-lsp` and future tree-sitter/word providers then register alongside.

### Suggest filtering: bounded fuzzy DP scoring with match positions, incremental re-filter, and filterText/label split

`partial` · value **high** · effort **L** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** `fuzzyScore` is a Smith-Waterman-style DP over pattern×word, but with three cost controls: (a) a linear `isPatternInWord` subsequence pre-check that also records, per pattern position, the *earliest* possible word match (`_minWordMatchPos`), (b) `_fillInMaxWordMatchPos` scanning backwards for the *latest* possible match, so each DP row only iterates the [min,max] window instead of the whole word, (c) `_maxLen` truncation. `_doScore` (:847) hands out 7 for a case-exact common-prefix hit, 5 for case-insensitive or camelCase-boundary or post-separator hits, 1 otherwise, with a −3/−5 penalty for a gap before the first match and a −5 gap-start penalty in the DP transitions (:739,:742); the backtrace pushes the matched word positions into the same tuple as the score, so the widget gets bold highlight ranges for free. `fuzzyScoreGracefulAggressive` (:921) retries up to 7 adjacent-character transpositions of the pattern (`cnoso`→`conso`) with a −3 penalty, which is what makes typos still match. CompletionModel then adds two systems-level wins: `Refilter.Incr` (:86) — when the user typed *more* characters, re-filter only the previously surviving items, never the full list — and a filterText/label split (:198-215) where the *rank* comes from scoring `filterText` but the *highlights* come from re-scoring the label with `anyScore`. Final sort is score → word-distance (locality bonus: `wordDistance.ts:52-76` ranks a candidate by how many enclosing bracket-scopes away its nearest textual occurrence is, keywords and never-seen words getting `2<<20`) → original provider order.

**Why it matters here.** Our current ranker is a 3-bucket prefix/ci-prefix/subsequence test with a `localeCompare` tiebreak, so `cthn` does not find `createThreadName`, nothing is bolded in the widget, a locally-used identifier does not beat an obscure global, and every keystroke re-scores the full server list from scratch.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/base/common/filters.ts:683-833 (fuzzyScore), :847-914 (_doScore), :631-643 (isPatternInWord pre-check), :835-845 (_fillInMaxWordMatchPos), :921-957 (fuzzyScoreGracefulAggressive), :656-667 (FuzzyScore tuple layout); vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/suggest/browser/completionModel.ts:82-89 (Refilter.Incr), :123-242 (_createCachedState), :198-224 (filterText vs label), :244-260 (_compareCompletionItems); wordDistance.ts:52-76

**Our code.** packages/lsp-plugin/src/completion.ts:578-618 — `rankCompletionItems` / `completionMatchScore` returns only 3/2/1 and `isSubsequence` is a boolean; no match positions are produced. completion.ts:300-309 `completionLabelElement` sets `textContent` only, so no highlight rendering exists. completionController.ts:209-217 re-ranks the *whole* item list on every render and slices to 100. No word-distance or locality concept anywhere in packages/.

**Verifier.** Highest-value item in this set. Two separable deliverables: (a) a scorer returning match positions — needed purely so the widget can bold the matched characters, which is the single most visible quality difference between our list and Monaco's; (b) the bounded DP itself, which only matters once lists get long. Do (a) first; it can wrap the existing 3/2/1 scorer if the DP is deferred. Note the `.slice(0, 100)` at completionController.ts:216 means the DP cost ceiling is already capped for rendering but not for scoring — scoring still touches every item, so the [min,max] window bound is the part that actually pays.

**How to implement.** Port `fuzzyScore` + `_doScore` + `anyScore` into `packages/editor/src/editor/fuzzyScore.ts` (pure, no VS Code deps — it only needs the `_table/_arrows/_diag` module-level typed arrays and CharCode checks). Return the same `[score, wordStart, ...matchPositions]` tuple. In completion.ts, replace `completionMatchScore` with it, keep `sortText` as a third-level tiebreak, and change `completionLabelElement` to emit `<span>`/`<mark>` runs from the match positions. Add the `Refilter.Incr` distinction to `CompletionController`: keep the last filtered array and, when the new prefix extends the old one, filter that instead of the raw list. Locality bonus can reuse `packages/editor/src/editor/tokenIndex.ts` / the viewport word scan behind `occurrences.ts` to build a word→offsets map for the enclosing fold/bracket scope.

### Snippet engine: placeholder mirrors, transforms with regex + case-shorthands, choices, and indentation normalization

`partial` · value **high** · effort **L** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Snippets parse into a marker tree, not a string+ranges pair. Placeholders with the same index form a *group* (`groupBy(placeholders, Placeholder.compareByIndex)`), so `$1` appearing three times gives one tab stop with three live decorations that all update together — mirrors. Each stop's decoration options are swapped on every `move()`: the active stop uses grow-on-edge stickiness while inactive ones use never-grow, which is precisely what makes typing at a placeholder boundary do the right thing. Transforms (`${1/(.*)/${1:capitalize}/}`) run when the caret *leaves* the stop (:102-125), re-indenting any newlines the transform introduced. `adjustWhitespace` (:427) rewrites the template's Text markers before insertion so a multi-line snippet lands at the caller's indentation and in the caller's tab style. Nested placeholders are real (`enclosingPlaceholders`), and `_hasPlaceholderBeenCollapsed` (:189-210) skips a stop the user has emptied.

**Why it matters here.** Everything past `${1:name}` in our parser is silently lossy, and mirrors are the single most-noticed snippet feature (rename a `$1` and every copy follows). LSP servers routinely ship snippets with transforms and choices; TypeScript's own `Method Signature` completions use nested placeholders.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/snippet/browser/snippetParser.ts:228-283 (Placeholder, compareByIndex), :285-317 (Choice), :318-365 (Transform.resolve / _replace with else-branch), :368-489 (FormatString: upcase/downcase/capitalize/pascalcase/camelcase/kebabcase/snakecase + if/else), :490-530 (Variable with transform), :567 (placeholders()), :599 (enclosingPlaceholders); snippetSession.ts:28-92 (OneSnippet, groupBy(placeholders, compareByIndex)), :93-190 (move(): applies placeholder transforms on leave, flips decoration stickiness so the *active* stop grows at its edges and inactive ones do not, skips stops the user deleted), :427-479 (adjustWhitespace: normalizes each snippet line's indentation against the insertion line's leading whitespace and the model's tab settings), :306-330 (merge for multi-cursor); snippetVariables.ts:79-393 (Selection/Model/Clipboard/Comment/Time/Workspace/Random resolvers)

**Our code.** packages/editor/src/editor/snippet.ts:117-147 — nested placeholder defaults are flattened to plain text and their stops dropped (comment at :124-125); a choice `${1|a,b|}` inserts only the first option (:130-137); no `/regex/format/flags` transform branch exists at all; `variableValue` (:197-202) answers exactly two variables and returns `''` for everything else. packages/lsp-plugin/src/completion.ts:454-470 — `completionSnippetStops` explicitly keeps only `stop.ranges[0]`, with the comment 'a repeated stop's mirrors are not edited together yet'. packages/editor/src/editor/snippetSession.ts:27-40 tracks one anchor pair per stop, and bails entirely when `ranges.length <= 1`. No indentation normalization of the inserted template anywhere.

**Verifier.** Keep, and note the cheapest high-value slice: the parser already produces mirror ranges, so mirrors are a *plumbing* fix (widen SnippetStop to N anchor pairs in snippetSession.ts, drop the `ranges[0]` truncation in completion.ts:467) rather than a parser rewrite. That alone makes `for (const ${1:x} of ${2:xs}) { ${1:x} }` behave. Transforms (`${1/(.*)/${1:/upcase}/}`) are genuinely absent and are the long tail — lower value, do last. Indentation normalization matters most for multi-line server snippets inserted at depth; today they land flush-left relative to the insertion point, which is visible in any TS `for`/`try` completion.

**How to implement.** Restructure `snippet.ts` from a string-builder into a marker tree (Text | Placeholder | Choice | Variable | Transform), keeping `parseSnippet()` as a thin adapter that walks the tree to produce `{ text, stops }` so existing callers do not break. Extend `SnippetStop` to carry all its ranges and mark one as primary. In `snippetSession.ts`, track a group of anchor pairs per stop and, on each edit inside the active stop, mirror the text into the sibling ranges via `packages/editor/src/editor/textEdits.ts` in the same edit chain (our anchors already survive edits, so the tracking half is done). Port `FormatString` verbatim — it is dependency-free — and run transforms in `SnippetSession.move()` before advancing. Add indentation normalization using the existing `packages/editor/src/editor/indentation.ts` helpers.

### Commit characters: accepting the focused suggestion on the next typed character

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **S** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Servers attach `commitCharacters` to a completion item (TypeScript sends `.`, `,`, `(` for most items). Monaco listens on `onWillType` — i.e. the character is intercepted *before* insertion — and if it is in the focused item's set, accepts the completion instead, then lets the character through. The set is recomputed only when the focused item changes, and is cleared when the widget hides or the model goes idle. `NoAfterUndoStop` means a single Ctrl+Z undoes both the accepted completion and the typed character together.

**Why it matters here.** It is the interaction that makes typing `arr.fil(` produce `arr.filter(` without ever pressing Enter or Tab, and it is essentially free for us because our TypeScript worker already produces the data and throws it away.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/suggest/browser/suggestCommitCharacters.ts:14-70 (CommitCharacterController: rebuilds a CharacterSet from the *focused* item's commitCharacters on every focus change, and on editor.onWillType — before the character reaches the buffer — accepts if the character is in the set and the widget is not frozen); wired at suggestController.ts:169 with `InsertFlags.NoAfterUndoStop` so the commit character and the completion share one undo step. Contrast CM5: codemirror5/addon/hint/show-hint.js:118-127 (cursorActivity closes the widget when the character before the caret matches `closeCharacters`, default `/[\s()\[\]{};:>,]/`) and :508-513 (`completeSingle`, `closeOnPick`, `updateOnCursorActivity` defaults) — a coarser, close-instead-of-commit variant of the same idea.

**Our code.** packages/typescript-lsp/src/typescriptLsp.worker.ts:652 already emits `commitCharacters: entry.commitCharacters ?? info.defaultCommitCharacters`, but no consumer exists: grep for `commitCharacters` in packages/lsp-plugin/src returns nothing, and completionController.ts:293-330 only handles ArrowUp/Down, PageUp/Down, Escape, Enter and Tab.

**Verifier.** Keep — genuinely small and the ingredients are on both ends already (server emits the set; a beforeinput hook exists to intercept). Two mechanism details from Monaco are load-bearing and easy to get wrong: the set must be recomputed only when the *focused item* changes (not per keystroke), and it must be cleared when the widget hides or the model changes, otherwise a stale set commits a suggestion that is no longer on screen. Also: the character must still be inserted after the accept, in the same transaction, or `foo` + `.` yields `foo` with a lost dot. Rank just below claim 3, since commit characters on a widget that hides on every selection change would rarely fire.

**How to implement.** In `CompletionController`, track the currently selected item (the widget already exposes `selectedItem()`, completion.ts:115) and derive a `Set<string>` of its commit characters whenever selection changes. In `handleCompletionKeyDown`, before the Enter/Tab branch, check single-character `event.key` against that set; if it matches, call `acceptCompletion()` and let the keystroke proceed rather than preventing default — our edit chain (`packages/editor/src/editor/editChain.ts`) should coalesce the two into one undo entry, which is worth asserting in a test. Gate it behind an option, since a wrong commit character is the most annoying possible completion bug.

### Format-on-type, gated by provider trigger characters and cancelled by any edit before the caret

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **M** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** Format-on-type is not 'format the line after every keystroke'. The provider declares which characters may trigger it (`}`, `;`, newline for TS), the editor builds a `CharacterSet` once per model/option change, and only those characters fire a `textDocument/onTypeFormatting` request. Because the request is async and the user keeps typing, a listener watches for content changes while it is in flight and cancels if a change occurred at or before the caret's line — otherwise the returned edits, computed for an older document, would corrupt text the user has since typed. Format-on-paste is the same machinery keyed on the paste range instead of a character.

**Why it matters here.** Auto-indenting a closing brace, re-spacing a statement on `;`, and reformatting pasted code are the three highest-frequency formatting interactions; document-level formatting is the rare one. The cancellation guard is the non-obvious part and is exactly the class of bug that produces duplicated or scrambled text.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/format/browser/formatActions.ts:31-150 (FormatOnType: _update builds a CharacterSet from `support.autoFormatTriggerCharacters` at :80-93; _trigger at :97 bails on multi-cursor or non-empty selection, then installs an onDidChangeModelContent guard at :111-129 that cancels the in-flight request if any change lands at or before the caret's line, or on a model flush), :155-230 (FormatOnPaste, triggered from editor.onDidPaste with the pasted range)

**Our code.** Only `textDocument/formatting` is requested anywhere in packages/ (grep over packages/lsp-plugin/src, packages/lsp/src, packages/typescript-lsp/src yields no `onTypeFormatting` and no `rangeFormatting`). packages/lsp-plugin/src/formatting.ts has no trigger-character or paste path. `documentOnTypeFormattingProvider` capability is not advertised in packages/lsp/src/capabilities.ts.

**Verifier.** Keep, and note it is the *only* claim here that fixes a defect users hit on every single block they close: today typing `}` at the end of an indented body leaves it at body depth, because we have no on-type dedent from any source. Two independent routes — (a) a local, synchronous on-type reindent driven by tree-sitter (we already have brackets: packages/editor/src/editor/bracketMatching.ts, and `BracketInfo` in plugins.ts), which is cheap and has no async race at all; (b) the LSP route, which needs the capability advertised in packages/lsp/src/capabilities.ts, a CharacterSet gate, and the in-flight cancellation Monaco does. Route (a) is far better value for our architecture; take Monaco's *trigger-character gating* idea and drop the async machinery.

**How to implement.** Add an `OnTypeFormattingController` in packages/lsp-plugin/src modelled on `documentHighlightController.ts`: read `serverCapabilities.documentOnTypeFormattingProvider.{firstTriggerCharacter, moreTriggerCharacter}` (already reachable via `this.client.serverCapabilities`), build a Set once per connection, and hook the same `update(snapshot, kind, change)` path CompletionController uses (`kind === 'content'` with a single-character edit) to fire the request. Reuse `latestAsyncRequest.ts` for cancellation, plus an explicit guard: capture the document version/snapshot identity at request time and drop the result if any edit landed at or before the request offset — our `DocumentSessionChange` carries edit offsets so this is a cheap comparison. Route the result through the same `formattingEdits()` (with the minimization from finding #5). Paste is a second trigger on the same controller.

### Inline completions / ghost text: deriving renderable inline parts from an arbitrary text edit

`partial` · value **medium** · effort **M** · feature · from **monaco**

**What it is.** An inline suggestion arrives as a plain `TextReplacement` (range + text), which may replace text, not just append. `computeGhostText` first strips the common prefix, rejects multi-line edits whose first lines are not a pure prefix, then handles the indentation case explicitly (a suggestion that starts inside the line's leading whitespace is re-based so ghost text never re-renders existing indentation). It diffs the replaced text against the suggestion with `smartDiff` — an LCS where parentheses are remapped to depth-and-group-unique synthetic character codes, so brackets align structurally instead of greedily — and turns every pure-insertion change region into a `GhostTextPart` at a specific column. Any change region with `originalLength > 0` (a real deletion) aborts: ghost text can only *add*. `previewSuffixLength` splits each part into committed vs italic 'preview' text for partial acceptance. `mode: 'subwordSmart'` refuses to place ghost text before the caret. The diff result is memoized on the last (original, new) pair since it is recomputed on every render.

**Why it matters here.** This is the entire rendering contract for AI/LSP inline completions, and it is the hard part — the request plumbing is trivial by comparison. The bracket-aware diff is what stops ghost text from looking scrambled when a suggestion completes a call expression.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/inlineCompletions/browser/model/computeGhostText.ts:19-118 (computeGhostText), :36-62 (indentation-touching special case), :120-152 (cachingDiff with a smartness retry), :162-215 (smartDiff: bracket-aware LCS by remapping '(' and ')' to synthetic char codes so `if ()` aligns against `if (f() = 1)` correctly); model/ghostText.ts:16-160 (GhostText/GhostTextPart/GhostTextReplacement); model/inlineCompletionIsVisible.ts:12-30

**Our code.** No `inlineCompletion` or `ghostText` anywhere in packages/. However the *rendering* substrate exists: `packages/editor/src/displayTransforms.ts:51-72` defines `DisplayInjectedTextRow` with before/after placement, className and display columns, and `packages/editor/src/inlineMap.ts:26-49` defines anchored `InlineReplacementSpec`/`InlineReplacementRange` — plus the uncommitted packages/editor/test/inlineReplacementPainting.test.ts shows this path is actively being worked on.

**Verifier.** Downgraded to partial: we have the hard half. Rendering anchored, wrap-aware, theme-able inline text that the cursor and column arithmetic understand (inlineColumnToSourceColumn / sourceColumnToInlineColumn in displayTransforms.ts) is the expensive part of ghost text and it is built and tested. What is missing is the ~200 lines of `computeGhostText` logic — strip the common prefix, reject multi-line edits whose first line is not a pure prefix, re-base a suggestion that starts inside leading whitespace so indentation is not re-rendered — plus a provider and an accept/partial-accept command. That is a genuinely small increment on top of existing machinery and should be ranked much higher than a 'missing' label suggests. One caveat: verify zero-width InlineReplacementSpec actually survives inlineMap's dedup/grouping before committing to that route.

**How to implement.** Port `computeGhostText` + `smartDiff` into `packages/editor/src/editor/ghostText.ts` operating on our offsets rather than line/column (drop the ColumnRange machinery; our `TextEdit {from,to,text}` maps directly). Feed the resulting parts into `displayTransforms.ts` as injected text at an offset with a dim className — no new rendering primitive needed. Keep the `cachingDiff` memo, it is called per frame. The provider side then rides on the registry from finding #1 (`textDocument/inlineCompletion` for LSP, or a local provider), with Tab-to-accept coordinating with `snippetSession.ts` and the completion widget for key precedence.

### Semantic tokens: delta protocol with in-place Uint32Array splicing

`missing` · value **medium** · effort **L** · performance · from **monaco**

**What it is.** The provider is asked `provideDocumentSemanticTokens(model, lastResultId, token)` and may answer either a full `SemanticTokens` (flat Uint32Array of 5-tuples: deltaLine, deltaStartChar, length, tokenType, tokenModifiers) or a `SemanticTokensEdits` — a list of `{ start, deleteCount, data }` splices *against the previous array*. Applying them allocates one destination `Uint32Array` sized `src.length + Σ(data.length - deleteCount)` and fills it back-to-front (:348-372), copying the untouched tail, then each edit's data, then the untouched head — one allocation, no intermediate arrays, no per-token objects. Invalid edit starts are detected and the whole result discarded rather than producing garbage (:350-354). `releaseDocumentSemanticTokens(resultId)` tells the provider it may drop its cached previous result. Content changes that arrive while a request is in flight are accumulated and force a reschedule rather than being applied to stale tokens (:296-305).

**Why it matters here.** Semantic tokens are the layer that distinguishes a type from a variable from a parameter — the thing tree-sitter alone cannot do for TypeScript. The delta encoding is what makes them affordable on a large file: a one-character edit produces a few-hundred-byte splice instead of a megabyte array, and the flat-array representation composes with the packed token format we already use.

**Reference.** vscode/src/vs/editor/contrib/semanticTokens/browser/documentSemanticTokens.ts:245 (lastResultId passed to the provider), :296-400 (_setDocumentSemanticTokens: SemanticTokensEdits branch at :325-380, empty-edits fast path at :331-337, backwards splice loop at :348-372, _copy at :288-295), plus `releaseDocumentSemanticTokens(resultId)` (:311, :421) and the pending-changes reschedule (:296-305); provider interface at vscode/src/vs/editor/common/languages.ts:2488-2499

**Our code.** No `semanticTokens` anywhere in packages/ (grep across all .ts files returns nothing). Highlighting comes from tree-sitter (packages/tree-sitter, packages/editor/src/syntax/packedTokens.ts) and shiki (packages/editor/src/shiki/). packages/lsp/src/capabilities.ts:11-49 does not advertise `textDocument.semanticTokens`.

**Verifier.** Lowest priority of the twelve for us. Two things are conflated: the *feature* (type-aware colouring that tree-sitter cannot do — distinguishing a type from a value, readonly, a namespace) and the *delta protocol* (an over-the-wire optimisation). Our tree-sitter + shiki stack already delivers most of the visible colour, so the feature is incremental; and the delta protocol only pays when a server re-sends tokens for a large file on every keystroke, which is a scenario we do not have yet. The genuinely transferable insight is the single-allocation splice at documentSemanticTokens.ts:340-358 — and we already do the SoA/transferable half in packedTokens.ts, so if we ever add this the integration cost is low. Rank it below claims 2, 3, 5, 7.

**How to implement.** We already have the right substrate: `packages/editor/src/syntax/packedTokens.ts` is a flat packed-token representation and `packages/editor/src/editor/tokenProjection.ts` merges token sources for rendering. Add a `SemanticTokensController` in packages/lsp-plugin that advertises `textDocument.semanticTokens` with `full: { delta: true }` in capabilities.ts, holds `{ resultId, data: Uint32Array }`, and ports the backwards splice loop verbatim. Decode the 5-tuples into our packed format keyed by the server's legend, and register the result as a second token layer in `tokenProjection.ts` so it overlays tree-sitter tokens rather than replacing them (which is exactly how VS Code layers semantic over TextMate). Debounce and reschedule on pending changes as the reference does.
